            | Windows                 | Yes                                                  | Not natively for EFS    |
            | Typical Use             | Database, Boot Volume                                | Shared Files, WordPress |
        EBS Multi-Attach vs EFS
            | If the question says...                                                                              | Choose...            |
            | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- |
            | Shared **block** storage, up to 16 EC2, same AZ, io1/io2                                             | **EBS Multi-Attach** |
            | Shared **file** storage, many EC2, multiple AZs, Linux applications (WordPress, CMS, shared uploads) | **Amazon EFS**       |
    Scalability:
        Can the system handle MORE users?
        Traffic များရင် Server တိုး။ Traffic လျော့ရင် Server လျော့။ AWS က Auto လုပ်ပေးတယ်။
        an application/system can handle greater loads by adaptation.
        two types:
            Vertical(Scale Up):
                Make ONE server stronger.
                Disadvantages is we cannot upgrade forever/No bigger instance exists.
            Horizontal(Scale Out):
                add more servers.
            | Vertical                    | Horizontal        |
            | --------------------------- | ----------------- |
            | Bigger server               | More servers      |
            | Scale Up                    | Scale Out         |
            | Limited                     | Nearly unlimited  |
            | Easier                      | More complex      |
            | Usually downtime for resize | Often no downtime |
    Elasticity:
        AWS automatically adds or removes servers.Like a rubber band:
            Stretch
            ↓
            Shrink
    High Availability:
        run at least 2 data centers.
        Server တစ်လုံးပျက်ရင်တောင် Website မပျက်ဘူး။ AZ တစ်ခု ပျက်ရင်တောင်
        နောက် AZ က ဆက်အလုပ်လုပ်တယ်။
        Goal: Reduce downtime.
            Example:
                Server A fails
                ↓
                Server B starts serving users
            There might be a few seconds of interruption.
    Fault Tolerance:
        Fault Tolerance means the system continues working even when a component fails, 
        with little or no interruption.
        The keyword is:
            Failure
            ↓
            Still Works
            Real-Life Example
                Imagine an airplane.
                It has:
                2 engines
                If one engine fails:
                Engine 1 ❌
                Engine 2 ✅

                The airplane continues flying.
                That is Fault Tolerance.
        Goal:  No interruption (or almost none).
    High Availability vs Fault Tolerance:
        Imagine your house loses electricity.
            High Availability:
            A generator starts after 10 seconds.
            Power ❌
            ↓
            10 seconds
            ↓
            Generator starts.
            Note:
                တစ်ခုခုပျက်ရင် Service ကို အမြန်ဆုံး ပြန်တက်အောင်လုပ်ထားတာ။
                Downtime နည်းအောင်။

            Fault Tolerance
            The generator is already running.
            Electricity changes instantly.
            Power ❌
            ↓
            Immediately
            ↓
            Generator

            You don't even notice.
            Note:
                တစ်ခုခုပျက်ရင်တောင် Service က မပျက်ဘဲ ဆက်အလုပ်လုပ်တာ။
    Load Balancer:
        spread load
        Single Point of Access (DNS)
            Each EC2 has a different IP.
            Instead, users always access: www.example.com
            DNS resolves to: Application Load Balancer
        Handle Failures Automatically
            EC2 တစ်လုံး ပျက်ရင် Load Balancer က Healthy EC2 ကို Auto ပို့ပေးတယ်။
        Health Checks
            Every few seconds,
            the Load Balancer asks:

            Are you alive?

            Example:
            GET /
            200 OK

            Healthy.

            If:
            500
            or
            Timeout

            Unhealthy.

            Load Balancer stops sending traffic.
        SSL Termination (HTTPS)
            Normally

            Browser
            ↓
            HTTPS
            ↓
            EC2

            EC2 decrypts HTTPS.

            Instead,
            Load Balancer can decrypt.

            Browser
            ↓
            HTTPS
            ↓
            Load Balancer
            ↓
            HTTP
            ↓
            EC2

            This reduces work for EC2.
        Stickiness (Cookies)
            Login ဝင်ပြီးရင် Request အားလုံးကို Server တစ်လုံးတည်းကို ပို့ချင်ရင် Stickiness သုံးတယ်။
            Imagine you login.
                EC2-1
                ↓
                Logged In

                Next request:

                Without stickiness

                EC2-2

                Session disappears.

                With stickiness:

                Login
                ↓
                EC2-1
                ↓
                Next Request
                ↓
                EC2-1

                The Load Balancer uses a cookie to remember where to send your requests.
        Separate Public and Private Traffic:
            Very common AWS architecture.
                Internet
                ↓
                Public Load Balancer
                ↓

                Private EC2

                Notice:
                EC2 has:
                No Public IP
                Much more secure.
                Users only access the Load Balancer.
    Elastic Load Balancer:
        a managed service.
            "Managed" mean: AWS manages it for you.
        Imagine Two Choices
            Build Your Own Load Balancer
                You launch an EC2.

                EC2
                ↓
                Install Ubuntu
                ↓
                Install Nginx
                ↓
                Configure Load Balancing
                Now you are responsible for everything.

            Using ELB
                Create ELB
                ↓
                Done
                AWS manages everything.
        It costs less to setup your own load balancer.
            Nginx ကို ကိုယ်တိုင်တင်ရင် EC2 တစ်လုံးစာပဲကုန်ကျမယ်။
            ဒါပေမယ့် Maintenance အကုန် ကိုယ်တိုင်လုပ်ရမယ်။
            Production မှာ AWS ELB သုံးတာက ပိုကောင်းတယ်။
        Integrated with Many AWS Services
            ASG က EC2 အသစ် ဖန်တီးလိုက်တာနဲ့ ELB က Auto သိပြီး Traffic ပို့ပေးတယ်။
        4 kinds of managed Load Balancer:
            Classic Load Balancer(CLB): v1 2009
                HTTP, HTTPS, TCP, SSL(Secure TCP)
            Application Load Balancer(ALB): v2 2016
                HTTP, HTTPS, Websocket.
            Network Load Balancer(NLB): v2 2017
                TCP, TLS(Secure TCP), UDP
                    extremely fast.
                    AWS says: Millions of requests per second.
            Gateway Load Balancer(GWLB): 2020
                Operates at layer 3 (Network layer) - IP Protocol
            Note: recommended to use newer generation.
                some can be setup as internal(private) or external(public) elbs.

                Just remember OSI layer, these:
                    Application Layer (L7)
                    ↓
                    Transport Layer (L4)
                    ↓
                    Network Layer (L3)
            ALB:
                Routing tables:
                    based on path/hostname/query string.
                target groups:
                    ec2 instances, can be managed by ASG.
                    ec2 tasks, managed by ec2 itself.
                    lambda functions,
                    IP addresses,
                    ALB can route to multiple target groups
                        eg: for /user route to go one target group,
                        eg: for /search route to go one target group,
                        query string routing:
                            /requests route go to ALB v2
                            ?Platform=Mobile -> target group 1(aws ec2 based)
                            ?Platform=Desktop -> target group 2(on-premises Private IP routing)
                    Health checks are at target group level.
                aws automatically created: Fixed hostname(DNS)(xxx.region.elb.amazonaws.com)
                    Note: normally don't remember this dns name.
                    use Route 53 (or another DNS provider)
                        eg:
                            www.mycompany.com
                                    │
                                    ▼
                            Route53
                                    │
                                    ▼
                            xxx.region.elb.amazonaws.com
                the application servers don't see the IP of the client directly.
                    Your laptop IP is:
                        1.2.3.4

                        You open: www.example.com

                        The request goes like this:

                        Your Browser
                        1.2.3.4
                            │
                            ▼
                        Application Load Balancer
                            │
                            ▼
                        EC2

                        Question: What IP does EC2 see?

                        Many beginners think:
                        1.2.3.4

                        ❌ Wrong.

                        Actually,

                        EC2 sees: ALB's Private IP

                        because ALB forwards the request.
                        Then How Can EC2 Know the Real Client IP?
                        AWS adds a special HTTP header: X-Forwarded-For
                the true IP of client is inserted in the header X-Forwarded-For.
                we can also get Port(X-Forwarded-Port) and proto (X-Forwarded-Proto)
                    X-Forwarded-Port:
                        Client က ဘယ် Port(80/443, etc.) ကနေ ဝင်လာလဲဆိုတာ Header ထဲမှာသိမ်းထားတယ်။
                    X-Forwarded-Proto:
                        Browser က HTTPS နဲ့ဝင်လာတယ်။ ALB က SSL ဖြုတ်ပြီး EC2 ကို HTTP နဲ့ပို့တယ်။
                        ဒါပေမယ့်Application က Client က HTTPS နဲ့လာတာကိုသိဖို့သုံး
                            Complete Flow
                                Browser

                                IP = 1.2.3.4

                                HTTPS

                                        │

                                        ▼

                                Application Load Balancer

                                        │

                                Adds

                                X-Forwarded-For: 1.2.3.4

                                X-Forwarded-Port: 443

                                X-Forwarded-Proto: https

                                        │

                                HTTP

                                        ▼

                                EC2
                Note:
                    | Header            | Meaning                           |
                    | ----------------- | --------------------------------- |
                    | X-Forwarded-For   | Original Client IP                |
                    | X-Forwarded-Port  | Original Port (80/443, etc.)      |
                    | X-Forwarded-Proto | Original Protocol (HTTP or HTTPS) |
                Discussion:
                    EC2 only receives HTTP with LB using or without LB?
                    aws: No. EC2 can receive either HTTP or HTTPS. It depends on your architecture.
                    Case 1: Without a Load Balancer

                        Your architecture:

                        Browser
                        │
                        HTTPS
                        │
                        ▼
                        EC2

                        In this case:

                        ✅ The browser talks directly to the EC2 instance.
                        ✅ EC2 must have an SSL/TLS certificate installed (for example, in Nginx or Apache).
                        ✅ EC2 decrypts the HTTPS traffic itself.

                        Example:

                        Client
                            │
                        HTTPS (443)
                            │
                            ▼
                        Nginx (SSL Certificate)
                            │
                        HTTP
                            │
                        Laravel

                        So EC2 receives HTTPS on port 443, and Nginx terminates SSL.
                    Case 2: With an Application Load Balancer (Most Common)

                        Architecture:

                        Browser
                            │
                        HTTPS
                            │
                            ▼
                        ALB
                            │
                        HTTP
                            │
                            ▼
                        EC2

                        Here:

                        The SSL certificate is attached to the ALB (often using AWS Certificate Manager).
                        The ALB decrypts the HTTPS request.
                        The EC2 instance receives plain HTTP.

                        This is called: SSL Termination

                        This is the default architecture used by many AWS applications.
                    Can ALB Send HTTPS to EC2?

                        Yes!

                        You can configure the target group to use:
                            HTTP (port 80)
                            HTTPS (port 443)

                        Example:

                        Browser
                            │
                        HTTPS
                            ▼
                        ALB
                            │
                        HTTPS
                            ▼
                        EC2

                        This is called end-to-end encryption.
                    Which One Is More Common?

                        For most web applications:

                        Browser
                        │
                        HTTPS
                        ▼
                        ALB
                        │
                        HTTP
                        ▼
                        EC2

                        Reason:

                        Simpler certificate management
                        Lower CPU usage on EC2
                        Easier certificate renewal with ACM

                        Real Laravel Example
                            Without ALB:

                                Internet

                                ↓

                                HTTPS

                                ↓

                                Nginx (Certificate)

                                ↓

                                Laravel

                                You manage the certificate on the EC2 instance.
                            With ALB:

                                Internet

                                ↓

                                HTTPS

                                ↓

                                ALB (Certificate)

                                ↓

                                HTTP

                                ↓

                                Laravel

                                No certificate is needed on the EC2 instance (unless you intentionally choose HTTPS between ALB and EC2).
            NLB:
                Traffic အရမ်းများတဲ့ System တွေမှာ NLB ကို သုံး
                It understands:
                    TCP
                    UDP
                    TLS
                It does NOT understand:
                    /api
                    /login
                Unlike ALB, it never checks:
                    URL
                    HTTP headers
                    Cookies
                It simply forwards the network traffic.
                Ultra-Low Latency
                Public Static IP per AZ
                    NLB က AZ တစ်ခုစီအတွက် Static Public IPရတယ်။
                    IP မပြောင်းဘူး။ Firewall, Whitelist, Partner Company, VPN တွေမှာ အရမ်းအသုံးဝင်တယ်။
                    ALB has:
                        Hostname: my-alb.ap-northeast-1.elb.amazonaws.com

                        The underlying IP addresses can change over time.
                        You normally use DNS.
                    NLB gives: Static IP
                        Example:

                        AZ-a

                        3.10.20.30

                        AZ-b

                        3.10.20.31

                        These IP addresses remain stable.
                Supports assigning Elastic IP.
                Question: 
                    A company must whitelist a fixed public IP address in a partner's firewall.
                    ✅ Answer: Network Load Balancer with Elastic IPs
                three kinds of targets:
                    EC2 Instances
                        Exactly like ALB.
                    IP Addresses(Private IP Only)
                        Because NLB forwards traffic inside your VPC.
                    ALB as a Target
                        can forward traffic to an Application Load Balancer.
                        Why would anyone do this?

                        Imagine:

                        You need:
                        ✅ Static IP
                        AND
                        ✅ Path-based Routing
                        Problem:
                            ALB
                            ✔ Path Routing
                            ❌ Static IP
                            NLB
                            ✔ Static IP
                            ❌ Path Routing
                        Solution:
                            Internet
                            ↓
                            NLB
                            (Static IP)
                            ↓
                            ALB
                            (Path Routing)
                            ↓
                            EC2

                        Now you have both.

                        This is a real AWS architecture.
                        Company တစ်ခုက Static IP လည်း လိုတယ်။ Path Routing လည်း လိုတယ်။ 
                        ဒါဆို NLB, ALB ၂ ခုကို တွဲသုံးနိုင်တယ်။
                Health Checks:
                    Unlike ALB, NLB supports several health check protocols.
                    Website ကို မစစ်ဘူး။ Port ဖွင့်ထားလား Connection ရလား ဒါပဲ စစ်တယ်။
                    Example
                        Suppose Nginx is running,

                        but Laravel has crashed.

                        TCP check:

                        Port 80

                        Open

                        Healthy

                        Even though Laravel isn't working.

                        HTTP check:

                        GET /

                        ↓

                        500 Internal Server Error

                        Unhealthy.

                        So HTTP health checks are often better for web applications.
            GLB:
                special load balancer for security devices.
                GWLB က Website အတွက် Load Balancer မဟုတ်ဘူး။ Security Device တွေကို Load Balance လုပ်ဖို့ ဖန်တီးထားတာ။
                Suppose a company has this architecture:
                    Internet
                    ↓
                    EC2

                    Everything works.
                    Before traffic reaches the application, we want to inspect every packet.
                    For example:
                        Virus detection
                        SQL Injection detection
                        Malware detection
                        Packet inspection
                        Company Firewall

                    How do you do this?

                    Without GWLB
                        Internet
                        ↓
                        Firewall EC2
                        ↓
                        Application EC2

                    Problems:
                        Need to manage firewall scaling
                        Need multiple firewall instances
                        Difficult routing
                        Difficult High Availability
                AWS solved this by creating: Gateway Load Balancer.
                Example: Firewalls, Intrusion Detection(IDS) and Prevention Systems(IPS), Deep Packet Inspection Systems(DPI), payload manipulation
                    IDS is like Security Camera. IPS can block attacks.
                    DPI က Packet ရဲ့ အပြင်ပိုင်းကိုပဲ မကြည့်ဘူး။ Packet ထဲက Data ကိုပါ ဖတ်ပြီး စစ်ဆေးတယ်။
                    Payload Manipulation is 
                        Imagine the packet contains:
                            <script>alert('hack')</script>
                        A security appliance could:
                            remove it
                            replace it
                            block it
                        before it reaches your application.
                Combine: Transparent Network Gateway + LB
                    Transparent ဆိုတာ User မသိဘဲ နောက်ကွယ်မှာလုပ်နေတဲ့သဘော။ Browser က Firewall ရှိမှန်းမသိဘူး။
                Use the GENEVE protocol on port 6081.
                Note:
                    AWS က Firewall ကို ကိုယ်တိုင် မပေးဘူး။ Partner Company တွေရဲ့ Firewall Software ကို EC2 ပေါ်မှာ Run လို့ရတယ်။
                    GWLB က အဲဒီ Firewall တွေကို Load Balance လုပ်ပေးတယ်။
            ALB vs NLB
                | Feature                     | ALB                    | NLB           |
                | --------------------------- | ---------------------- | ------------- |
                | OSI Layer                   | Layer 7                | Layer 4       |
                | Protocols                   | HTTP, HTTPS, WebSocket | TCP, TLS, UDP |
                | Path Routing                | ✅ Yes                  | ❌ No          |
                | Hostname Routing            | ✅ Yes                  | ❌ No          |
                | Query String Routing        | ✅ Yes                  | ❌ No          |
                | Static IP                   | ❌ No                   | ✅ Yes         |
                | Elastic IP                  | ❌ No                   | ✅ Yes         |
                | Very Low Latency            | Good                   | ⭐ Best        |
                | Millions of TCP Connections | Good                   | ⭐ Excellent   |
        Sticky Sessions:
            supported on three LB: CLB, ALB, NLB.
            User က Login လုပ်တယ်။ Login Data ကို EC2-A မှာ သိမ်းထားတယ်။ 
            နောက် Request တစ်ခုမှာ ALB က EC2-B ဆီ ပို့လိုက်ရင် EC2-B မှာ Login Data မရှိဘူး။ User ကLogout ဖြစ်သွားနိုင်တယ်။
            Instead of randomly choosing every request, Every request goes to the same server.
            That's called Session Affinity or Sticky Session.
            To remember, AWS sends a cookie to the browser.
                Example:
                Set-Cookie: AWSALB=xxxxxxxx
                Browser stores it.
                Next request:
                    Cookie: AWSALB=xxxxxxxx
                ALB reads it and says: "Oh! This user belongs to EC2-A."
                Then forwards the request to EC2-A.
                The cookie doesn't live forever.
            
            Is Sticky Session the Best Solution?
            AWS says:
                Not really.
                Instead,
                store sessions centrally. like: redis.
                Other options include storing sessions in a shared database.
            အားနည်းချက်
                Enabling stickiness may bring imbalance.
                Load Balance မညီနိုင်ဘူး
                Modern cloud applications usually avoid relying on sticky sessions.
                Instead:
                    Users
                    ↓
                    ALB
                    ↓
                    EC2-A
                    ↓
                    shared session store (Redis)

                    EC2-B
                    ↓
                    shared session store (Redis)

                Any server can handle any request.
                This scales much better.
            Cookie Names:
                Cookie Name Must Be Unique
                two cookie have:
                    Duration-based Cookies:
                        LB creates. not do developer. AWSALB for alb, AWSELB for clb.
                            Duration means 1 hr, 2 hr, 1 day. after that time, expires -> alb can choose another ec2.
                    Application-based Cookie:
                        your application creates. as like as laravel_session, PHPSESSID.
                        Never use AWSALB, AWSALBAPP, or AWSALBG as your application's cookie names.
                        On setting of ALB attribute, we must be tell cookie name.
                Which One Should We Use?
                    Most companies use
                    Duration-based
                    because it's simpler.
                    Application-based is used only when the application already has its own session cookie and you want ALB to use it.
        Cross-Zone LB:
            Cross-Zone မဖွင့်ထားရင်
            AZ-A က LB က AZ-A ထဲက EC2 တွေဆီပဲ ပို့လို့ရတယ်။
            AZ-B က LB ကလည်း AZ-B ထဲက EC2 ဆီပဲ ပို့တယ်။
            ဒါကြောင့် EC2 အရေအတွက် မညီရင် Load မညီတော့ဘူး။
            With Cross-Zone Load Balancing:
                LB-A can send traffic to all EC2s.
                LB-B can also send traffic to all EC2s.
                ဒါကြောင့် Load Balance ညီသွားတယ်။
            Which Load Balancers Support Cross-Zone?
                | Load Balancer | Default                            |
                | ------------- | ---------------------------------- |
                | ALB           | ✅ Enabled                          |
                | CLB           | ❌ Disabled (can enable with pay charge )            |
                | NLB           | ❌ Disabled by default (can enable no charge) |
        SSL/TLS basic:
            SSL: Secure Socket Layer
            TLS: Transport Layer Security, newer version
            nowadays, TLS cert are mainly used. but people still refer as SSL
            public SSL cert are issued by Certificate authorities (CA)
            LB uses an X.509 certicate (SSL/TLS server certificate)
            
            can upload own cert. Instead of buying a certificate manually,
                using ACM(AWS Certificate Manager)
                Advantages:
                    Free public certificates (for eligible public domains)
                    Automatic renewal
                    Easy attachment to ALB
            Where Is the Certificate Installed?
                not on EC2, very often it is installed on the Load Balancer.
                    Browser
                    ↓
                    HTTPS
                    ↓
                    ALB
                    ↓
                    HTTP
                    ↓
                    EC2
            ALB can store all: Default certificate, optional list of certs
                How Does ALB Know Which Certificate to Use?
                    using SNI(Server Name Indication)
                        SNI ဆိုတာ Browser က "ဘယ် Domain ကိုဝင်တာလဲ" ဆိုတာ ALB ကိုပြောပေးတာ။
                        ALB က သက်ဆိုင်တဲ့ Certificate ကိုရွေးပေးတယ်။
                        Question:
                            One ALB serves:
                                shop.example.com
                                api.example.com
                            Each has a different certificate.
                            How does ALB know which certificate to present?
                            ✅ Answer: SNI (Server Name Indication)
                        Note:
                            only work for ALB & NLB(newer generation), CloudFront.
                            not work for CLB (older gen)
                Security Policy:
                    Not all browsers support modern TLS.
                    Example:
                        Old Windows XP browser
                        ↓
                        TLS 1.0 only

                        Modern Chrome
                        ↓
                        TLS 1.3

                        ALB lets you choose
                        which TLS versions to support.
                    Example:

                        Strict Policy
                            TLS 1.3
                            TLS 1.2
                        Old browsers cannot connect.

                        Legacy Policy
                            TLS 1.0
                            TLS 1.1
                            TLS 1.2
                        Older browsers can still connect.
            Connection Draininig:
                User က 2 GB file Download လုပ်နေတယ်။ Download မပြီးသေးဘူး။
                အဲ့ဒီအချိန်မှာ EC2 ကို Terminate လုပ်လိုက်ရင် User ရဲ့ Download ချက်ချင်း ပြတ်သွားမယ်။
                ဒါက User Experience မကောင်းဘူး။
                Solution is Connection Draininig.
                    Instead of immediately terminating EC2,
                        AWS says: "Wait a little."
                            Not Wait Forever:
                                set a timeout: 30, 60, 300 seconds, 
                    In-flight Requests:
                        A request that is currently being processed.
                        အလုပ်လုပ်နေဆဲ Request
                    What Happens During Deregistration?
                        EC2-A ကို ဖြုတ်တော့မယ်ဆိုရင် User အဟောင်းတွေက ဆက်သုံးလို့ရတယ်။
                        ဒါပေမယ့် User အသစ်တွေကို EC2-A ဆီ မပို့တော့ဘူး။
                        Suppose your Target Group has:
                            EC2-A
                            EC2-B
                        You remove EC2-A.
                        ALB marks it as: Deregistering

                        Now ALB behaves like this.
                        Existing Users
                            User 1
                            ↓
                            EC2-A

                            Continue...

                            Allowed.

                        New Users
                            User 2
                            ↓
                            EC2-B

                            New requests are not sent to EC2-A anymore.
                Connection Draining vs Deregistration Delay
                    AWS changed the name.
                        | Load Balancer | Feature Name         |
                        | ------------- | -------------------- |
                        | CLB           | Connection Draining  |
                        | ALB           | Deregistration Delay |
                        | NLB           | Deregistration Delay |

                    The behavior is the same.
                    Only the name changed.
            Note:
                HTTP က စာသားအတိုင်းပို့တယ်။
                HTTPS က Encryption လုပ်ပြီးပို့တယ်။ ဒါကြောင့် Password, Credit Cardတို့ကိုလုံခြုံစေတယ်။
                Certificate ကို EC2 မှာ ထားလို့ရတယ်။ ဒါပေမယ့် AWS Best Practice က ALB မှာထားတာ။

                Most common: HTTPS → ALB → HTTP → EC2 (SSL termination at the ALB)
                Also possible: HTTPS → ALB → HTTPS → EC2 (end-to-end encryption)

                CLB = One Certificate
                    CLB က Certificate တစ်ခုတည်းပဲထည့်လို့ရတယ်။ Domain ၃ ခုရှိရင် CLB ၃ ခုလိုမယ်။
                ALB/NLB = Multiple Certificates using SNI
                
                Cipher: an encryption algorithm.
                    Encrypt data (make it unreadable)
                    Decrypt data (turn it back into the original)
                ciphers clients: chrome, edge, iPhone app, etc..
                    During HTTPS Connection

                    Imagine this conversation:
                        Browser
                        │
                        ▼
                        ALB

                    Browser says:

                    "I support these encryption methods:"

                    AES-128
                    AES-256
                    CHACHA20

                    ALB replies:

                    "I only allow these:"

                    AES-256
                    CHACHA20

                    Both choose:

                    AES-256

                    Now the connection is encrypted using AES-256.

                    This process is part of the TLS handshake.
    Auto Scaling Group:
        Free
        scale out(add instances) to increase load.
        scale in(remove instances) to decrese load.
        ensure min and max instance value.
            Minimum Capacity ဆိုတာ AWS က "ဘယ်လိုပဲဖြစ်ဖြစ် ဒီအရေအတွက်ထက် မလျော့စေရဘူး"
            Desired Capacity ဆိုတာ လက်ရှိ ရှိစေချင်တဲ့ EC2 အရေအတွက်။
            Maximum ဆိုတာ AWS က ဘယ်လောက် အများဆုံး EC2 ဖန်တီးခွင့်ရှိလဲ
        flow:
            Users -> ELB -> ASG -> EC2 instances
            This relationship must always be true.
                Minimum ≤ Desired ≤ Maximum
        Launch Template:
            EC2 တစ်လုံး ဖန်တီးဖို့ လိုအပ်တဲ့ Setting အားလုံးကို သိမ်းထားတဲ့ Template
            how an Auto Scaling Group knows what kind of EC2 instance to create?
                ASG = Manager
                Launch Template = Blueprint (or recipe)
            contain:
                • AMI + Instance Type
                • EC2 User Data
                • EBS Volumes
                • Security Groups
                • SSH Key Pair
                • IAM Roles for your EC2 Instances
                • Network + Subnets Information
                • Load Balancer Information
            CloudWatch alarms & Scaling:
                Auto Scaling Group = Worker
                CloudWatch Alarm = Manager
                The manager watches the servers and tells the worker what to do.
                    If Average CPU > 70%
                    ↓
                    Alarm
                Alarm Triggers Auto Scaling
            Average CPU:
                Metrics such as Average CPU are computed for the overall ASG instances.
                Suppose
                    EC2-1

                    80%
                    EC2-2

                    60%
                    EC2-3

                    40%

                    Average: (80 + 60 + 40) ÷ 3 = 60%

                    CloudWatch looks at the average of all instances in the ASG
                flow:
                    CloudWatch Metric
                        ↓
                    CloudWatch Alarm
                    ↓
                    Auto Scaling Policy
                    ↓
                    Auto Scaling Group
                    ↓
                    Launch / Terminate EC2
        Scaling Policies:
            Dynamic Scaling:
                AWS က အလိုအလျောက်EC2 တိုး/လျှော့လုပ်
                    Target Tracking Scaling:
                        simple to set-up
                        eg: I want the average ASG CPU to stay at around 40%

                        Scale Out is fast, Scale In is slow.
                    Simple/Step Scaling:
                        smarter than Simple Scaling.
                        When a CloudWatch alarm is triggered (CPU > 70%), then add 2 units
                        When a CloudWatch alarm is triggered (CPU < 3>0%), then remove 1 units
                    
            Scheduled Scaling:
                Traffic လာမယ့်အချိန်ကို ကြိုသိနေတယ်
                anticipate a scaling based on known usage patterns
                eg: increase then min capacity to 10 at 5 pm on Fridays
            Predictive scaling:
                continuously forecast load and schedule scaling ahead
                newer and smarter scaling policy in AWS
                Predictive Scaling ဆိုတာ AWS က အရင်က Traffic History ကို လေ့လာပြီး
                    နောက်တစ်ခါ Traffic များမယ့်အချိန်ကိုခန့်မှန်းပြီး EC2 တွေကို ကြိုတင် ဖန်တီးပေးတာပါ
            Good Metrics to Scale on:
                CPUUtilization

            Question:
                A company notices that traffic increases every weekday at 9 AM. 
                They want AWS to automatically learn this pattern and add EC2 instances before users arrive, 
                without manually creating schedules.

                ✅ Answer: Predictive Scaling
                ❌ Not Scheduled Scaling, because Scheduled Scaling requires you to define the schedule yourself.
            | Scaling Policy     | Trigger                                              |
            | ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |
            | Target Tracking    | Maintain a target metric (e.g., CPU 50%)             |
            | Simple Scaling     | One CloudWatch alarm → one fixed action              |
            | Step Scaling       | Different alarm thresholds → different actions       |
            | Scheduled Scaling  | Time-based schedule set by you                       |
            | Predictive Scaling | AWS forecasts future demand using historical metrics |

            The difference is:
                | Policy    | Question it answers              |
                | --------- | -------------------------------- |
                | Dynamic   | **React** to current traffic     |
                | Scheduled | **Prepare** for expected traffic |
        Termination policy:
            If desired decrease/Scale-In, How to choose termination of instance?
                AWS does not simply terminate the newest (latest) instance first.
                AWS follows a termination policy.
                Termination policy:
                    priorities:
                        Keep Availability Zones balanced.
                        Prefer terminating instances that use older launch templates/configurations.
                        Then apply the configured termination policy (which may consider launch time).
                    explanation:
                        Step 1: Is one AZ holding more instances?

                            Example:

                            AZ-A

                            3 instances
                            AZ-B

                            1 instance

                            AWS prefers to terminate an instance from AZ-A to keep the AZs balanced.

                            This helps maintain High Availability.
                        Step 2: Which Instance Should Be Removed?

                            If there are multiple candidates, AWS looks at the termination policy.

                            The default policy generally prefers to terminate instances launched from older launch configurations/templates or mixed-instance configurations first, before considering launch time.

                            If all instances are equivalent, launch time may become one of the deciding factors.

                            Example

                            Suppose:

                            10:00

                            EC2-A
                            10:05

                            EC2-B
                            10:10

                            EC2-C
                            10:15

                            EC2-D

                            If all four are:

                            Same Launch Template
                            Same AZ balance
                            Same configuration

                            Then AWS may terminate one of the newer instances according to its default policy.

                            But don't memorize "latest is always deleted." That is not the rule.

                            Why?

                            Imagine:

                            Old instances:

                            Launch Template v1

                            New instances:

                            Launch Template v2

                            You update your application.

                            AWS would rather keep:

                            v2

                            and remove:

                            v1

                            because v2 contains the latest software.
                        Can Customize It.
        metrics:
            Based on CloudWatch metrics, launch another EC2.
            1. CPUUtilization(most common)
                Average CPU utilization across your EC2 instances
            2. RequestCountPerTarget
                CPU ကိုမကြည့်ဘူး။ ALB က EC2 တစ်လုံးချင်းစီကို Request ဘယ်လောက်ဝင်လဲဆိုတာ ကြည့်တယ်
                    Why not CPU?
                        Sometimes
                        Application က CPU မသုံးဘူး။
                        Example
                            Nginx
                            Static Website
                            Image Server

                        CPU က 15% ပဲရှိတယ်။

                        ဒါပေမယ့် Request 30000/sec ရှိနိုင်တယ်။

                        CPU ကိုကြည့်ရင် AWS က "Load မရှိဘူး" လို့ထင်မယ်။

                        တကယ်တော့

                        Request တွေ အရမ်းများနေတယ်။

                        ဒါကြောင့် RequestCountPerTarget ကိုသုံးတာ။
            3. Network In / Network Out
                Bandwidth ကြည့်တာ Example: Video Streaming
            4. Custom CloudWatch Metric
                AWS ပေးထားတဲ့ Metric တွေမလုံလောက်ရင် ကိုယ်တိုင် Metric ပို့နိုင်
            | Metric                | Best For                      | Why?                                        |
            | --------------------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
            | CPUUtilization        | Compute-intensive apps        | CPU load reflects demand                    |
            | RequestCountPerTarget | ALB web applications          | Measures actual traffic per EC2             |
            | Network In/Out        | Streaming, downloads, proxies | Network bandwidth is the bottleneck         |
            | Custom Metric         | Special business logic        | Scale based on application-specific metrics |
        Cooldown Period:
            Auto Scaling Down (Cooldown Period):
            mean: after scale-out/in instance, aws does not check immediately CPU again.
            (default Cooldown = 300 seconds)
            During these 5 minutes:
                No more Scale Out
                No more Scale In
            Imagine:
                Suppose your architecture is:
                            Users
                            │
                            ▼
                    Application Load Balancer
                    /        |         \
                    ▼         ▼          ▼
                EC2 #1    EC2 #2     EC2 #3

                Current traffic is high.

                CPU Average = 80%

                Your Auto Scaling Policy is:

                    CPU > 70% → Add 1 EC2

                AWS launches a new EC2.

                                Users
                                │
                                ▼
                        Application Load Balancer
                    /      |      |      \
                    ▼       ▼      ▼       ▼
                EC2#1   EC2#2   EC2#3   EC2#4

                Now the question is...
                Should AWS immediately check the CPU again and add another EC2?
                ❌ No!
                AWS waits for a period called the Cooldown Period.
            Use a ready-to-use AMI to reduce configuration time.
        Instance Warmup:
            meaning: Don't include this new EC2 in Auto Scaling metrics until it's actually working.
            Imagine:
                Suppose your restaurant is getting crowded.
                    Current staff:

                    Restaurant

                    Chef A
                    Chef B

                    Customers increase.

                    You hire Chef C.

                    Now ask yourself these questions:

                    Question 1

                    Should I count Chef C as a full worker immediately?

                    ❌ No.

                    He is still:

                    Wearing uniform
                    Learning the kitchen
                    Preparing ingredients

                    This is exactly what Instance Warmup is.
            Real example:
                Suppose you have:

                        Users
                        │
                        ▼
                        ALB
                        │
                        ▼
                        EC2-1
                        EC2-2

                        Average CPU

                        80%

                        Target Tracking Policy

                        Target CPU = 50%

                        AWS launches EC3.

                        Timeline
                        12:00
                        EC2-1
                        CPU = 80%

                        EC2-2
                        CPU = 80%

                        Average

                        80%

                        Launch EC3.

                        12:01

                        EC3 is booting.

                        It is NOT serving traffic yet.

                        EC2-1 = 80%

                        EC2-2 = 80%

                        EC2-3 = 0%

                        Now imagine AWS includes EC3 in the average immediately.

                        Average CPU becomes

                        (80+80+0)/3

                        =53%

                        AWS thinks:

                        Great!

                        CPU almost reached target.

                        But actually...

                        EC3 isn't working yet!

                        Real workload is still:

                        EC2-1

                        EC2-2

                        Both still at

                        80%

                        This is WRONG.

                        Solution

                        AWS ignores EC3 for a while.

                        Warmup = 300 sec

                        During warmup,

                        AWS calculates average using only:

                        EC2-1

                        EC2-2

                        After EC3 becomes ready,

                        AWS starts including it.

                        Now CPU becomes

                        EC2-1 =45%

                        EC2-2 =40%

                        EC2-3 =35%

                        Average

                        40%

                        Now the metric is accurate.
        Health Check Grace Period:
            It is NOT about scaling.
            It is about health checks.
            meaning: prevented the instance from being terminated while it was still starting.
            Imagine:
                Your manager checks every minute:

                "Is Chef C working?"

                But Chef C is still changing clothes.

                Should the manager fire him immediately?

                ❌ No.

                Give him time first.

                This is Health Check Grace Period.
            Real example:
                Suppose EC3 starts.

                    AWS Health Check begins checking.

                    GET /

                    Response?

                    But EC3 is still booting.

                    Amazon Linux

                    ↓

                    Nginx starting

                    ↓

                    PHP starting

                    ↓

                    Laravel starting

                    ↓

                    Database connecting

                    Health Check gets:

                    HTTP 503

                    or

                    Connection refused

                    AWS thinks:

                    Instance unhealthy!

                    Terminate it!

                    Launch another one!

                    That would create an endless loop.
        Comparison:
            | Feature  | Instance Warmup                          | Health Check Grace Period      |
            | -------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
            | Purpose  | Scaling metrics                          | Health checks                  |
            | Used by  | Auto Scaling policies                    | EC2/ALB health checks          |
            | Ignores  | CloudWatch metrics from the new instance | Failed health checks           |
            | Prevents | Wrong scaling decisions                  | Premature instance termination |
        Important:
            ASG only manages the EC2 instances that it created (or that are attached to it).
            It does not manage EC2 instances that you launched manually.

            One Thing Students Often Confuse
                People think:
                    Desired = Current Total EC2
                ❌ Wrong.

                Correct is:
                    Desired = Current EC2 owned by THIS ASG

        Note:
            Auto Scaling = CPU only.
            CloudWatch can trigger scaling based on many different metrics.
            Examples metrics:
                🌐 Web servers → Request count per target (ALB)
                📊 API servers → Average CPU or request rate
                📨 Queue workers → Queue length (e.g., Amazon SQS messages)
                🎥 Video processing → Number of jobs waiting
RDS:
    DB service for Relational Database Service,
    allows: Postres, Mysql, MariaDb, Oracle, Microsoft SQL Server
    advantage using RDS vs on EC2.
        Automated Provisioning
        OS Patching
        Point-in-Time Restore (PITR)
            can restore to: nearest time before mistake [DELETE FROM customers;]
        Monitoring Dashboards
        Read Replicas
            for read request(=read only kinds) -> use replica 1, 2
            for write request(=INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE) -> use primary
            primary to replica 1,2 with asychronous replication, a little 2 sec replication lag.
                asychronous replication: data store on Primary first, return success. and then later, copy to replica.
            To inconsistent data, can write only Primary, and then send to Replica.
            architecture:
                                   Users
                                    │
                        ┌───────────┴───────────┐
                        ▼                       ▼
                    Read Request           Write Request
                        │                       │
                        ▼                       ▼
                Read Replica            Primary RDS
                                                │
                                        Async Replication
                                                │
                                                ▼
                                        Read Replica 
            Can a Read Replica Become the Main Database?
                Yes.

                If needed, AWS lets you promote a Read Replica.

                Primary
                ↓
                Failure

                You can promote:

                Replica
                ↓
                New Primary

                ⚠️ This is usually a manual action.

                Unlike Multi-AZ, Read Replica does not automatically fail over.
            Network Cost:
                in aws there's a network cost when data goes from one AZ to another.
                Within same region, you don't pay that fee. If Cross-region, pay fees.
        Multi-AZ
            use Standby.
                The Standby is not used for application traffic(read/write).
                It only waits for a failure.
                Don't confuse it with Read Replica.

                Suppose your database is:

                Tokyo AZ-A

                Primary RDS

                AWS creates another copy:

                Tokyo AZ-B

                Standby RDS

                Normally:

                Applications
                ↓
                Primary

                Standby is not used.

                If AZ-A fails:

                Primary
                ↓
                Fail

                AWS automatically switches to:

                Standby

                Users usually notice little or no interruption.
                This is Disaster Recovery (DR).
            Synchronous Replication:
                eg: If a customer places an order, return a success response after both(AZs)
                databases have the data.
            Unlike Asynchronous Replication:
                eg: If a customer places an order, return a success response after Primary db saved. 
                later, copy to Replica. there can be a delay.
            automatic faliover:
                if Primary(AZ-A) crash, aws detect : Primary Unhealthy.
                Immediately, AWS promotes the StandBy to Primary.
                still use One DNS endpoint.
                No Manual Intervention.
                    Imagine your application connects like this:
                        mydb.xxxxxx.ap-northeast-1.rds.amazonaws.com

                        Your application always uses one endpoint.

                            DB_HOST=mydb.xxxxxx.ap-northeast-1.rds.amazonaws.com

                        Suppose AZ-A fails.

                        Do you need to change:

                            DB_HOST=...

                        ❌ No.

                        AWS automatically changes where that DNS name points.
                        After failover:
                            DNS
                            ↓
                            New Primary (AZ-B)

                            Your application still uses the same hostname.
                            No code changes.
                            No configuration changes.
        Read Replica VS Multi-AZ
            | Read Replica               | Multi-AZ                           |
            | -------------------------- | ---------------------------------- |
            | 🚀 Improve performance     | 🛡️ Improve availability |
            | Handles read traffic       | Waits as a standby       |
            | Asynchronous replication   | Synchronous replication  |
            | Manual promotion if needed | Automatic failover       |
            | Used for scaling reads     | Not used for scaling     |
            | Improve read performance   | Improve availability               |
            | Read traffic               | Disaster recovery                  |
            | Can serve queries          | Cannot serve queries while standby |
            | Can have multiple replicas | One standby copy                   |
        Easy Remember:
            Read Replica = Performance
            Multi-AZ = High Availability
            Read Replica helps your database work faster. Multi-AZ helps your database survive failures.
        Scaling
        Storage backed by EBS
            RDS stores database files on Amazon EBS.
        Memory Trick:
            | Requirement                     | Choose                  |
            | ------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
            | Want AWS to manage the database | ✅ RDS                   |
            | Need OS access (SSH)            | ✅ Database on EC2       |
            | Improve read performance        | ✅ Read Replica          |
            | Automatic failover              |  Multi-AZ              |
            | Restore to a specific second    | ✅ Point-in-Time Restore |
            | High Availability               | ✅ Multi-AZ              |
            | Disaster Recovery               | ✅ Multi-AZ              |
    Auto Scaling:
        if running out of free storage, it scales automatically.
        have to set: Max Storage threshold
            The threshold protects you from unlimited storage growth and unexpected costs.
            AWS can do: 20 GB -> 30 GB -> 40 GB -> 60 GB -> 100 GB
            AWS doesn't increase storage immediately.
            It waits until all conditions are met.
            Automatic modify:
                free storage < 10% of allocated storage
                low-storage lasts at least 5 min
                6 hr have passed since last modification
        only changes storage capacity.
        does not increase:
            CPU, RAM, Database instance class
        Can Storage Shrink Automatically?
            meaning: if your data much smaller, Will AWS reduce storage back to lower?
        ❌ No.
        Storage Auto Scaling only increases storage.
        It never automatically decreases it.
    RDS Custom:
        Unlike normal RDS:
            AWS manages almost everything: OS, DB, Backup, Monitoring, Maintenance
        AWS gives you access to the operating system.
            Currently, RDS Custom mainly supports:
            Oracle
            Microsoft SQL Server

            Not MySQL or PostgreSQL.
        can connect using ssh or AWS Systems Manager/Session Manager.
            Configure Settings
            Install Patches

            Enable Native Features
            SSH Access
            Automation Mode
                Normally,

                RDS Custom still performs some AWS management.

                Imagine AWS is continuously checking:

                    Database
                    OS
                    Configuration

                If AWS notices something unexpected,

                it may try to restore the supported configuration.

                Suppose you want to make major OS changes.

                AWS says:

                Disable Automation

                Now AWS temporarily stops managing the instance.

                You make your changes.

                Example:

                Install Software
                ↓
                Modify OS
                ↓
                Restart Database

                When finished,
                you can enable automation again.
        Comparison Table:
            | Feature                 | RDS   | RDS Custom |
            | ----------------------- | ----- | ---------- |
            | Managed by AWS          | ✅ Yes | Partial    |
            | SSH Access              | ❌ No  | ✅ Yes      |
            | OS Customization        | ❌ No  | ✅ Yes      |
            | Install Custom Software | ❌ No  | ✅ Yes      |
            | Oracle Support          | ✅ Yes | ✅ Yes      |
            | SQL Server Support      | ✅ Yes | ✅ Yes      |
            | AWS Automation          | Full  | Partial    |
            | Admin Access            | ❌ No  | ✅ Yes      |
        Memory Table:
            | Requirement                                 | Choose       |
            | ------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
            | AWS manages everything                      | ✅ RDS        |
            | Need SSH access                             | ✅ RDS Custom |
            | Need OS customization                       | ✅ RDS Custom |
            | Need Oracle/SQL Server with custom settings | ✅ RDS Custom |
            | Need the easiest managed database           | ✅ RDS        |
    Amazon Aurora:
        Aurora is AWS's own database engine.
            think: 
                MySQL
                    ↓
                AWS improves it
                    ↓
                Aurora MySQL
            or
                PostgreSQL
                    ↓
                AWS improves it
                    ↓
                Aurora PostgreSQL
            Aurora is compatible with MySQL and PostgreSQL, 
                but AWS redesigned the database engine to work better in the cloud.
            Performance:
                5x Faster than RDS MySQL
                3x Faster than RDS PostgreSQL
            storage auto growth:
                AWS automatically grows storage. max: 256 TB
                Normal RDS may need to increase or use Storage Auto Scaling.
            Aurora Replicas:
                very fast, replication lag is less than 15 millisec.
                support 15 replicas.
            Instant Failover
                Normal RDS Multi-AZ
                    if primary is crash, promote StandBy, DNS update(take some time)
                Aurora
                    Writer
                        ↓
                    Crash
                        ↓
                    Reader becomes Writer
                        ↓
                    Almost Immediately

                    AWS calls this:
                        High Availability
                        Native

                    Aurora was designed with high availability from the beginning.
            More expensive:
                than 20% of Standard RDS.
            6 copies of your data
                Each AZ has 2 copies.
                This protects your data if an AZ fails.
                4 out of 6 Needed for Writes.
                    imagine: insert new order.
                        Aurora sends this write to all six storage copies: COPY 1,2,3,4,5,6
                        Does every copy have to respond?
                        ❌ No.
                        Aurora only needs 4 successful acknowledgements. EG: COPY 1,2,3,4
                        if Four copies succeeded.
                        Aurora accepts the write.
                        Why Not Require All 6?
                        Imagine one server is temporarily slow.
                        If Aurora waited for all six copies,
                        every write would become slower.
                        Instead,
                        AWS says: "Four successful copies are enough."
                        This keeps the database both fast and durable.
                3 out of 6 Needed for Reads
                    Aurora doesn't need all six copies.
                    AWS requires 3 healthy copies for reads.
                Self-Healing
                    If one Copy corrupted, Aurora automatically repairs it. It copies data from the healthy copies.
                Peer-to-Peer Replication:
                    all 6 copies as friends.
                    If one copy loses some data,
                    it asks the other healthy copies for the missing data.
                    It doesn't always need the primary database to repair it.
                Storage Is Striped Across Hundreds of Volumes
                    Instead of one huge storage disk,
                    your data is spread across hundreds of storage volumes.
                    Imagine you have a very large book.
                    Instead of storing the whole book in one locker,
                    you divide it into many small sections.
                            Book
                            ↓
                            Chapter 1
                            ↓
                            Chapter 2
                            ↓
                            Chapter 3
                    Each chapter is stored separately.
                    Aurora does the same thing.
                One Aurora Writer
                15 Read Replicas.
                Automatic Failover (<30 Seconds), Aurora immediately chooses one replica.
                The Writer and the Read Replicas all use the same distributed storage layer.
                Unlike standard RDS, Aurora replicas don't each keep a completely separate copy of the database. 
                They share Aurora's distributed storage, which is one reason replication is much faster.
                Notice:
                    Writer Instance = 1
                    Storage Copies = 6
                    These are not the same thing.
                    TWO Layers in Aurora
                                Application
                                    │
                                    ▼
                        +-------------------------+
                        | Aurora Writer Instance  |   ← Compute Layer
                        +-------------------------+
                                    │
                                    ▼
                    +--------------------------------+
                    | Aurora Storage Layer (6 Copies)|  ← Storage Layer
                    +--------------------------------+

                    Aurora Writer Instance purpose = execute SQL
                    Aurora Storage purpose = store data
                Aurora Cluster:
                    Unlike RDS, Aurora is not just one database instance.
                    Aurora groups multiple instances into a Cluster.
                    Think of a cluster as a team.
                        Aurora Cluster
                        ├── Writer Instance (1)
                        ├── Reader Instance (0~15)
                        ├── Reader Instance
                        ├── Reader Instance
                        └── Shared Storage (6 copies across 3 AZs)
                    Cluster Architecture:
                                        Aurora Cluster
                            -------------------------------------------------
                                        Writer Endpoint
                                                │
                                                ▼
                                    +------------------+
                                    |  Writer Instance |
                                    +------------------+
                                                │
                                                │
                                ---------------------------------------
                                Shared Aurora Storage (6 Copies)
                                ---------------------------------------
                                │             │              │
                                ▼             ▼              ▼
                            +-------------+ +-------------+ +-------------+
                            | Reader #1   | | Reader #2   | | Reader #3   |
                            +-------------+ +-------------+ +-------------+
                                    ▲             ▲             ▲
                                        Reader Endpoint

                        Every instance shares the same storage.
                        This is different from RDS.
                    Why is Aurora Replication So Fast?
                        normal DS Read Replica: must copy all changes. Sometimes: 2 sec, 5 sec lag
                        Aurora is different.
                                Writer
                                    │
                                    ▼
                            Shared Storage
                                    ▲
                                Readers

                            The Reader doesn't copy the database.
                            It simply reads from the same distributed storage. replicat lag <10 milliseconds very fast.
                    Writer Endpoint:
                        Applications that write data use. eg: DB_HOST=mydb.cluster-xyz.ap-northeast-1.rds.amazonaws.com
                    Reader Endpoint:
                        Aurora provides another endpoint. Reader Endpoint. eg: READ_DB_HOST=mydb.cluster-ro-xyz.ap-northeast-1.rds.amazonaws.com
                        AWS automatically distributes them.
                            Reader Endpoint
                                ↓
                                Reader1
                                ↓
                                Reader2
                                ↓
                                Reader3
                        No load balancer needed.
                    Aurora Auto Scaling:
                        if Reader Too many read requests, aws automatically create another Reader.
                        How Does Auto Scaling Decide?
                            Aurora uses CloudWatch Metrics.
                                eg: Average Reader CPU > 70%
                                if traffic decreases, aws removes one Reader.
                        Auto Scaling Only Affects Readers.
                        It does NOT create another Writer. Writer must only one.
                    Writer Failover
                        if the Writer crashes, Aurora promotes one Reader.
                        Applications continue using the same Writer Endpoint.
                    Cluster Endpoints:
                        Writer Endpoint
                        Reader Endpoint
                        Instance Endpoint
                            Each instance also has its own endpoint.
                        Normally, applications use:
                            Writer Endpoint
                            Reader Endpoint
                            Not the individual instance endpoints.
                    Aurora Custom Endpoint:
                        define a subset of Aurora instance as a Custom Endpoint
                        eg: run analytical queries on specifc replicas
                            The Problem
                                Suppose your company has two applications.
                                Website
                                    SELECT Product Name
                                    SELECT Price
                                    SELECT Reviews

                                These are small, fast queries.

                                Data Analytics Team

                                Every night they run:
                                    SELECT *
                                    FROM orders
                                    JOIN customers
                                    JOIN products
                                    JOIN payments
                                    WHERE ...
                                This query takes:

                                5 minutes
                                Uses lots of CPU
                                Uses lots of memory

                                If this query goes through the Reader Endpoint...

                                Reader Endpoint
                                ↓
                                Reader1
                                ↓
                                Reader2
                                ↓
                                Reader3

                                AWS may randomly choose Reader2.

                                Now Reader2 becomes busy.

                                Unfortunately,

                                normal website users may also be sent to Reader2.

                                Result:

                                Website becomes slower.
                            Solution: Custom Endpoint

                                Aurora lets you create your own endpoint.

                                Example:

                                Analytics Endpoint
                                ↓
                                Reader3

                                Now:

                                Website:

                                Reader Endpoint
                                ↓
                                Reader1
                                ↓
                                Reader2

                                Analytics:

                                Custom Endpoint
                                ↓
                                Reader3

                                Heavy reports never affect website users.
                            Another Example
                                    Suppose your cluster:
                                        Writer

                                        Reader1
                                        Reader2
                                        Reader3
                                        Reader4

                                    You decide:

                                        Reader1 + Reader2
                                        ↓
                                        Website

                                        Reader3 + Reader4
                                        ↓
                                        Business Intelligence Team

                                    Create:

                                    Website Endpoint
                                    ↓
                                    Reader1
                                    Reader2

                                    and

                                    Analytics Endpoint
                                    ↓
                                    Reader3
                                    Reader4

                                    Each team gets its own Readers.
                        reader endpoint is generally not used after defining custom endpoints.
                            It means:

                                Instead of:

                                Everyone
                                ↓
                                Reader Endpoint

                                companies often use:

                                Web Application
                                ↓
                                Web Endpoint

                                Analytics

                                ↓

                                Analytics Endpoint

                                Reporting

                                ↓

                                Report Endpoint

                                More control.
                    Aurora Serverless:
                        Instead of choosing instance size,
                        AWS adjusts capacity automatically.
                        Everything happens automatically.
                        No Capacity Planning
                            Normal Aurora
                            You ask:
                                How many CPUs?
                                How much RAM?
                                Which instance size?

                            Aurora Serverless
                            AWS decides.
                            No planning.
                        Pay Per Second
                    Global Aurora:
                        Unlike Aurora stores data across 3 AZs, this is for entire Region.
                            if the entire Tokyo Region has a major outage, all AZs(6 copies) will unavailable.
                            Solution: Another Region.
                                Option 1: Aurora Cross-Region Read Replica
                                    Primary Region handles reads and writes.
                                    Secondary Region is read-only.
                                    Good for disaster recovery.
                                        Example
                                            Suppose your customers are only in Japan.

                                            Users
                                            ↓
                                            Tokyo Aurora

                                            You also create:

                                            Singapore: Read Replica

                                            Normally: Nobody uses Singapore.

                                            It's just waiting.
                                            If Tokyo fails,
                                            you can promote the Singapore replica.
                                    But, Promotion is manual.
                                Option 2: Aurora Global Database (Recommended)
                                    Primary Region (read / write)
                                    Secondary Regions Only no Writing. supports up to 16 Aurora Read Replicas.
                                    Helps for decreasing latency
                                    replication lag is less than second
                                    Promoting another region (for disaster recovery) has an RTO of < 1 minute
                                    Typical cross-region replication takes less than I second
                                        RTO: Recovery Time Objective
                                            How long does it take to recover the service?
                                        RPO: Recovery Point Objective
                                            How much data might be lost?
                                Cross-Region Read Replica vs Global Database
                                    | Feature            | Cross-Region Read Replica | Aurora Global Database         |
                                    | ------------------ | ------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
                                    | Disaster Recovery  | ✅                         | ✅                              |
                                    | Cross-Region Reads | ✅                         | ✅                              |
                                    | Replication        | Standard cross-region     | Optimized global replication   |
                                    | Replication Lag    | Higher                    | Usually < 1 second             |
                                    | Failover           | Manual promotion          | Faster promotion (RTO < 1 min) |
                                    | Recommended by AWS | Sometimes                 | ✅ Yes                          |
                    Aurora Machine Learning:
                        eg:
                            Normally, what does a database do?

                            It stores data.

                            Example:
                                SELECT * FROM users;
                                INSERT INTO orders;
                                UPDATE products;

                            Simple CRUD operations.

                            Now imagine an e-commerce website.

                            Customer buys:
                                iPhone
                                AirPods
                                Apple Watch

                            The website wants to answer:

                            "What product should we recommend next?"

                            Can SQL answer that?

                            ❌ No.

                            This is a Machine Learning (ML) problem.
                        Without Aurora Machine Learning,
                            The application has to:
                                Read from Aurora
                                Call another ML service
                                Receive prediction
                                Show result
                                Many steps.
                        With Aurora Machine Learning,
                            Aurora can directly communicate with AWS Machine Learning services
                                Aurora can call AWS ML services using SQL.
                                Think of it like:
                                    SELECT predict(...);

                                Aurora sends the data to an AWS ML service behind the scenes.
                                The application simply receives the prediction.
                        Amazon SageMaker:
                            Need your model.
                            Suppose your company created an AI model.
                            Aurora can send transaction data to SageMaker.
                                SageMaker replies:
                                Fraud Probability: 98%
                        Amazon Comprehend:
                            No model trainning required.
