                            This service understands text.
                            Example:
                            Customer review:

                            "This phone is amazing!"

                            Comprehend analyzes it.

                            Result: Positive

                            Another review:

                            "The product is terrible."

                            Result: Negative

                            This is called: Sentiment Analysis.
                    Babelfish:
                        Babelfish helps Microsoft SQL Server applications run on Aurora PostgreSQL
                        with little or no application code changes.
                        Babelfish is the translator.
                            eg scenairo:
                                First, The Problem
                                    Imagine a company has an old application.

                                    Company System
                                    ↓
                                    Microsoft SQL Server

                                    The application was developed many years ago.

                                    It uses:
                                        SQL Server Driver
                                        T-SQL (Transact-SQL)
                                        SQL Server connection

                                    Example:

                                        SELECT TOP 10 * FROM Customers;

                                    Notice:

                                    TOP 10

                                    This is T-SQL, a Microsoft SQL Server syntax.

                                    Problem

                                    The company wants to migrate to AWS.

                                    Instead of:

                                    Microsoft SQL Server

                                    they want:

                                    Aurora PostgreSQL

                                    Why?
                                        Lower cost
                                        Better scalability
                                        AWS managed service

                                    But...

                                    Aurora PostgreSQL normally understands PostgreSQL syntax.

                                    Example:

                                        SELECT * FROM customers LIMIT 10;

                                    Notice:

                                    LIMIT

                                    instead of:

                                    TOP

                                    These are different SQL dialects.

                                    Without Babelfish

                                    Application sends:

                                        SELECT TOP 10 * FROM Customers;

                                    Aurora PostgreSQL receives:

                                    ❌ ERROR

                                    Because PostgreSQL doesn't understand:

                                    TOP
                                    With Babelfish

                                    Now install Babelfish.

                                    Application still sends:

                                        SELECT TOP 10 * FROM Customers;

                                    Babelfish translates it internally.

                                    Application
                                    ↓
                                    Babelfish
                                    ↓
                                    Aurora PostgreSQL

                                    Internally it becomes:

                                        SELECT * FROM Customers LIMIT 10;

                                    Aurora understands it.

                                    Application works.
                        The same applications can be used after a migration of your database (using AWS SCT and DMS)
                            SCT: Schema Conversion Tool
                                Purpose: Convert database schema.
                                    Example:
                                        SQL Server
                                        ↓
                                        PostgreSQL

                                    Converts:
                                        Tables
                                        Views
                                        Stored Procedures (where possible)
                                        Data types
                            DMS: Database Migration Service
                                Purpose: Move the data.
                                    Example:
                                        SQL Server
                                        ↓
                                        DMS
                                        ↓
                                        Aurora PostgreSQL

                                    Copies:
                                        Customer data
                                        Orders
                                        Products
                            Complete Migration Process
                                Suppose you have:
                                SQL Server

                                Step 1 Use: WS SCT
                                Convert schema.

                                Step 2 Use: AWS DMS
                                Copy data. 

                                Step 3 Enable: Babelfish

                                Application still speaks SQL Server language.
                                Everything works.
                            What Babelfish Does and Doesn't Do?
                                ✔ It Does
                                    Understands many T-SQL commands.
                                    Lets SQL Server applications communicate with Aurora PostgreSQL.
                                    Reduces code changes during migration.
                                ✘ It Doesn't
                                    Convert every SQL Server feature perfectly.
                                    Replace AWS SCT or AWS DMS.
                                    Turn Aurora PostgreSQL into Microsoft SQL Server.
                            Babelfish is a compatibility layer, not a full SQL Server implementation.
                    RDS Backup:
                        Two Types of Backup:
                            Automated backup: (AWS automatically)
                                daily full backup
                                    But What Happens Between backup time and Tomorrow?
                                    If AWS only backed up once a day,
                                    all these changes would be lost.
                                    That's why AWS also saves:
                                Transaction Logs
                                    Every 5 minutes, daily full backup, RDS backs up the database transaction logs.
                                    Because of Point-in-Time Restore (PITR).
                                        Imagine this timeline:
                                        2:00 AM
                                        Daily Backup
                                        ↓
                                        9:00 AM
                                        Customer A created
                                        ↓
                                        11:30 AM
                                        Customer B created
                                        ↓
                                        3:00 PM
                                        Someone deleted all data

                                        Oops!

                                        Can we restore?

                                        Yes.

                                        Not just to:
                                        2:00 AM
                                        We can restore to:
                                        2:59 PM
                                        Just before the mistake.
                                        That's called: Point-in-Time Restore
                                Retention Period:
                                    1~35 days. AWS Keeps your choosen days.
                                    if Retention = 0, AWS disables Automated Backups. No PITR. No automatic recovery.
                            Manual DB Snapshots: (You manually create)
                                AWS never creates it automatically.
                                    You click: Create Snapshot
                                        Example: Before Upgrading Database
                                Retention is foreover.
                            Which One Supports PITR?
                                Automated Backup ✅ Yes
                                Manual Snapshot ❌ No
                            If you Stopped RDS -> Still pay storage.
                                any beginners think: "I stopped my database, so I pay nothing."
                                ❌ Wrong.
                                You still pay for:
                                    Storage (EBS)
                                    Backup storage (if applicable)
                                You only stop paying for the DB instance compute.
                                So, just create snapshot, and then delete RDS.
                                pay only for Snapshot storage cost. very cheap.
                                    flow:
                                        Create Snapshot
                                            ↓
                                        Delete Database

                                        Now: You only pay for: Snapshot Storage
                                    Example
                                            Bad:

                                            Stop RDS

                                            ↓

                                            6 months

                                            ↓

                                            Still paying storage

                                            Better:

                                            Snapshot

                                            ↓

                                            Delete RDS

                                            ↓

                                            6 months

                                            ↓

                                            Restore Snapshot

                                            Lower cost.
                        | Feature                        | Automated Backup                              | Manual Snapshot                              |
                        | ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
                        | Created automatically          | ✅ Yes                                         | ❌ No                                         |
                        | Created manually               | ❌ No                                          | ✅ Yes                                        |
                        | Supports Point-in-Time Restore | ✅ Yes                                         | ❌ No                                         |
                        | Uses transaction logs          | ✅ Every 5 minutes                             | ❌ No                                         |
                        | Retention                      | 1–35 days                                     | Until you delete it                          |
                        | Best for                       | Disaster recovery and accidental data changes | Long-term backup, before upgrades/migrations |
                    Restoring/Migrate a RDS/Aurora Backup:
                        Important: when restore RDS snapshot, AWS does not overwrite the existing database.
                        Restore MySQL RDS from snapshot:
                                AWS create new database from backup snapshot.
                                so, Both databases exist.
                                    Why?
                                    Because AWS wants to prevent accidental overwrites.

                                    Imagine:
                                        Current Database
                                        ↓
                                        Restore Wrong Snapshot
                                        ↓
                                        Everything Lost

                                    That would be dangerous.

                                    Instead AWS creates a new database instance.

                                    You decide later:
                                        Compare data
                                        Test
                                        Switch applications
                        Restore MySQL RDS from S3:
                            AWS simply imports from: s3 directly.
                                scenario:
                                    our office want to import On-Premises MySQL db.
                                    first, upload to s3 using mysqldump.
                                    and then import on rds from s3.
                                    Fast and reliable.
                        Restore Aurora MySQL from S3:
                            Aurora is a little different.
                                AWS recommends using: Percona XtraBackup
                                    Percona XtraBackup:
                                        instead of mysqldump.
                                        Think of it as: Professional MySQL Backup Tool
                                        Advantages:
                                            Faster
                                            Better for large databases
                                            Hot backups (backup without stopping the database)
                                        Why Doesn't Aurora Use mysqldump?
                                            Imagine database size: 5 TB

                                            Using: mysqldump

                                            Very slow.
                                            Percona XtraBackup is optimized for enterprise-scale MySQL databases.
                                Upload to S3.
                                Create Aurora Cluster.
                                choose restore from s3.
                        Comparison Note:
                            | RDS MySQL                | Aurora MySQL                    |
                            | ------------------------ | ------------------------------- |
                            | Import from S3           | Import from S3                  |
                            | Usually uses mysqldump   | Usually uses Percona XtraBackup |
                            | Creates new RDS instance | Creates new Aurora cluster      |
                Aurora Database Cloning:
                    scenair: 
                        if want to test staging env from prod db 10TB.
                        normal flow: prd db -> create snapshot -> restore snapshot -> staging db
                        problem is take time, Storage, money because 10 TB.
                        Aurora Cloning is fixed for this problem.
                    AWS do: "Both databases can share the same storage for now."
                    Architecture:
                            Production Cluster
                                    │
                    ========================================
                    Shared Aurora Storage (10 TB)
                    ========================================
                                    ▲
                                    │
                            Clone Cluster

                    No data is copied.
                    Creation is almost immediate.
                    Why Is It So Fast?
                        No copying needed.
                    Copy-on-Write (CoW):
                        after Clone, Storage still only 100 GB.(same of Prod db 100GB, not 200 GB.)
                        Developer updates the Clone.(update xxx sql),
                        Should Production also change?
                        ❌ No. Production must stay safe.
                        Production is untouched. Without risking Production.
                        AWS now copies only the changed data.
                        After update:
                            Shared Storage 100 GB
                            +
                            Changed Blocks 2 MB
                        that is Copy-on-Write (CoW).
                        When finished,
                            delete the Clone. Simple.
                Aurora Security:
                    at-rest encryption:
                        at-rest means: Data stored on disk.
                            Imagine your database:
                                Customer
                                Orders
                                Payments
                                Product
                            These files are stored on storage (EBS for RDS, Aurora's distributed storage for Aurora).
                            This is called: Data At Rest.
                        AWS uses KMS Key.
                        Must be defined at launch time
                        cannot directly encrypt an existing db.
                            instead, 
                            first create snapshot -> restore snapshot with enable encryption
                            now AWS creates: production-db-encrypted
                        Read Replica Rule:
                            if Primary is unencrypted, Read Replica cannot encrypt.
                            they must match.
                    in-flight encryption:
                        In-flight: means network.
                        using TLS/SSL.
                        Memory Trick:
                            📦At-rest = Lock the box.
                            🚚In-flight = Lock the delivery truck.
                    iam Database authentication:
                        Use IAM Role to connect db
                            instead: normal username and password
                        IAM Role -> Temporary authentication Token.
                            EC2 instance can connect without storing a database password.
                            No permanent password in your application.
                    security groups:
                        Need to control which EC2 instances can access Aurora.
                    no ssh available:
                        normal RDS cannot get ssh access.
                        Only RDS Custom, can access ssh or SSM Session Manager.
                    audit logs can be enabled:
                        Enable:
                            Audit Logs
                        aws records user, timee, sql, ip, etc.. sent them to CloudWatch Logs.
                        Architecture:
                            Aurora
                                ↓
                            Audit Logs
                                ↓
                            CloudWatch Logs
                    Memory:
                        | Requirement                   | AWS Feature                      |
                        | ----------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
                        | Encrypt stored database data  | ✅ KMS (At-rest Encryption)       |
                        | Encrypt data over the network | ✅ TLS/SSL (In-flight Encryption) |
                        | Remove database passwords     | ✅ IAM Database Authentication    |
                        | Restrict network access       | ✅ Security Groups                |
                        | Need SSH access to DB host    | ✅ RDS Custom                     |
                        | Keep audit history            | ✅ Audit Logs → CloudWatch Logs   |
                RDS Proxy:
                    RDS cannot support unlimited connections.
                        eg:  RDS Maximum Connections : 100
                            But Lambda creates: 10,000 Connections
                            result: Connection Timeout, Database Busy, Application Errors
                            Why Are DB Connections Expensive?
                                Opening a connection is not free.
                                Every connection uses:
                                    CPU
                                    RAM
                                    Database threads
                                Example:
                                    Connection 1: 5 MB RAM
                                    1000 connections: 5000 MB RAM
                                Huge waste.
                            Solution → RDS Proxy
                                lambda -> RDS Proxy -> RDS
                    Connection Pooling
                        Without Proxy: 1000 Lambdas -> 1000 Connections -> RDS
                        With Proxy: 1000 Lambdas -> 20 Connections -> RDS
                    Lambda Special:
                        lambda is difference from ec2.
                            ec2 -> always running.
                                maybe 10 ec2 servers
                                10 connections.
                            lambda -> start/stop very quickly. so AWS recommend: RDS Proxy.
                    Failover Improvement:
                        if primary crash, proxy reconnect New Primary behind the scenes. less distruption.
                    Serverless:
                        aws manages everything.
                        RDS Proxy automatically scales.
                    Highly Available:
                        Like RDS Multi-AZ.
                        Proxy runs across multiple AZs.
                        If one AZ fails,
                        Proxy continues.
                    Secrets Manager:
                        instead storing password in app's coding, use "AWS Secrets Manager".
                        RDS Proxy retrieves it automatically.
                    Supported Databases
                        RDS Proxy works with:
                            RDS:
                                MySQL
                                PostgreSQL
                                MariaDB
                                SQL Server

                            Aurora:
                                MySQL
                                PostgreSQL
                    Inside VPC Only
                        RDS Proxy has: Private IP.
                        Only resources inside the VPC 
                        (or connected through VPN/Direct Connect, etc.) can access Proxy.
                    Why No Code Changes?
                        Your application still connects to: Database Endpoint
                        Instead of: RDS Endpoint
                        you simply use: RDS Proxy Endpoint
                        Minimal changes.
                Summary:
                    | Feature               | RDS MySQL                     | Aurora                            |
                    | --------------------- | ----------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
                    | AWS Managed           | ✅                             | ✅                                 |
                    | MySQL Compatible      | ✅                             | ✅                                 |
                    | PostgreSQL Compatible | ✅                             | ✅ (Aurora PostgreSQL)             |
                    | Performance           | Normal                        | Up to 5× faster (MySQL workloads) |
                    | Storage Growth        | Manual / Storage Auto Scaling | Automatic (10 GB increments)      |
                    | Maximum Storage       | Lower (depends on engine)     | 256 TB                            |
                    | Read Replicas         | Supported                     | Up to 15 with very low lag        |
                    | Failover              | Multi-AZ                      | Faster, built-in HA               |
                    | Cost                  | Lower                         | Higher (~20% more)                |

                    | Question says...                 | Answer                    |
                    | -------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
                    | INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE             | Writer Endpoint           |
                    | SELECT queries                   | Reader Endpoint           |
                    | Increase read performance        | Add Aurora Read Replicas  |
                    | Automatically add/remove readers | Aurora Auto Scaling       |
                    | Writer crashes                   | Reader promoted to Writer |
                    | One shared storage               | Aurora Cluster            |
                    | 6 copies across 3 AZs            | Aurora Storage            |

                    | Feature               | RDS                                              | Aurora                                                |
                    | --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- |
                    | Storage               | Each instance has its own storage                | All instances share one distributed storage layer     |
                    | Read Replication      | Replicas copy data from the primary              | Readers share the same storage, so lag is very low    |
                    | Writer                | 1                                                | 1                                                     |
                    | Read Replicas         | Supported (engine-dependent limits)              | Up to 15                                              |
                    | Reader Load Balancing | Application or custom solution                   | Built-in Reader Endpoint                              |
                    | Auto Scaling          | Auto Scaling Group is for EC2, not RDS instances | Can automatically add/remove **Aurora Read Replicas** |
    Amazon ElasticCache:
        Cache:
            Cache Miss: fist user when cache not have state.
            Cache Hit: later user when cache have.
            why Cache So Fast?
                RDS stores data on storage devices.
                Even SSDs are slower than RAM.
                ElastiCache stores data: RAM (Memory)
                RAM is much faster.
        managed service for: Redis or Memcached.
            Redis and Memcached are the database engines.
            ElastiCache is the AWS managed service.
                Amazon ElastiCache
                    │
                    ├── Redis
                    └── Memcached
            think like RDS,
                RDS is a managed database service.
                Inside RDS, you choose a database engine.
                    Amazon RDS
                        │
                        ├── MySQL
                        ├── PostgreSQL
                        ├── MariaDB
                        ├── Oracle
                        └── SQL Server
        in-memory db with high performance, low latency
        Read-Intensive Workloads
        Stateless Applications
            state means: Current information that must be remembered.
            statefull: System/Server က State ကို ကိုယ်တိုင် မှတ်ထားတယ်။
                e.g: stores login sessions locally. Depend on EC2.
            stateless: Server က ဘာမှ မမှတ်ထားဘူး။ လိုရင် Shared Storage(Redis) သွားကြည့်မယ်။
                e.g: stores login sessinons in Redis. Not depend on EC2, EC2 are stateless.
        aws takes care of OS Maintenance/patching, 
            optimizations, setup, configuration, 
            monitoring, failure recovery Just like RDS.
        Only frequently accessed ("hot") data is stored in cache.
        Why Does ElastiCache Require Code Changes?
            Cache Flow:
                Application
                    ↓
                    Check Cache
                    ↓
                    Hit?
                    ↓
                    Yes
                    ↓
                    Return
                    ------------
                    No
                    ↓
                    Read Database
                    ↓
                    Store in Cache
                    ↓
                    Return
            This is called the Cache-Aside Pattern
        Cache Engine:
            Redis:
                feature rich cache.
                Multi AZ with Auto-Failover
                Read Replicas to scale reads and have high availability
                Data Durability using AOF persistence
                    e.g: when electricity goes off, everything lost
                    Redis solves this using AOF.
                    AOF : Append Only File. 
                    Redis က Command တွေကို File ထဲမှာ တစ်ကြောင်းချင်း မှတ်ထားတယ်။
                        Every command
                            SET name Yan

                            Redis writes

                                SET name Yan

                            into a file.

                            Later

                                SET age 30

                            Again

                            saved.

                            File becomes

                                SET name Yan
                                SET age 30
                                SET city Tokyo

                            If Redis crashes
                            AWS restarts Redis.
                            Redis reads the file.

                                SET name Yan
                                SET age 30
                                SET city Tokyo

                            Everything comes back.
                Backup and restore features
                    Redis supports Snapshot Exactly like RDS Snapshot.
                Supports Sets and Sorted Sets
                    Redis isn't just: key=value
                    It supports:
                        String
                        List
                        Set
                        Sorted Set
                        Hash
                        Queue
                        Pub/Sub
                        Streams
                Use Cases:
                    Gaming Leaderboards, Online Shop, Music App.
                        use Sorted Set (ZSET)
                            Redis automatically keeps them sorted by score.
                            You don't need to manually sort after every update.
                            Sorted Sets guarantee uniqueness.
            Memcached:
                simple/High-Speed Cache.
                Store data in RAM as fast as possible.
                Multi-node for partitioning of data (sharding)
                    Suppose you have
                        1 Million Products
                        One Memcached server may not have enough RAM.
                        So AWS lets you create multiple Memcached nodes.

                        Example:

                        Node 1
                        ↓
                        Product 1 ~ 300,000

                        Node 2
                        ↓
                        Product 300,001 ~ 600,000

                        Node 3
                        ↓
                        Product 600,001 ~ 1,000,000

                        This is called: Sharding
                        or
                        Partitioning.

                        Instead of storing everything on one server,
                        we split it across multiple servers.
                No high availability (replication)
                    Redis
                        Primary
                        ↓
                        Replica

                        If Primary dies,
                        Replica becomes Primary.

                    Memcached
                        Node
                        No replica.
                        If server dies
                        Cache Lost
                Non persistent
                    stores only RAM
                    If machine restarts
                    Everything Lost
                    No recovery.
                Backup and restore (Serverless)
                    Traditional Memcached → ❌ No persistence
                    Traditional Memcached → ❌ No backup
                    Redis → ✅ Persistence + Backup
                Multi-threaded architecture
                    Unlike redis, redis is use One Core -> Processes Commands.
                    Memcached:
                        CPU 1
                        CPU 2
                        CPU 3
                        CPU 4
                    All can process requests simultaneously.
            When Should I Choose Memcached?
                Suppose
                    Requirements:
                        Just cache
                        Extremely fast
                        No replication needed
                        No backup needed
                        No persistence needed
                    Choose ✅ Memcached.
                Suppose
                    Requirements:

                    Session storage
                    Replication
                    Failover
                    Persistence
                    Backup

                    Choose ✅ Redis.
        Cache Security:
            layer 1: Security Group like building gate.
                check: "Who is allowed to enter the building?"
            layer 2: Redis AUTH like room guard.
                The office door asks Password?
                can use IAM Authentication without embedding long-live password.
            IAM Policy:
                controls AWS API operations like CRUD. not can read cache data.
            SSL Encryption:
                Network ပေါ်က Data ကိ Encryption လုပ်ပေးတာ။
            Memcached Authentication:
                use SASL(Simple Authentication and Security Layer) unlike Redis use AUTH
            Note:
                Security Group က "အဆောက်အဦထဲ ဝင်လို့ရမရ" ကိုစစ်တယ်။
                Redis AUTH က "Redis ကို သုံးခွင့်ရှိမရှိ" ကိုစစ်တယ်။
                IAM Authentication သုံးရင် Password မရေးတော့ဘူး။ 
                EC2 ရဲ့ IAM Role ကို အသုံးပြုနိုင်တယ်။ ပိုလုံခြုံတယ်။
                IAM Policy က Redis Data ကို ကာကွယ်တာ မဟုတ်ဘူး။ 
                IAM Policy က AWS Resource ကို CRUD access.
                EXAM Trick:
                    Who can create or delete an ElastiCache cluster? => IAM Policy
                    Need network restriction? => Security Group
                    Need password for Redis? => Redis Auth Token
                    Need encrypted communication? => SSL/TLS
                    Need authentication for Memcached? => SASL
        Caching Pattern:
            Lazy Loading (Cache Aside):
                Don't put everything into Redis.
                Only cache data when someone requests it.
                လိုတဲ့အချိန်မှ Cache ထဲ ထည့်တာ။
                ဘာလို့ "Lazy" လို့ခေါ်လဲ?
                    ဘာမှ မကြိုလုပ်ဘူး။
                    User တောင်းမှ လုပ်တယ်။
                    အဲ့ဒါကြောင့် Lazy။
                One Problem is have:
                    Redis ထဲက Data က Database နဲ့ မတူတော့ဘူး။
                    ဒါကို Stale Cache(Data အဟောင်း) လို့ခေါ်တယ်။
                    Suppose Admin changes
                        Price

                        1000$
                        ↓
                        900$

                        RDS updated.

                        Redis

                        still has

                        1000$

                        Oops!

                        Old data.
                        This is called: Stale Data
            Write Through:
                Whenever database changes,
                Update Redis too.
                Database ထဲ Update လုပ်တဲ့အချိန် Redis ကိုလည်း တစ်ခါတည်း Update လုပ်တာ။
            Session Store:
                Session ကို EC2 ထဲ မသိမ်းဘူး။ Redis ထဲသိမ်းတယ်။ => Stateless
                TTL(Time To Live) => session have expire time, 
                    Data ဘယ်လောက်ကြာရင် အလိုလိုဖျက်မလဲ
                    Redis က အလိုလို Delete လုပ်ပေးတယ်။
            famous joke among programmers:
                Cache Invalidation:
                    "Redis ထဲက Data အဟောင်းကို ဘယ်အချိန်မှာ ဖျက်မလဲ?" ဆိုတဲ့ ပြဿနာ။
                    ဥပမာ...
                        Shopee မှာ
                            iPhone
                            1000$
                        ကို Cache လုပ်ထားတယ်။
                        Admin က
                        900$
                        ပြောင်းလိုက်တယ်။

                        အခု
                        Redis ထဲမှာ

                        1000$

                        ကျန်နေတယ်။

                        မေးခွန်းက

                        ဘယ်အချိန် Update လုပ်မလဲ?

                        ချက်ချင်းလား?

                        ၁ မိနစ်နေရင်လား?

                        Redis ကို Delete လုပ်မလား?

                        TTL ကုန်တဲ့အထိ စောင့်မလား?

                        ဒါကို ဆုံးဖြတ်ရတာ
                        တကယ်ခက်တယ်။
                        အဲ့ဒါကို Cache Invalidation Problem လို့ခေါ်တယ်။
                Naming Things:
                    in Programming: difficult to Choose good names for variables, functions, classes, 
                        APIs, databases, tables, etc.
            | Pattern                        | Myanmar Meaning                              | Best For                     |
            | ------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
            | **Lazy Loading (Cache Aside)** | User တောင်းမှ Cache ထဲသိမ်း                  | Read-heavy systems           |
            | **Write Through**              | Database Update လုပ်တာနဲ့ Cache ကိုပါ Update | Data consistency             |
            | **Session Store**              | User Session ကို Redis ထဲသိမ်း               | Load Balancer + Auto Scaling |
        Ports Note:
            Important ports:
                FTP: 21
                SSH: 22
                SFTP: 22 (same as SSH)
                HTTP: 80
                HTTPS: 443
            vs RDS Databases ports:
                PostgreSQL: 5432
                MySQL: 3306
                Oracle RDS: 1521
                MSSQL Server: 1433
                MariaDB: 3306 (same as MySQL)
                Aurora: 5432 (if PostgreSQL compatible) or 3306 (if MySQL compatible)
Route 53:
    AWS's DNS Service.
        Route 53 simply answers:
            "The IP address of www.myshop.com is 18.100.20.30"
    Why is it called Route 53?
        DNS uses
            Port: 53

            Both
                UDP 53
            and
                TCP 53

            That's why
            AWS named it: Route53.
    DNS:
        domain name system
            translate the human friendly hostname into the machine IP addresses.
            www.google.com => 172.217.18.36
            the backbone of internet
            hierarchical naming structure.
        terminnologies:
            Domain Registrar: sell domain names.
            DNS Record: A rule that tells DNS what to do.
                www.myshop.com => 18.100.20.30
            Zone File: all DNS Records stored
            Name Server: DNS Question တွေကိုဖြေပေးတဲ့ Server
                Browser က www.myshop.com ရဲ့ IP ဘာလဲ? လို့မေးတယ်။
                Name Server က 18.100.20.30လို့ပြန်ဖြေတယ်။
            Authoritative vs Non-Authoritative:
                Authoritative: Original Domain Owner
                Non-Authoritative: Copied Answer.
                    Your ISP, Google DNS.
                        may already remember the answer by caching it. 
                        but, doesn't own the domain.
            top-level domains (TLDs) : .com/.net/.org
                last part of domain.
            Second Level Domain (SLD): SLD ဆိုတာ သင်ဝယ်တဲ့ Domain Name
            FQDN: Fully Qualified Domain Name
            http/https: protocol
    Highly Available:
        Route 53 has many DNS servers around the world.
        Route53
            ├── USA
            ├── Japan
            ├── Singapore
            ├── Europe
            └── Australia

            If one server dies,
            another answers immediately.
    Scalable:
        User 100 ယောက်ပဲဖြစ်ဖြစ်
        User 10 သန်းဖြစ်ဖြစ်
        Route53 က
        အလိုအလျောက် Handle လုပ်နိုင်တယ်။
    Fully Managed:
        AWS does everything.
        You only create DNS records.
    Authoritative DNS:
        You can update DNS records when changing IP.
    Domain Registrar:
        domain seller.
    Health Checks
        coolecst Route 53 features.
        Every 30 seconds
        Route53 checks
        Is EC2 alive? if Unhealthy, Route53 change to another server.
    100% Availability SLA:
        AWS Service တွေထဲမှာ 100% SLA ပေးတဲ့ တစ်ခုတည်းသော Service
    Records:
        think Route53 as an address book,
        each entry is called a Record.
        your domain may have many records.
        contains:
            name: domain/subdomain
            record type:
                A means IPv4 address, 
                AAAA means IPv6 address, 
                CNAME means one domain to another domain. no IP.
                    Why CNAME is usefull?
                        if 100 subdomains all point to one server.
                            without CNAME, need to add every A record when IP changes
                            with CNAME, change one A Record only. Much easier.
                    cannot create CNAME with same of Root Domain(Zone Apex)
                        e.g: Root Domain is myshop.com.
                        cannot create CNAME with myshop.com. but, can create : www.myshop.com, etc
                NS means Authoritative Name Servers
                advanced:
                    | Record | Purpose                                                          |
                    | ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
                    | MX     | Mail Server                                                      |
                    | TXT    | Verification / SPF / DKIM                                        |
                    | CAA    | Which CA can issue SSL certificates                              |
                    | SOA    | Zone information                                                 |
                    | PTR    | Reverse DNS (IP → Domain)                                        |
                    | DS     | DNSSEC                                                           |
                    | NAPTR  | SIP/VoIP services                                                |
                    | SPF    | Legacy email sender policy (today usually stored in TXT records) |
            value: answer of Route53 returns.
            routing policy: which server should Route53 returns?
            TTL(time to live): how long should they remember it?
                e.g: 300 sec(5 min)
                The resolver can cache the answer for 5 minutes.
                High TTL: e.g: 24hr
                    less traffic on Route 53
                Less TTL: e.g, 60 sec.
                    More traffic on Route 53, more costs.
                Except of Alias records, TTL is mandatory for each dns record.
                    Alias Record မှာ TTL ကို User က သတ်မှတ်လို့မရဘူး။
                    ဘာကြောင့်လဲ?
                    AWS Resource တွေက
                    IP Address ပြောင်းနိုင်တယ်။
                    ဥပမာ
                        ALB

                        Today
                        ↓
                        54.10.20.30

                        Tomorrow

                        AWS က

                        ALB Infrastructure ပြောင်းလိုက်တယ်။
                        ↓
                        18.200.100.50

                        ဆိုရင်

                        AWS က DNS Cache ကိုလည်း
                        အလိုအလျောက် Handle လုပ်ပေးရမယ်။
                        ဒါကြောင့်
                        TTL ကို AWS ကပဲ စီမံတယ်။
                Suppose
                    Route53
                    www.myshop.com
                    ↓
                    18.100.20.30

                    TTL: 300 sec

                    Google DNS asks.
                    Route53 replies.
                    Google DNS remembers it.

                    For 5 minutes

                    Next user asks
                    www.myshop.com
                    Google DNS doesn't ask Route53 again.
                    It answers directly.
                    Much faster.
                    After 5 minutes
                    Cache expires.
                    Google DNS asks Route53 again.
                Hosted Zones:
                    A Zone File stores DNS records.
                    Public Hosted Zone:
                        Anyone on the Internet can access it.
                        Anyone can query the DNS
                    Private Hosted Zone:
                        Internet အတွက် မဟုတ်ဘူး။ VPC အတွင်းမှာပဲ အသုံးပြုတဲ့ DNS
                        Internet က မမြင်ရဘူး။ VPC ထဲက EC2 တွေပဲ DNS Resolve လုပ်နိုင်
                        Internal System တွေအတွက် အသုံးပြု
                Check domain:
                    nslookup:
                        nslookup domain.top level
                        if nslookup not found: sudo yum install -y bind-utils
                    dig:
                        to query DNS server, Domain Information Groper: dig command
                        Basic Lookup: Check the IP address of a domain.
                            dig example.com
                        Check Specific Records: Query things like TXT, MX, or NS records.
                            dig example.com MX
            CNAME vs Alias:
                CNAME record:
                    Points a hostname to another hostname.
                    Domain → Domain
                    CNAME can point anywhere: external domain: Google, Facebook, GitHub.
                    eg: api.myshop.com
                        ↓
                        my-alb-123456.ap-northeast-1.elb.amazonaws.com
                    CNAME Limitation:
                        CNAME cannot be used for Root Domain / Zone Apex.
                        it only work for sub domain.
                    return hostname.
                        CNAME
                            www.example.com
                            ↓
                            my-alb.amazonaws.com

                            Browser receives

                            my-alb.amazonaws.com

                            Then it performs another DNS lookup.

                            www.example.com
                            ↓
                            CNAME
                            ↓
                            my-alb.amazonaws.com
                            ↓
                            A Record
                            ↓
                            52.xxx.xxx.xxx

                            Two steps.
                Alias record:
                    free.
                    Why did AWS create Alias?
                    Everyone wants to point their root domain to an AWS service.
                    It is not a standard DNS record.
                    It exists only in Route 53.
                        AWS Resource တွေကိုပဲ ညွှန်နိုင်တယ်။
                        Google, Facebook, GitHub
                        စတဲ့ External Domain တွေကို မညွှန်နိုင်ဘူး။
                    Native Health Check
                        AWS Resource နဲ့
                        Native Integration ရှိတယ်။
                        ALB, CloudFront, S3 တို့ရဲ့
                        Health ကို Route53 က တိုက်ရိုက်သိနိုင်တယ်။
                    Alias record is always of type: A/AAAA for aws resources(IPv4/IPv6)
                        Alias Record က
                        ALB ကိုညွှန်ထားပေမယ့် Browser ကို ALB Hostname ပြန်မပေးဘူး။
                        Route53 က ALB ရဲ့ IP Address ကို အရင်ရှာပြီး
                        Browser ကို IP Address ပဲ ပြန်ပေးတယ်။
                        Suppose you create:
                            example.com
                            ↓
                            Alias
                            ↓
                            my-alb.amazonaws.com

                            When a client asks: example.com ?

                            Route53 doesn't return
                                my-alb.amazonaws.com
                            Instead, Route53 first resolves the ALB hostname itself.

                            my-alb.amazonaws.com
                            ↓
                            52.193.xxx.xxx
                            Then Route53 returns the IP Address.
                    Alias record targets:
                        ELB, CloudFront Distributions, API Gateway, Elastic Beanstalk, S3 websites, VPC interface endpoints, 
                        Global accelerator,
                        route 53 record in same hosted zone.
                        You cannot set an Alias record for an EC2 DNS name.
                Differences:
                    | Feature                      | CNAME                      | Alias                                     |
                    | ---------------------------- | -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
                    | Standard DNS                 | ✅ Yes                      | ❌ AWS-only                                |
                    | Points to any hostname       | ✅                          | ❌ AWS resources only                      |
                    | Works for Root Domain        | ❌                          | ✅                                         |
                    | Works for Subdomain          | ✅                          | ✅                                         |
                    | AWS Health Check Integration | ❌                          | ✅                                         |
                    | Route53 Query Charge         | Standard DNS query charges | ✅ Free for Alias queries to AWS resources |
                    | TTL                          | You specify                | AWS manages automatically                 |
    Routing Policy:
        Route53 must decide which answer to return.
        Route53 never sends traffic.
            Route53 က Traffic ကို လမ်းမညွှန်ဘူး။
            သူလုပ်တာက "ဒီ Domain အတွက်
            ဘယ် IP ကို သွားရမလဲ?" ဆိုတဲ့ Question ကို ဖြေပေးတာပဲ။
            ပြီးရင် Browser က IP ကို တိုက်ရိုက်သွားတယ်။
        different decision rules:
            Simple Routing:
                Always return one resource.
                ဘာမှ မစဉ်းစားဘူး။
                User မေးရင် Record ထဲမှာ ရှိတာကို
                ပြန်ပေးလိုက်တာပဲ။
                eg:
                    www.myshop.com
                    ↓
                    18.100.20.30
                    No intelligence.
                    No failover.
                    No weighting.
                Can specify multiple values in the same record.
                    If multiple values are returned, a random one is chosen by the client.
                    Is this Load Balancing?
                    Many beginners think
                    "Wow, that's Load Balancing."
                    ❌ Not really.
                    Because
                        No health check
                        No traffic percentage
                        No intelligent routing

                    It's just
                    multiple IPs.
                    The client decides.
                When Alias enabled, specify only one AWS resource.
                cannot be associated with Health Checks.
            Weighted Routing:
                Instead of
                    Every user goes to Server A
                You can say
                    70% → Server A
                    30% → Server B
                Return records based on percentage.
                    traffic(%) = weight for a specific record/sum of all weights for all records.
                    Weights don't need to sum up to 100.
                    Any numbers are okay. AWS only cares about the ratio.
                DNS records must have same name and type(A/AAAA,etc).
                Can be associated with Health Checks.
                If all records have weight 0, Everyone(server) becomes equal.
                Traffic ကို ရာခိုင်နှုန်းအလိုက် ခွဲပေးတာ
                Deployment strategies:
                    Current version:  1.0
                        Running on EC2 A
                        Customers are using it every day.
                        Now your developer finishes Version 2.0
                        Question:
                        How do you release it?
                        Strategy 1: Blue/Green Development
                            Current website
                            Blue Environment
                            EC2 Blue
                            ↓
                            Version 1

                            Now, deploy Version 2
                            to another server.
                            Green Environment
                            ↓
                            EC2 Green
                            ↓
                            Version 2
                            Notice
                            Both servers exist at the same time.
                                        Customers
                                            │

                                    Route53 / ALB

                                    │       │

                                    Blue     Green

                                Version1  Version2
                            After testing
                                Change DNS or Load Balancer
                                ↓
                                100%
                                ↓
                                Green
                                Blue becomes backup.
                            If something goes wrong?
                                Green Bug
                                ↓
                                Switch back
                                ↓
                                Blue
                                Very easy rollback.
                        Strategy 2: Canary Deployment.
                            Canary is different.
                            Instead of switching everyone, only a few users receive the new version.
                            Version အသစ်ကို Customer အားလုံးကို မပေးသေးဘူး။
                            ဥပမာ
                                1000 ယောက်ရှိရင်
                                950
                                ↓
                                Old Version
                                50
                                ↓
                                New Version
                                အရင်စမ်းကြည့်တယ်။
                                Bug မရှိရင် Traffic ကို တဖြည်းဖြည်းတိုးတယ်။
                            Why called "Canary"?
                                Interesting history 😊

                                Long ago,
                                miners carried a
                                Canary bird
                                into coal mines.

                                Mine
                                ↓
                                Canary

                                If poisonous gas leaked,
                                the canary died first.
                                Workers escaped.
                                The bird became the "early warning."
                                Software copied this idea.
                                Instead of risking
                                all users,
                                risk only
                                a few users.
                                That's why it's called Canary Deployment.
            Failover Routing:
                Primary Server ပျက်ရင် Backup Server ကို ပြောင်းပေးတယ်။
                Disaster Recovery အတွက် သုံးတယ်။
            Latency Routing:
                Latency ဆိုတာ Request တစ်ခု ပို့ပြီး Response ပြန်ရတဲ့ အချိန် ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
                return lowest latency. Network အမြန်ဆုံး Region.
                Can be associated with Health Checks.
                Japan User
                    ↓
                    Tokyo Server
                USA User
                    ↓
                Virginia Server
                ဘယ် Region က Response Time ပိုမြန်လဲ အဲဒီကို DNS ဖြေပေးတယ်။
            Geolocation Routing:
                Geolocation က Latency မကြည့်ဘူး။
                User ဘယ်နိုင်ငံကလဲ အဲဒါကိုပဲ ကြည့်တယ်။
                Japan
                    ↓
                Japanese Website

                Myanmar
                    ↓
                Myanmar Website
            Multi-Value Answer:
                Route53 က Healthy ဖြစ်တဲ့ IP အများကြီး ပြန်ပေးတယ်။
                Browser က တစ်ခုရွေးပြီး Connect လုပ်တယ်။
            Geoproximity:
                uses Actual Distance plus Bias.
                Mostly used with Traffic Flow.
            Summary:
                Simple => Always answer the same
                Weighted => Split by %
                Failover => Primary → Backup
                Latency => fastest region
                Geolocation => country
                Multi Value => return multiple healthy IPS
                Geoproximity => nearest region + Bias
    Health Checks:
        Health Check ဆိုတာ Route53 က Server ကို အမြဲစစ်နေတဲ့ Doctor လိုပါပဲ။
        are only for public resources. 
            Route53 Health Checker က Internet ပေါ်ကလာတာပါ။ ဒါကြောင့် Public IP ရှိတဲ့ Server ကိုပဲ စစ်နိုင်တယ်။
            Health Checkers are AWS services running outside your VPC. 
            They need to access your endpoint through the Internet.
            e.g: if Private EC2, internet to Private EC2 is Impossible because no public IP.
        CloudWatch Alarm Health Check
            health check use cloudwatch, it cannot be checked directly to Private RDS.
                because CloudWatch already monitors CPU, Free Storage, Connections, metrics.
        Calculated Health Checks
            Instead of checking a server, Route53 check other health checks.
                e.g:
                    Health Check A => Healthy
                    Health Check B => Healthy
                    Health Check C => Dead
                    ↓
                    Calculated Health Check
                    ↓
                    Healthy
            combine the results of multiple health checks into a single Health check.
            we can use OR, AND or NOT(Reverse the result).
                real world example:
                    Suppose
                        you own: shop.com
                        Website needs
                        3 services.
                            Web Server
                            API Server
                            Database Proxy

                        Each has Health Check.

                        If all must work
                        ↓
                        Use AND.

                        Visual:
                            Web => Healthy
                            API => Healthy
                            DB => Healthy
                            ↓
                            Website => Healthy.

                            If API fails
                            ↓
                            Website => Fail.
                NOT(Reverse the result):
                    Server က မပျက်ဘူး။ ဒါပေမယ့် Maintenance Mode ဝင်နေတယ်။
                    User က အသုံးမပြုနိုင်ဘူး။
                    ဒါကြောင့် Healthy ဖြစ်နေပေမယ့် Business အတွက် Unhealthy ဖြစ်ချင်တယ်။
                    အဲဒီအချိန် NOT အသုံးဝင်တယ်။
            can monitor up to 256 Child Health Checks.
            perform maintenance without causing all health checks to fail.
            ဥပမာ 
                Server ၂ လုံးရှိတယ်။ Server A ကို Upgrade လုပ်မယ်။ Upgrade လုပ်နေချိန် Health Check Fail ဖြစ်မယ်။
                ဒါပေမယ့် Server B ကောင်းနေသေးတယ်။
                Calculated Health Check မှာ At least 1 လို့ထားရင် Website တစ်ခုလုံး Healthy လို့ပဲ သတ်မှတ်နေမယ်။
                User တွေ Website ကို ဆက်သုံးလို့ရတယ်။
        Integrated with CloudWatch
            Every Health Check produces CloudWatch Metrics.
            in CloudWatch Dashboard, we can see Healthy, Unhealthy, Response Time, Failures.
        Monitor and Endpoint:
            15 global health checkers will check the endpoint health
                Route53 Health Check ဆိုတာ Server ကို Computer တစ်လုံးတည်းက စစ်တာ မဟုတ်ဘူး။ 
                    AWS က ကမ္ဘာအနှံ့မှာရှိတဲ့ Health Checker ၁၅ ခုလောက်က တစ်ပြိုင်တည်းစစ်ပါတယ်။
            Threshold default: 3
                တစ်ခါကောင်းရုံနဲ့ Healthy မဖြစ်ဘူး။ ၃ ကြိမ်ဆက်တိုက် အောင်မှ Healthy ဖြစ်တယ်။
            Interval default: 30 sec
                lower sec > much faster check -> but, more aws costs
            18% Rule:
                If >18% of health checkers report healthy.
                e.g: 3 Healthy, 12 Dead of 15 Global Checkers, 25% > 18%, So, Healthy.
                Health Checker ၁၅ ခုရှိတယ်ဆိုရင် ၃ ခုလောက်က "Server ကောင်းတယ်" လို့ပြောရင် Healthy လို့ သတ်မှတ်နိုင်တယ်။
            all 2xx and 3xx are health status.
            Text Matching:
                Instead of checking only HTTP 200, Route53 can also check specific text: e.g: Application Ready
                because sometime website is live, but, db is die, but, http return 200. it doesnot know.
                so, this specific matching will solved.
            Firewall Rule:
                firewall can block AWS Health Checker IP. So, AWS ရဲ့ Health Checker IP ranges ကို allow လုပ်ထားရပါတယ်။
    Geolocation Routing:
        Geolocation Routing ဆိုတာ User ဘယ်နိုင်ငံ၊ ဘယ်တိုက်၊ ဘယ်ပြည်နယ်က လာတာလဲဆိုတာကို ကြည့်ပြီး
        DNS Record ကို ရွေးပေးတာ ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
        Internet Speed (Latency) ကို မကြည့်ဘူး။
        Restrict Content Distribution:
            ဒီနိုင်ငံမှာပဲ ကြည့်ခွင့်ရှိတယ် ဆိုတဲ့ Content Restriction လုပ်ဖို့ Geolocation Routing သုံးနိုင်တယ်။
        Most Precise Rule Win:
            Rule ၂ ခုရှိတယ်။
                Asia(Continent-တိုက်ကြီး)
                ↓
                Singapore

                Japan
                ↓
                Tokyo

                Japan က Asia ထဲမှာ ပါတယ်။

                ဒါပေမယ့် Country Rule က ပိုတိကျတဲ့အတွက်

                Tokyo ကိုပဲ ရွေးမယ်။
        Real World Usages:
            Netflix, Amazon, GOV web site
        Default Record:
            Rule မရှိတဲ့ နိုင်ငံက User လာရင် ဘယ်ကိုပို့မလဲ? ဒါကြောင့် Default Record လိုတယ်။
            Suppose
                Rules
                    Japan
                    Singapore
                    USA
                Then

                a user comes from Brazil.

                No rule.

                Without Default
                ↓
                DNS fails.
                Instead
                    Create
                        Default
                        ↓
                        Virginia
                    Now

                    Brazil
                    ↓
                    Virginia
        | Latency Routing          | Geolocation Routing                 |
        | ------------------------ | ----------------------------------- |
        | Uses network latency     | Uses user's location                |
        | Chooses fastest Region   | Chooses configured Region           |
        | Performance optimization | Localization and regional rules     |
        | Germany may go to US     | Germany follows Germany/Europe rule |
    Comparison:
        | Routing Policy  | Decision Based On                              | Best Use Case                                  |
        | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
        | **Simple**      | One resource (or random among multiple values) | Basic DNS                                      |
        | **Weighted**    | Configured weights                             | Blue/Green, Canary deployments                 |
        | **Latency**     | Lowest network latency                         | Best performance                               |
        | **Geolocation** | User's continent/country/state                 | Language, legal restrictions, regional content |
        | **Failover**    | Health Check status                            | Disaster Recovery                              |
        | **Multi-Value** | Healthy endpoints                              | Simple DNS load distribution                   |
    Geoproximity Routing:
        If we have two server, aws draw a boundary on between.
        Unlike Geolocation, AWS lets you manually change each server's geographic influence.
            Geoproximity Routing ဆိုတာ User နဲ့ Serverဘယ်သူက
            မြေပုံအရ ပိုနီးလဲဆိုတာကိုကြည့်ပြီးရွေးပေးတာ။
            ဒါပေမယ့် Boundary ကို ကိုယ်တိုင် ပြောင်းလို့ရတယ်။
            ဒါက Geolocation နဲ့ မတူတဲ့အချက်ပါ။
        What is Bias?
            AWS lets you say
                I want Tokyo to serve more users.
                or
                I want Singapore to serve fewer users.

                This is called Bias.
            Positive Bias (+):
                expand(1 to 99), more traffic, larger boundary, more user will get.
                Coverage Area ကို ချဲ့လိုက်တာ။ ပိုများတဲ့ User တွေသွားမယ်။
            Negative Bias (-):
                Shrink (-1 to -99), less traffic,
                Coverage ကို ကျုံ့လိုက်တာ။ ဒါကြောင့် User နည်းသွားမယ်။
        Non-AWS Resources:
            if we own DataCenter, AWS cannot know Geolocation, So need to set Latitude and longitude.
        Why use Geoproximity?
            Suppose
                Tokyo Server is expensive.

                Singapore is cheaper.

                You want more users to go Singapore.

                Set: Singapore => Bias +60

                Singapore serves more users.
        Traffic Flow:
            Must use Route53 Traffic Flow.
            Geoproximity Routing ကို
            Route53 Console မှာ Simple, Weighted, Latency, Geolocation toe lo
            Record တစ်ခုထည့်သလို မလုပ်နိုင်ဘူး။
            Route 53 Traffic Flow ဆိုတဲ့ Advanced Feature ကို သုံးရပါတယ်။
    IP-based Routing:
        Based on user's IP address (CIDR)
            More control than Geolocation.
            Useful when users from the same country should be routed differently.
            Can optimize traffic for known ISP networks.
    Multi-Value Routing:
        maximum return: 8 healthy records although 20 healthy server.
        Multi-Value is not a substitute for having an ELB
            Not Like ELB:
                Route53 က IP Address တွေ ပြန်ပေးရုံပဲ။ 
                ဘယ် Server ကို တကယ်သွားမလဲဆိုတာ Client (Browser / OS)က ဆုံးဖြတ်တာ။
                AWS က မထိန်းချုပ်ဘူး။
            ELB: 
                The Load Balancer makes the decision.
                It continuously monitors the servers and distributes requests.
                Request တစ်ခုချင်းစီကို Load Balancer ကိုယ်တိုင် Server ရွေးပေးတာ။
        When should we use Multi-Value?
            Good for
                Small websites
                Simple redundancy
                Multiple EC2 instances
                Basic availability
            Not good for
                Thousands of requests
                Sticky Sessions
                WebSockets
                Path-based routing
                Cookie-based routing
                Advanced load balancing.
    Route 53 Resolver:
        Every VPC has a built-in DNS server(Route53 Resolver)
        EC2 အချင်းချင်း Hostname နဲ့ ခေါ်နိုင်တာ Route53 Resolver ကြောင့် ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
        By default, it resolves:
            EC2 internal hostnames
            Private Hosted Zone records
            Public DNS names
    Then why do we need Hybrid DNS?
        Hybrid DNS:
            many companies already have their own DNS servers.
                eg: Company Office => Windows DNS => 192.168.1.10
            AWS also has Route53 Resolver.
            Now both need to communicate.
                AWS ထဲမှာ Route53 Resolver ရှိတယ်။ Office ထဲမှာ Windows DNS ရှိတယ်။ နှစ်ခု တစ်ယောက်နဲ့တစ်ယောက် မသိဘူး။
                ဒါကြောင့် Hybrid DNS လိုလာတယ်။
            Networks can be:
                VPC itself / Peered VPC
                On-premises Network (connected through
                Direct Connect or AWS VPN)
            Why VPN or Direct Connect?
                AWS cannot directly access your office.
                Need a network connection.
                Usually
                    Site-to-Site VPN
                    or
                    AWS Direct Connect
            Note:
                Hybrid means
                    Two different environments working together.
            Inbound Endpoint:
                Used when on-premises DNS servers need to resolve AWS private DNS names.
                Requires:
                    VPN or Direct Connect
                    Security Group allowing UDP/TCP port 53
                    Endpoints deployed in at least two subnets/AZs for high availability
                What is an Inbound Endpoint?
                    Allows your on-premises DNS resolver to resolve DNS records inside AWS.
                    Or simply:
                    Outside → AWS
                Inbound Endpoint ဆိုတာ အပြင် (On-Premises) ကနေ AWS ထဲက Private Hosted Zone EC2 Hostnameတို့ကို
                    လာမေးနိုင်အောင် လုပ်ပေးတဲ့ "DNS Door"ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
                Inbound means: into AWS
                flow:
                    Office DNS
                    asks
                    Inbound Endpoint
                    Inbound Endpoint asks
                    Route53 Resolver
                    Route53 Resolver replies
                    10.0.2.50
                    Office receives
                    the answer.
                Why can't Office contact Route53 Resolver directly?
                    Route53 Resolver is managed internally by AWS. It has no public interface.
                    AWS exposes special ENIs(Elastic Network Interfaces) called Inbound.
                High availability:
                    When creating
                        an Inbound Endpoint
                        AWS asks
                        for
                            Subnet A
                            Subnet B
                    Why?
                        High Availability.
                        If one AZ dies, the other still answers DNS queries.
                Security Group:
                    Inbound Endpoint is inside of VPC, SG must allow DNS traffic.
            Outbound Endpoint:
                AWS ---> Outside
                Allows Route 53 Resolver to forward DNS queries from AWS to your on-premises DNS server.
                AWS ထဲက EC2, Lambda စတဲ့ Resources တွေက
                On-Premises DNS ကို မေးချင်တဲ့အခါ အသုံးပြုတဲ့ DNS Door ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
                Flow:
                    Suppose EC2 runs
                        ping printer.office.local
                        Step 1
                            EC2
                            ↓
                            Route53 Resolver
                        Step 2
                            Resolver says

                            "I don't know."

                            But

                            there is

                            a forwarding rule.
                            ↓
                            Forward to

                            Outbound Endpoint
                        Step 3
                            Outbound Endpoint

                            sends

                            the query

                            over VPN.
                            ↓
                            Office DNS.
                        Step 4
                            Office DNS replies

                            192.168.1.50
                        Step 5
                            EC2 gets the answer.
                            Done.
                    Why do we need Forwarding Rules?
                        Outbound Endpoint
                        does NOT automatically know
                        which domains should go to Office.
                        You must create a forwarding rule.
                        e.g:
                            Example
                                office.local
                                ↓
                                Forward to

                                192.168.1.10

                                Now

                                Every time

                                AWS sees

                                *.office.local
                                ↓
                                Forward

                                to

                                Office DNS.
                    Why not just use Public DNS?
                    Security Group:
                        lives inside your VPC. allow UDP 53, TCP 53
                    Two Subnets:
                        for High Availability.
            Important:
                Ask yourself:
                    "Who is asking the DNS question?"

                If the question comes from
                    On-Premises → Inbound Endpoint
                    AWS (EC2/Lambda/etc.) → Outbound Endpoint
Architecture Thinking Framework
    1. What is the application?
    2. What data must be persistent?
    3. What data must be shared?
    4. What needs to scale?
    5. What cannot be lost?
    6. What needs to be fast?
    7. How do we make it Highly Available?

    WhatIsTheTime.com
        requirements:
            ✓ Shows the current time
            ✓ No database
            ✓ Start small
            ✓ Downtime is acceptable
            ✓ Later: vertically and horizontally scalable
            ✓ Eventually: No downtime
        Architecture:
                        Users
                            │
                    Route53
                            │
                    CloudFront

                            │
                    Application Load Balancer
                            │
                Auto Scaling Group
                    │              │
                EC2            EC2
                    │              │
                    (Stateless App)
    Stateful Web App: MyClothes.com
        Requirement:
            • Shopping website
            • Hundreds of concurrent users
            • Need horizontal scalability
            • Keep web application stateless
            • Shopping cart must not be lost
            • User details stored in database
        Breakdown:
            | Requirement               | Keyword                        |
            | ------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
            | Shopping website          | Web Application                |
            | Hundreds of users         | Scalability                    |
            | Horizontal scalability    | Multiple servers               |
            | Stateless web application | Session cannot stay inside EC2 |
            | Shopping cart not lost    | External session storage       |
            | User profile              | Database                       |
        Normal Architecture:
                            Users
                                │
                                ▼
                        Application Load Balancer

                                │
                    Auto Scaling Group (EC2)
                    │                     │
                EC2-1                 EC2-2
                    │                     │
                    ├────────────┐
                    ▼            ▼
            ElastiCache      Amazon S3

            Shopping Cart       Images
                    │
                    ▼
                Amazon RDS

            User Information
        Better Architecture:
                            Users
                                │
                            Route53 DNS
                                │
                            AWS WAF
                                │
                            CloudFront
                                │
                ┌────────────────────────┐

                │   Application Load      │
                │      Balancer           │
                └────────────────────────┘
                                │
                        Auto Scaling Group
                    ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                    EC2                  EC2
                    │                     │
                    ├──────────┬──────────┤
                    ▼          ▼          ▼
                ElastiCache      S3        CloudWatch
                (Shopping Cart) (Images)   (Monitoring)
                    │
                    ▼
                Amazon RDS Multi-AZ
                │
                Read Replica
        Midset:
            | Requirement                | AWS Service               | Why?                                         |
            | -------------------------- | ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
            | Many users                 | ALB + Auto Scaling        | Distribute traffic and scale horizontally    |
            | Stateless app              | EC2 without local session | Any instance can handle any request          |
            | Shopping cart              | ElastiCache Redis         | Fast shared session storage                  |
            | User information           | Amazon RDS                | Persistent relational data                   |
            | Product images             | Amazon S3                 | Durable object storage shared by all servers |
            | Fast global image delivery | CloudFront                | Cache content close to users                 |
            | High availability          | Multi-AZ                  | Survive AZ failures                          |
            | Database read scaling      | Read Replica              | Offload read traffic                         |
            | Protection                 | AWS WAF                   | Block common web attacks                     |
            | Monitoring                 | CloudWatch                | Observe system health                        |
        How to think:
            Where does the traffic enter? → Route 53 → CloudFront (if needed) → ALB
            How does the application scale? → Auto Scaling Group
            Can any EC2 instance handle any request? If not, move session/state outside the instance.
            What data must survive a reboot or instance replacement? → RDS, S3, EFS depending on the data type.
            What needs to be extremely fast but doesn't require permanent storage? → ElastiCache.
            How do we make every layer highly available? → Multi-AZ, health checks, redundancy.
            How do we improve security and monitoring? → Security Groups, IAM, WAF, CloudWatch.
    Stateful Web App: MyWordPress.com
