        Requirements:
            ✓ Fully scalable
            ✓ WordPress
            ✓ Picture uploads
            ✓ MySQL database
        Breakdown:
            | Requirement     | Technical Problem             |
            | --------------- | ----------------------------- |
            | Fully scalable  | Multiple WordPress servers    |
            | Picture uploads | Uploaded files must be shared |
            | MySQL           | Central database              |
            | WordPress       | PHP application               |
        Normal Architecture:
                                Users
                                    │
                                Route53
                                    │
                                CloudFront
                                    │
                                    ALB
                                    │
                        Auto Scaling Group
                        │                  │
                    WordPress          WordPress
                        │                  │
                        └────────┬─────────┘
                                │
                            Amazon EFS
                                │
                            Uploaded Images
                                │
                                ▼
                        Amazon RDS MySQL
        Midset:
            | Requirement        | Solution           | Why                                     |
            | ------------------ | ------------------ | --------------------------------------- |
            | Many users         | ALB + Auto Scaling | Horizontal scaling                      |
            | WordPress files    | EFS                | Shared filesystem for all EC2 instances |
            | Blog database      | RDS MySQL          | Managed, durable relational database    |
            | High availability  | Multi-AZ RDS       | Automatic failover                      |
            | Fast images        | CloudFront         | Global caching                          |
            | Huge media library | S3 (optional)      | Durable, low-cost object storage        |
        How to Think:
            What needs to scale? → Web servers → ALB + Auto Scaling
            What data must be shared between all web servers? → Uploaded files → EFS
            What data must be persistent? → Posts, users, comments → RDS MySQL
            What content is read frequently? → Images → CloudFront (and possibly S3 for media offloading)
    Summary:
        | Project    | Biggest Problem | AWS Solution               |
        | ---------- | --------------- | -------------------------- |
        | WhatIsTheTime  | Shopping Cart   | ElastiCache Redis      |
        | MyClothes  | Shopping Cart   | ElastiCache Redis          |
        | WordPress  | Uploaded Files  | Amazon EFS                 |
    rsync:
        rsync = Remote Sync
        to synchronize files and folders between two locations.
        but only copies the differences, making it much faster than copying everything every time unlike normal: cp.
        rsync -avz \
            /home/app/uploads/ \
            ubuntu@10.0.0.5:/home/app/uploads/
        It uses SSH, so the transfer is encrypted.
    Instantiating Applications quickly:
        EC2 instance:
            golden ami:
                ami: A snapshot/template of an EC2 instance.
                Running EC2
                    ↓
                Create AMI
                    ↓
                Golden AMI
                Why is it called "Golden"?
                    perfect pre-configured server.
                    Everything is already installed.
            User Data:
                Imagine: every server needs a different hostname.
                    e.g: Server-1 => API_URL=prod, Server-2 => API_URL=dev, we cannot put on ami.
                    User Data solves this.
            Hybrid:
                best practice: Golden AMI + User Data.(Elastic Beanstalk)
                Golden AMI already contains all software.
                User Data only does small changes.
        RDS Database:
            RDS Snapshot
        RDS Volume:
            EBS Snapshot
    Developer Problem on AWS:
        Most web app have the same Infrastructure(ALB + ASG)
        want to do single way of deplopment.
        that problem solved by called Elastic Beanstalk.
Elastic Beanstalk:
    PAAS.
    developer centric view of deploying app.
    Managed service:
        automatically handles capacity provisioning, lb, scaling, application health monitoring. instance configuration...
        just app code is responsibility of developer.
    full control over the configuration
    Beanstalk is free but pay for underlying instances.
    When Not to use Elastic Beanstalk?
        | Scenario                        | Better Service                         |
        | ------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
        | Run individual functions        | AWS Lambda                             |
        | Run containers at large scale   | Amazon ECS / Amazon EKS                |
        | Static React/Vue website        | Amazon S3 + CloudFront or AWS Amplify  |
        | Fully customized infrastructure | EC2 + CloudFormation / CDK / Terraform |
    components:
        application(Top Level):
            simply a container that groups everything together.
            collection of components: environments, versions, configurations, ...
        application version: an iteration of you app code.
            Every deployment creates a new version.
            Usually they're stored in S3 behind the scenes.
        environment:
            A running copy of one Application Version.
            One environment always runs one version.
            Environment = Infrastructure
            Each Environment owns AWS resources.Completely separate.
        Environment Tier:
            Web Server Tier:
                common, Handles HTTP, HTTPS requests.
            Worker Tier:
                No users connect directly.
                Instead
                messages come from SQS.
        Note:
            Application = Project
            Application Version = Your code release
            Environment = A deployed, running copy of one version
            Environment Tier = How the application runs (Web Server or Worker)
s3:
    one of main building blocks of AWS.
    infinitely scaling storage.
    many websites use Amazon S3 as backbone.
    for backup and storage.
    disaster recovery, archive, Hybrid cloud storage, application storage, 
    media hosting, data lakes & big data analysic
    software delivery, static website
    Buckets:
        directories to store objects(files)
        region level. s3 look like a global service but, buckets are created in a region.
        Naming:
            shared global namespace: globally unique name. Nobody cannot create same.
            account regional namespace: allows for reuse of same bucket name across regions
        Naming constraints:
            no uppercase, no underscore.
            not an ip, must start with lowercase or number.
            must not start with a prefix xn--
            must not end with a suffix minus -s3alias.
    Objects:
        have a Key.
        Key is everything after the bucket name.
            eg: s3://my-bucket/my_file.txt.
                part of [my_file.txt.] is Key.
            eg: s3://my-bucket/my_folder/another_folder/my_file.txt.
                part of [my_folder/another_folder/my_file.txt.] is Key.
        composed of prefix + object name.
            eg: s3://my-bucket/my_folder/another_folder/my_file.txt.
                part of [my_folder/another_folder/] is prefix.
                part of [my_file.txt] is object name.
        no concept of 'directories' within buckets.
            Why do I see folders?
            Actually
                There are no real folders.
                S3 only stores one string.
                The / is just a character. AWS Console pretends it is a folder 
                    to make it easier for humans.
        Max: 50 TB.
            if >50 GB, must use 'multi-part upload'
        Metadata:
            list of text key/value pairs.
        Tags:
            unicode key/value pair
        Version ID: if versioning is enabled.
    Security:
        User-Based:
            IAM Policies:
                လူကို Permission ပေးတာ
                which API calls should be allowed for a specifc user from IAM.
        Resource-Based:
            Bucket Policies:
                Permission ကို Bucket မှာရေးတာ ဖြစ်တယ်။
                more used.
                bucket wide rules from the s3 console - allows cross account.
                structure:
                    {
                        "Version": "2012-10-17",
                        "Statement": [
                            {
                                "Effect": "Allow",
                                "Principal": "...",
                                "Action": "...",
                                "Resource": "..."
                            }
                        ]
                    }
                    Resource: Which bucket or object does this policy apply to?
                        All objects inside the bucket. eg: arn:aws:s3:::study-files/*
                    Effect: Allow or Deny.
                    Action: Which S3 API operations are allowed?
                    Principal: Who gets this permission?
                        One AWS account: 
                            "Principal": {
                                "AWS": "arn:aws:iam::111122223333:root"
                            }
                        Everyone:
                            "Principal": "*"
                        Usecase:
                            Cross-Account Access:
                                Company A owns the bucket.
                                Company B needs to upload files.
                                Instead of creating IAM users in Company A,
                                Bucket Policy says:
                                Principal
                                ↓
                                Company B Account

                                Now Company B can access.
                            Only One IAM User:
                                Principal
                                    ↓
                                    John
                    Policy Generator:
                        https://awspolicygen.s3.amazonaws.com/policygen.html
            Object Access Control list(ACL):
                Object-level special permissions
                older feature, rarely used.
                    AWS recommends Disable ACLs and use Bucket Policy.
            Bucket Access Control list(ACL):
                less common (can be disabled)
            Bucket settings for Block Public Access:
                created to prevent company data leaks. safety switch.
                    Even if the Bucket Policy says:
                    Allow everyone
                    Block Public Access says:
                    ❌ Ignore that. Keep the bucket private.
        Note:
            AWS checks
                IAM Policy OR Bucket Policy If either allows -> Access.
                BUT there is another rule.
                Someone is DENY, result -> Deny.
                AWS က Permission စစ်တဲ့အချိန်
                    ဒီလိုစဉ်းစားတယ်။
                    IAM မှာ Allow ရှိလား?
                    ဒါမှမဟုတ်
                    Bucket Policy မှာ Allow ရှိလား?
                    တစ်ခုခုမှာ Allow ရှိရင် ဝင်လို့ရတယ်။
                    ဒါပေမယ့် တစ်နေရာရာမှာ Deny ရှိသွားရင် အားလုံးကို ပယ်လိုက်တယ်။
                    ဒါကို AWS မှာ Explicit Deny Overrides Allow
            If there is NO Allow anywhere, the result is DENY.
                AWS has two types of deny:
                    Implicit Deny (default) ← very common
                    Explicit Deny ← overrides everything
                    | IAM Policy | Bucket Policy | Result  | Reason                      |
                    | ---------- | ------------- | ------- | --------------------------- |
                    | Nothing    | Nothing       | ❌ DENY  | Implicit Deny (default)     |
                    | Allow      | Nothing       | ✅ ALLOW | IAM grants access           |
                    | Nothing    | Allow         | ✅ ALLOW | Bucket Policy grants access |
                    | Allow      | Allow         | ✅ ALLOW | Either Allow is sufficient  |
                    | Allow      | Deny          | ❌ DENY  | Explicit Deny overrides     |
                    | Deny       | Allow         | ❌ DENY  | Explicit Deny overrides     |
                    | Deny       | Deny          | ❌ DENY  | Explicit Deny               |
            Final Access = (IAM Allow OR Bucket Policy Allow)
                            AND
                            No Explicit Deny
            | Security Method | Attached To         | Typical Use                                            |
            | --------------- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
            | IAM Policy      | User / Group / Role | Control what identities in your account can do         |
            | Bucket Policy   | Bucket              | Cross-account access, public access, bucket-wide rules |
            | Object ACL      | Individual object   | Legacy, fine-grained object permissions                |
            | Bucket ACL      | Bucket              | Legacy, rarely used
        Encryption: encrypt object in s3 using encryption key.
    Static Website:
        can host static website
        web url depending on the region.
        error.html will show not have route calling.
        http://bucket-name.s3-website-aws-region.amazonaws.com
    Versioning:
        enabled at bucket level.
            without versioning, old file will lost and replace by new.
            with versioning, nothing lost. every upload -> become new version.
            before versioning enabled Uploaded file, will set VersionID: null.
            when delete file, add 'delete markers' on file.
            if undo, delete the 'delete markers' file.
        Suspend:
            after enable, later change to Suspend,
                AWS never delete all versions. previous version still available.
                Only new uploads stop receiving new version IDs. null will set.
    Replicaton:
        Replication means:
            Automatically copy objects from one S3 bucket to another.
        two types:
            CRR: cross-region replicaton.
                Source and destination are in different AWS Regions.
                Why use CRR?
                    Disaster Recovery
                    Lower Latency
                    Compliance
            SRR: same-region replication.
                Source and destination buckets are in the same AWS Region.
                e.g: Log Aggregation
                    Application uploads logs.
                            App
                            ↓
                            logs bucket
                            ↓
                            Replicate
                            ↓
                            Analytics bucket

                            Now another team can analyze logs without touching the production bucket.
                            This is called Log Aggregation.
        requirements:
            must enable versioning on source and destination buckets.
            must grant the necessary IAM permissions.
        Asynchronous:
            Does AWS copy it immediately?
                Not exactly.
                There may be a short delay.
            Asynchronous flow:
                AWS does this:
                    Upload File
                    ↓
                    Upload Success ✅
                    ↓
                    (Background)
                    ↓
                    Replicate to Singapore
                    Your upload finishes immediately.
                    Replication happens later in the background.
            Batch Replicaton:
        Buckets can be in different AWS accounts
            Source Bucket in account A => Destination Bucket in account B.
        IMPORTANT:
            Only new objects uploaded after replication is enabled are replicated.
                Replication ဖွင့်ပြီးမှ အသစ် Upload လုပ်တဲ့ File တွေကိုပဲ AWS က Copy လုပ်ပေးတယ်။
                Replication မဖွင့်ခင်က ရှိပြီးသား File တွေကို အလိုအလျောက် မကူးဘူး။
            For Existing Objects,
                S3 Batch Replication:
                    Replicate everything that already exists.
                S3 Batch Replication also retries failures:
                    Suppose
                        photo1.jpg
                        failed to replicate yesterday.
                        You can ru S3 Batch Replication
                        again.
                        AWS will retry those failed objects.

                | Object Type         | Normal Replication | Batch Replication |
                | ------------------- | ------------------ | ----------------- |
                | New Objects         | ✅ Yes              | ✅ Yes             |
                | Existing Objects    | ❌ No               | ✅ Yes             |
                | Failed Replications | ❌ No               | ✅ Yes             |
        DELETE Operations:
            Some companies want:
                Delete in Production
                ↓
                Delete in Backup
            Others want:
                Delete in Production
                ↓
                Keep Backup
            AWS lets you choose.
            AWS intentionally does not replicate version-specific deletes.
                AWS က Backup ကို ကာကွယ်ချင်တယ်။
                တစ်ယောက်ယောက်က Source Bucket မှာ Version ID နဲ့ Permanent Delete လုပ်လိုက်ရင်
                Destination Bucket ကို မလိုက်ဖျက်ဘူး။ ဒီလိုမှ Backup Copy ကျန်မယ်။
            | Delete Type                       | Replicated? |
            | --------------------------------- | ----------- |
            | Delete Marker                     | ✅ Optional  |
            | Permanent Delete using Version ID | ❌ No        |
        Chaining Replication:
            Replication is not transitive (no chaining).
            AWS intentionally avoids replication loops.
            Suppose
                Bucket A
                ↓
                Replicate
                ↓
                Bucket B
                ↓
                Replicate
                ↓
                Bucket C

                Question: Upload to Bucket A.
                Will it automatically reach Bucket C?
                    If after B -> stops -> doesnot continue to Bucket C.
                AWS intentionally avoids replication loops.
                    Imagine
                        A → B
                        B → C
                        C → A

                        Without this protection:
                        A
                        ↓
                        B
                        ↓
                        C
                        ↓
                        A
                        ↓
                        B
                        ↓
                        C

                        ∞

                        Infinite replication!
                        AWS prevents this.
    Durability:
        Same for all storage class.
        High durability 11 9's of objects across multiple AZs.
    Availability:
        varies depending on storage class
        eg: S3 Standard has 99.99% availability = not available 53 minutes a year.
    Storage Class:
        Minimum storage duration is from the upload date to current date.
        Amazon S3 Standard - General Purpose.
            နေ့တိုင်း အသုံးများတဲ့ File တွေအတွက်။
            default, think: I use this file all times.
            99.99 % availability,
            low latency, high thoughput.
            sustain 2 concurrent facility failures.
            use case:
                Big Data Analytics, mobile & gaming apps, content distribution...
        Amazon S3 Standard-Infrequent Access (IA)
            IA = Infrequent Access
            Files are rarely accessed
            use Cases: Disaster recovery, backups.
        Amazon S3 One Zone-Infrequent Access
            like Standard-IA, but, for only one AZ.
        Amazon S3 Glacier Instant Retrieval
            think like: Archive Storage. Not everyday files.
        Amazon S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval
            retrieval: Minutes to hours
            min storage duration of 90 days.
        Amazon S3 Glacier Deep Archive
            min storage duration of 180 days.
        Amazon S3 Intelligent Tiering
            moves object automatically between Access Tiers based on usage to low-cost.
        Note:
            | Storage Class        | Access Frequency  | Retrieval Speed                                        | Cost                        | Multi-AZ |
            | -------------------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------- | -------- |
            | Standard             | Frequent          | Immediate                                              | Highest                     | ✅        |
            | Standard-IA          | Rare              | Immediate                                              | Lower                       | ✅        |
            | One Zone-IA          | Rare              | Immediate                                              | Even Lower                  | ❌        |
            | Glacier Instant      | Very Rare         | Immediate                                              | Low                         | ✅        |
            | Glacier Flexible     | Archive           | Minutes to Hours                                       | Very Low                    | ✅        |
            | Glacier Deep Archive | Long-term Archive | Hours (up to ~12+ hours depending on retrieval option) | Lowest                      | ✅        |
            | Intelligent Tiering  | Unknown           | Immediate                                              | AWS optimizes automatically | ✅        |

        Don't forget the minimum storage duration:
        | Storage Class              | Retrieval Speed      | Minimum Storage | Typical Use                         |
        | -------------------------- | -------------------- | --------------- | ----------------------------------- |
        | Glacier Instant Retrieval  | Milliseconds         | 90 days         | Archive but immediate access needed |
        | Glacier Flexible Retrieval | 1 minute to 12 hours | 90 days         | Backup and archives                 |
        | Glacier Deep Archive       | 12–48 hours          | 180 days        | Very long-term archive              |
    Life Cycle:
        help automatically save money.
        moving Between Storage Class.
        An automatic schedule that tells S3 what to do with objects as they get older.
        Transition Actions:
            configure objects to transition to another storage class
        Expiration actions:
            configure objects to expire(delete)
        Rules can be created for certain prefix or objects tags
        Scenario 1:
            Your application on EC2 creates images thumbnails after profile photos are uploaded to Amazon S3. 
            These thumbnails can be easily recreated, and only need to be kept for 60 days. 
            The source images should be able to be immediately retrieved for these 60 days, 
            and afterwards, the user can wait up to 6 hours. How would you design this?
            thinking way:
                Source images (original profile photos)
                    First 60 days => fast retrieval => So, S3 Standard.
                    After 60 days -> up to 6 hours -> reduce cost -> S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval
                Thumbnails (generated images)
                    Important words:
                        easily recreated
                        only need to be kept for 60 days.
                        This means: They are not critical data. They are disposable. ec2 can create again and again.
                        Don't need the same durability requirement as original images
                        so, One Zone IA. not Standard-IA because reduce cost.
                        Architecture:
                                 User
                                    |
                                    |
                            Upload Profile Photo
                                    |
                                    ↓
                            +----------------+
                            | Original Image |
                            +----------------+
                                    |
                                    ↓
                                S3 Standard
                                    |
                                60 days later
                                    |
                                    ↓
                        Glacier Flexible Retrieval

                                    |
                                    |
                                    ↓
                                EC2
                            Generate Thumbnail
                                    |
                                    ↓

                            +----------------+
                            |   Thumbnail    |
                            +----------------+
                                    |
                                    ↓
                            S3 One Zone-IA
                                    |
                                60 days later
                                    |
                                    ↓
                                Delete
        Scenario 2:
            A rule in your company states that you should be able to recover your deleted S3 objects 
            immediately for 30 days, although this may happen rarely. After this time, 
            and for up to 365 days, deleted objects should be recoverable within 48 hours
            Architecture:
                            Object created
                                |
                                |
                                ↓

                            S3 Standard
                            (current version)


                            User deletes object
                                |
                                |
                                ↓

                            Delete Marker created

                            Old version becomes:

                            Noncurrent Version


                            Day 0 - Day 30

                            S3 Standard
                            (immediate recovery)


                            Day 30+

                            S3 Standard-IA
                            (immediate but cheaper)


                            Later

                            Glacier Deep Archive
                            (up to 48 hours)


                            Day 365

                            Delete permanently
        Noncurrent Version:
            Before delete:photo.jpg
                Version 1
                (current)

            After delete: photo.jpg
                Delete Marker
                (current)

                Version 1
                (noncurrent)
    Storage Class Analysis:
        help you decide to transition object.
        report is updated daily.
        recommendations for Standard and Standard IA.
            not work for One Zone IA or Glacier.
    Express One Zone:
        maximum performance, not maximum availability across multiple AZs.
        only signle AZ class. similar in concept to One Zone-IA.
        Directory Bucket:
            Unlike normal s3 uses: Bucket -> photos/ documents/ videos/
                S3 doesn't have directories (folders).
            introduces a special type called a Directory Bucket.
            as a bucket optimized for high-performance directory-style access.
            AWS က AI, EMR, Athena, SageMaker တို့လို Directory ပုံစံနဲ့ File သန်းချီပြီး
            အလုပ်လုပ်တဲ့ Application တွေအတွက် Storage Engine အသစ်တစ်ခု ဖန်တီးထားတယ်။
            ဒါကို Directory Bucket လို့ခေါ်တယ်။
        10x Better Performance
        50% Lower Request Costs
        Co-locate your storage and compute resources in the same AZ (reduces latency)
        Use cases: latency-sensitive apps, data-intensive apps, 
            Al & ML training, financial modeling, media processing, HPC...
        Best integrated with SageMaker Model Training, Athena, EMR, Glue, ...
        Do you need Directory Bucket?
            For 95% of applications, the answer is No.
            For example:
                Company website
                User profile images
                PDFs
                Videos
                Static website hosting
                Backups
            Use: ✅ Standard Bucket

            Use a Directory Bucket only when you need things like:
                AI model training
                HPC(High Performance Computing)
                Massive analytics
                Financial modeling
                Very low latency
                Hundreds of thousands of requests per second
    Requester Pays:
        In general,The person who owns the S3 bucket pays everything.
            The bucket owner pays:
                Storage cost
                Request cost (GET, PUT, LIST, etc.)
                Data transfer cost
        With Requester Pays buckets Enabled, 
            Requester pays:
                ✓ GET requests
                ✓ Data transfer
                ✓ Data retrieval costs
            Bucket owner pays:
                ✓ Storage
        Helpful when you want to share large datasets with other accounts
        The requester must be authenticated in AWS (cannot be anonymous)
        Important:
            cannot be anonymous!! Internet User cannot do. AWS cannot identify.
    Event Notifications:
        sometimes we want: When something happens in S3, automatically do something.
        eg: When a user uploads a profile picture, automatically create a thumbnail.
        this is called event-driven architecture.
        Types of S3 Events:
            Object Created:
                Triggered when:
                    Upload object
                    Copy object
                    Multipart upload completed
            Object Removed:
                Triggered when:
                    Delete object
                    Delete multiple objects
            Object Restore:
                Used with Glacier.
            Replication Events:
                related to CRR, SRR.
        S3 Event Notifications can trigger:
            Lambda, SNS, SQS
                when doing on SQS event lab,
                    need to edit to on SQS Policy using policy generator.
                    e.g:
                        {
                            "Version": "2012-10-17",
                            "Statement": [
                                {
                                    "Sid": "Statement1",
                                    "Effect": "Allow",
                                    "Principal": "*", // for everyone
                                    "Action": [
                                        "sqs:SendMessage" // for send msg api
                                    ],
                                        "Resource": "arn:aws:sqs:us-east-1:754660694887:demos3Queue01"
                                    }
                                ]
                            }
        Object Name Filtering: Suffix: *.jpg
        Delivery Timing:
            It is near real-time, but not guaranteed instantly.
        Asynchronous.
        Usage:
            Image Processing, Large File Processing,Multiple Consumers(Lambda/SQS/SNS)
        Event Notifications vs S3 Lifecycle
        |         | Event Notification | Lifecycle       |
        | ------- | ------------------ | --------------- |
        | Purpose | Trigger actions    | Manage storage  |
        | Timing  | Seconds            | Days/months     |
        | Example | Create thumbnail   | Move to Glacier |
        | Service | Lambda/SQS/SNS     | S3 itself       |
    Baseline Performance:
        Performance scales per prefix, and there is no limit to the number of prefixes
        s3 automatically scales.
            we don't need to add servers, disks, LBs.
        AWS guarantees that each prefix:
            | Operation                  | Requests/sec (per prefix) |
            | -------------------------- | ------------------------: |
            | PUT / COPY / POST / DELETE |                 **3,500** |
            | GET / HEAD                 |                 **5,500** |
        Prefix:
            remember: Key = Prefix + Object Name
        Why does AWS care about prefixes?
            Imagine you store everything in one prefix.
                my-bucket

                photos/
                    photo1
                    photo2
                    photo3
                    photo4
                    ...

                All requests hit:
                photos/
                Maximum baseline:
                GET
                5,500/sec
                Now imagine you organize them:
                    photos-us/
                    photos-japan/
                    photos-europe/
                    photos-asia/

                Now AWS can scale each prefix independently.
        GET vs HEAD:
            HEAD is like asking:
            without downloading the file.
                Does this object exist?
                How big is it?
                What's its Content-Type?
        Important:
            Performance scales per prefix, and there is no limit to the number of prefixes
                if 20,000 GET requests per second in one prefix, s3 can handle.
                    but not AWS only guarantees at least 5,500 GET/HEAD requests per second per prefix.
                    As a Solutions Architect, your job is not to hope AWS scales.
                    Your job is to design based on AWS guarantees.
            images/thumbnail/ and images/ are different prefixes.
    Performance:
        Multi-Part upload:
            > 100 MB, must use files > 5 GB
            Maximum S3 object size: 5 TB.
            Failure Handling Benefit
                This is one of the biggest advantages.
                Example:
                10 GB upload.
                Without multipart:
                9.9 GB uploaded
                Network failure
                Restart from 0 GB
                Painful.
                With multipart:
                    Part 1 ✅
                    Part 2 ✅
                    Part 3 ✅
                    Part 4 ❌ failed
                    Part 5 ✅
                Only retry:
                Part 4
                Not the whole file.
        S3 Transfer Acceleration:
            increase transfer speed by transferring file to AWS edge location.
            compatible with multi-part upload.
        Multipart Upload vs S3 Transfer Acceleration:
            Multipart Upload
                Improves:
                    Large file upload
                by:
                    parallel parts
            Transfer Acceleration:
                Improves:
                    Distance between user and S3
                by:
                    CloudFront Edge Locations
        Byte-Range Fetch:
            Request only a specific part (byte range) of an object.
                Instead of:
                    Download everything
                    you say:
                    Give me bytes 0 - 1000
                    or:
                    Give me bytes 5GB - 6GB
            Use Case 1: Parallelize Downloads
                Similar to Multipart Upload.
        Important:
            Upload large file
                    ↓
            Multipart Upload

            Download large file
                    ↓
            Byte-Range Fetch
    S3 Batch Operations:
        Run one operation on millions/billions of existing S3 objects without writing your own script.
        job:
            consists of a list of objects, the action to perform, and optional parameters.
                Manifest(object list): 
                    list of objects to process.
                    Where Does the Object List Come From?
                        Option 1: Provide Your Own Manifest.
                        Option 2: Use S3 Inventory
                            S3 Inventory creates reports.
                            and then 
                                S3 Inventory
                                        |
                                        |
                                        ▼

                                    Athena Query

                                        |
                                        |
                                    Filter objects

                                        |
                                        |
                                    S3 Batch Operations
                            Athena can query S3 Inventory.
                Operation:
                    What should AWS do: copy objects, Encrypt objects, etc..
                Parameters: extra configuration.
            Use Case: Encrypt Existing Objects,
                    Copy Objects Between Buckets, 
                    Modify Metadata, Modify Tags, 
                    Restore Glacier Objects
            Batch Operartions VS Life Cycle:
                |                   | S3 Lifecycle                  | S3 Batch Operations           |
                | ----------------- | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
                | Automatic?        | Yes                           | Manual job                    |
                | Existing objects? | Yes                           | Yes                           |
                | Purpose           | Storage management            | Bulk actions                  |
                | Example           | Move to Glacier after 90 days | Encrypt 1 million objects now |
                | Frequency         | Continuous                    | One-time or scheduled         |
            Batch Operations vs Event Notifications:
                |               | Event Notification  | Batch Operations  |
                | ------------- | ------------------- | ----------------- |
                | Trigger       | New event           | Manual job        |
                | Object timing | New objects         | Existing objects  |
                | Example       | New upload → Lambda | Process old files |
    Storage Lens:
        A dashboard and analytics tool to see how your S3 storage is being used, 
        where costs are coming from, and whether your data protection practices are good.
        collects:
            Storage usage
            Object count
            Request activity
            Cost-related metrics
            Data protection metrics
        Scope: Organization Level.
            Entire AWS Organization
                all accounts, all regions, all buckets.
        Metrics: 
            summary Metrics, cost-optimization Metrics, Data-Protection Metrics, Access-management Metrics.
            event Metrics, Performance Metrics, activity Metrics,
            Detailed Status Code Metrics
            free Vs Paid:
                Free Metrics:
                    automatically available for all customers,
                    contains around 28 usage metrics.
                    data is available for 14 days.
                Paid Metrics:
                    advanced additional paid metrics and features.
                    data is available for 15 months.
        Discover Anomalies:
            normal growth: +10 GB/days, suddenly, yesterday: +5 TB.
            storage lens can help identify: why did storage suddenly increase?
                Possible cause:
                    Application bug
                    Backup mistake
                    Large file uploads
        Can be configured to export metrics daily to an S3 bucket (CSV, Parquet)
        Default Dashboard:
            show multi-region and multi-account data.
            preconfigured by s3
            can't be deleted, but, can be disabled.
        Storage Lens vs S3 Inventory
            |           | Storage Lens                | S3 Inventory         |
            | --------- | --------------------------- | -------------------- |
            | Purpose   | Analyze storage usage       | List objects         |
            | Level     | Organization/account/bucket | Object level         |
            | Metrics   | Storage + activity          | Object metadata      |
            | Frequency | Dashboard + daily export    | Daily/weekly         |
            | Example   | "Which bucket costs most?"  | "Which files exist?" |
        Storage Lens vs CloudWatch
            |         | Storage Lens         | CloudWatch             |
            | ------- | -------------------- | ---------------------- |
            | Focus   | S3 storage analytics | AWS service monitoring |
            | Scope   | S3 organization view | Metrics/alarms         |
            | Example | Storage growth       | Request errors         |
    S3 Encryption:
        encrypt objects in s3 buckets.
        Encryption in transit(SSL/TLS):
            called SSL/TLS.
            two endpoints: HTTP, HTTPS(recommend).
            force encryption in transit:
                Force everyone to use HTTPS instead of HTTP when accessing your S3 bucket.
                on S3 BucketPolicy:
                    aws:SecureTransport: true
                    sample:
                        {
                            "Effect": "Deny",
                            "Principal": "*",
                            "Action": "s3:*",
                            "Resource": [
                                "arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket",
                                "arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket/*"
                            ],
                            "Condition": {
                                "Bool": {
                                "aws:SecureTransport": "false"
                                }
                            }
                        }
                exam point:
                    Force HTTPS
                    Encrypt data in transit
                    Prevent HTTP access to S3
                    Only allow SSL/TLS connections
        At-Rest Encryption:
                SSE-S3
                SSE-KMS
                SSE-C
                Client-Side Encryption
        4 methods:
            SSE(Server side encryption):
                SSE-S3:
                    default, keys handled, managed, owned by AWS. AWS does everything.
                    type: AES-256
                    must set header: "x-amz-server-side-encryption":"AES256"
                    enabled by default for new buckets & new object
                SSE-KMS:
                    using AWS KMS
                    keys handled, managed, owned by AWS KMS.
                    KMS advantages: user access control + audit key usage using CloudTrail
                    must set header: "x-amz-server-side-encryption":"aws:kms"
                    if we use SSE-KMS, impacted by KMS limits.
                        Every upload use: 
                            GenerateDataKey KMS API.
                        Every download use:
                            Decrypt KMS API.
                    KMS quota per sec (5500, 10000, 30000 req/s based on region)
                    can request a quota increase using the service quotas console.
                SSE-C:
                    S3 never stores your key.
                    manage by own key. fully managed by customer.
                        If you lose the key:
                            Data is gone.
                        AWS cannot recover it.
                    HTTPS must be used.
                    key must provided in HTTPS headers, for every HTTP request made.
                    flow:
                        Upload (PUT):
                            Your File
                                +
                            Your Encryption Key
                                ↓
                            HTTPS
                                ↓
                            S3 encrypts the file
                                ↓
                            Stores only the encrypted object
                                ↓
                            Discards your key

                        Download (GET):
                            Encrypted Object
                                +
                            You send the SAME key again
                                ↓
                            HTTPS
                                ↓
                            S3 decrypts temporarily
                                ↓
                            Returns plaintext to you
                                ↓
                            Discards the key again
            Client side encryption:
                S3 never sees plaintext.
                use client libraries such as S3 Client-Side Encryption library.
                clients must encrypt data themselves/decrypt data themselves
                fully managed the keys and encryption cycle.
            DSSE-KMS:
                Dual-Layer Server-Side Encryption with AWS KMS keys
                Two independent encryption layers.
                    Plain File
                        │
                        ▼
                    Encrypt (Layer 1 using KMS)
                        │
                        ▼
                    Encrypt again (Layer 2)
                        │
                        ▼
                    Stored Object
                Why was it introduced?
                    Some organizations (especially government, defense, finance, and regulated industries)
                    require dual-layer encryption to meet compliance standards.
            Default Encryption Vs Bucket Policies:
                SSE-S3 encryption is automatically applied to new objects.
                can 'force encrytion' using Bucket Policy, must be use SSE-S3 default.
                Bucket Policy are evaluated before 'Default encryption'.
    CORS:
        Cross-Origin Resource Sharing:
            Origin = Protocol + Domain + Port
                Example:
                    https://www.example.com
                    means
                        Protocol: HTTPS
                        Host:     www.example.com
                        Port:     443 (default)
                Another example:
                    http://www.example.com:8080

                    Origin is
                        Protocol: HTTP
                        Host: www.example.com
                        Port: 8080
                So, If any Protocol or Domain or Port are different, origin are different.
                So browsers have the: Same-Origin Policy.
                    Same-Origin Policy (SOP) enabled by default.
                    Same-Origin Policy (SOP):
                    The rule:
                        JavaScript from one origin cannot freely read data from another origin.
                        if difference origin => blocked.
                Without CORS:
                    if difference origin => blocked.
                With CORS enabled:
                    if difference origin => allowed.
                    Suppose:
                        Frontend: https://mywebsite.com

                        Images stored in: https://my-images.s3.amazonaws.com

                        User opens: https://mywebsite.com

                        JavaScript requests: https://my-images.s3.amazonaws.com/photo.jpg

                        Browser sees:
                            Origin A
                            ↓
                            Origin B
                            ↓
                            Different Origin

                        Without CORS: Blocked
                    CORS Configuration:
                        [
                            {
                                "AllowedOrigins": [
                                "https://mywebsite.com"
                                ],
                                "AllowedMethods": [
                                "GET"
                                ]
                            }
                        ]
                    Now S3 responds:
                        Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://mywebsite.com

                        Browser says: OK
                        Allowed
                        Show image.
                Important:
                    Many beginners think:
                        S3 blocks the request.
                        Actually:
                        The request usually reaches S3.

                        It is the browser that blocks JavaScript from accessing the response 
                        if the required CORS headers are missing.
                Real-World Example
                    Your React application:
                    https://app.company.com

                    Uploads images to:

                    https://mybucket.s3.amazonaws.com

                    Need CORS.
    MFA:
        muli-factor authentication:
        force users to generate a code on a device.
        MFA will required to 
            permanently delete a object version.
            Suspend versioning on bucket.
        MFA won't required:
            enable versioning, list deleted versions.
        To use MFA delete, Versioning must be enabled on bucket.
        Only bucket owner can enable/disabled MFA Delete.
            Bucket Owner:
                is the AWS Account, not an IAM user.
                eg: 
                    AWS Account (123456789012)
                        │
                        ├── Root User
                        │
                        ├── IAM User: Alice
                        │
                        ├── IAM User: Bob
                        │
                        └── IAM Role: AdminRole
                    Notice: There is only one AWS Account.
                    Inside the account can be many IAM users and roles.
    S3 Access Logs:
        for audit purpose, want to log all access to S3 buckets.
        any request made to S3
        target loggging bucket must be in same AWS region.
        not stored in CloudWatch. store on another s3 Bucket: Logging Bucket.
        And, logging bucket is not same target bucket.
        Warning:
            do not set your logging bucket to monitored bucket.
            it will create logging loop and your bucket will grow exponentially.
            Infinite Logging Loop:
                Suppose:
                    Bucket A
                    ↓
                    Stores logs
                    ↓
                    Bucket A

                    Same bucket.

                    Someone downloads a file: GET photo.jpg
                    S3 creates a log: PUT access-log-1.txt
                    But writing that log is also an S3 request.
                    So S3 logs it too: PUT access-log-2.txt
                    Writing that log creates another request: PUT access-log-3.txt

                    Then: PUT access-log-4.txt
                    ...
                    This continues repeatedly, causing unnecessary log growth and storage costs.
        Important:
            Logging bucket:
                must not be the same Application bucket.
                must be the same Region.
            | Service                      | What it logs                                                            |
            | ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
            | **S3 Server Access Logging** | Requests made **to a specific S3 bucket**                               |
            | **CloudTrail**               | AWS API calls (e.g., `CreateBucket`, `DeleteBucket`, `PutBucketPolicy`) |
            | **CloudWatch Logs**          | Application and system logs from services like EC2, Lambda, ECS         |
    Pre-Signed URLs:
        temporarily access for Private Bucket without requiring AWS credentials.
        as like temporary permission ticket.
        Permissions Inheritance:
            A Pre-Signed URL does not create new permissions.
            It only gives a temporary way to use the permissions that 
            the URL creator already has.
                if URL's Creator has GetObject permission, this Pre-Signed URL also too GetObject permission 
                called Permissions Inheritance.
        Expiration Time:
            URL is temporary. after it expires.
        AWS supports generating it using:
            S3 Console
            AWS CLI
            AWS SDK (Java, PHP, Python, Node.js, etc.)
        Why not make the bucket public?
            Suppose the bucket is public.
                Anyone can access:
                https://bucket/file.pdf

                Forever.

                No expiration.
                No control.

                Pre-Signed URL:

                Access
                ↓
                Only this file
                ↓
                Only this operation
                ↓
                Only until expiration

                Much more secure.
        Note:
            | Requirement                 | Solution           |
            | --------------------------- | ------------------ |
            | Temporary download          | Pre-Signed GET URL |
            | Temporary upload            | Pre-Signed PUT URL |
            | No AWS credentials for user | Pre-Signed URL     |
            | URL expires automatically   | Expiration time    |
            | Permission comes from       | Creator of the URL |
            Before expire URL, admin delete inheritance IAM user acc or permission,
            URL will stop working.
                | Situation                             | Existing Pre-Signed URL              |
                | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
                | URL expires                           | ❌ Stops working                      |
                | Object deleted                        | ❌ Stops working                      |
                | Bucket policy denies                  | ❌ Stops working                      |
                | IAM user permission removed           | Usually ❌ stops                      |
                | IAM user deleted                      | Usually ❌ stops                      |
                | IAM Role temporary credential changes | May continue until credential expiry |
    S3 Glacier Vault Lock:
        Data ကို တစ်ခါရေးပြီးရင် နောက်ထပ် ပြင်လို့မရ၊ 
        ဖျက်လို့မရအောင် lock လုပ်ထားတဲ့ compliance feature ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
        Adopt a WORM (Write Once Read Many) model
        create a Vault lock Policy
            Vault:
                a container for Glacier archives.
        lock the policy for future edits
            (can no longer be changed or deleted)
        What problem does Glacier Vault Lock solve?
            Many companies have compliance requirements:
            Examples:
                Financial records must be kept for 7 years
                Medical records cannot be modified
                Legal documents must not be deleted
            The problem: An administrator might accidentally change/delete archived data.
            AWS provides Vault Lock to prevent this.
        Vault Lock: Prevent modification/deletion.
        Exam Keyword:
            A company needs immutable storage for compliance.
    Object Lock:
        S3 Object တစ်ခုချင်းစီကို သတ်မှတ်ထားတဲ့အချိန်အတွင်း ဖျက်မရ၊ ပြင်မရအောင် lock လုပ်ခြင်း။
        Adopt a WORM (Write Once Read Many) model
        for special object only, not bucket level.
            Individual object version protection
        Retention mode:
            two Object Lock modes:
                Governance Mode:
                    normal user cannot delete, but, special user with s3:BypassGovernanceRetention can delete.
                Compliance Mode:
                    Object versions can't be overwritten or deleted by any user.
                        including root user.
                    Object retention modes can't be changed, and 
                        retention periods can't be shortened.
                            e.g: 10 years to 1 yr. result: Denied.
        Rentention Period:
            protect object for fixed period, can be extended.
                2 yrs => 5 yrs : can extend.
                5 yrs => 1 yr : violate WORM protection.
        Legal Hold:
            no expiration date.
            locked forever until removed.
            until someone manually removes the legal hold.
            Removing Legal Hold:
                can be freely placed and removed using s3:PubObjectLegalHold IAM permission.
        Important:
            "Prevent deletion even by root user" => Object Lock: Compliance Mode.
            "Some administrators can delete if necessary" => Object Lock: Governance Mode.
            "Immutable storage for compliance" => WORM => Object Lock / Vault Lock
            A company must prevent deletion of objects during a lawsuit. The duration is unknown. => S3 Object Lock Legal Hold
            Keep financial records for 7 years. => S3 Object Lock Retention Period
    S3 Access Points:
        when Different teams need different access Key path,
            without Access Point, hard to maintain on Bucket Policy.
        each access points have own security.
        Each Access Point has:
            its own DNS name (Internet Origin or VPC Origin)
                Access Points can control network access.
                Internet Origin:
                    default, user can access via Internet.
                VPC Origin:
                    Only allow access from inside a VPC.
                    Outside users: Block.
                    must create VPC endpoint, VPC Endpoint Policy must allow access to target Bucket/Access Point.
            an Access Point policy (similar to bucket policy)
                Access Point policy does not replace bucket policy.
            We can define the access point to be accessible only from within the VPC
            Why AWS created Access Points?
                simplify security.
                difference applications use the same bucket.
                Network restriction
    S3 Object Lambda:
        key idea:
            without changing the original object,
            allows you to modify an S3 object dynamically before returning it to the requester.
            think:
                Normal S3 Access:

                User
                |
                |
                ▼
                S3 Bucket
                |
                |
                ▼
                Original Object

                With S3 Object Lambda:

                User
                |
                |
                ▼
                S3 Object Lambda Access Point
                |
                |
                ▼
                Lambda Function
                |
                |
                ▼
                S3 Bucket
                |
                |
                ▼
                Original Object

                Lambda sits between the user and S3.
        use case:
            Redacting PII(Personally Identifiable Information)
                Production application needs full data.
                Analytics team should NOT see personal information.
        Solution: S3 Object Lambda.
        Create S3 Object Lambda Access Point.
            an extension of S3 Access Points.
        Object Lambda vs Normal Access Point:
            Control access to S3 objects. => Normal Access Point
            Modify data before returning it. => Object Lambda
CloudFront & AWS Global Accelerator:
    Without CloudFront:
        Origin = Tokyo
        user is on New York, very long distance => higher latency, slower download.
    CloudFront:
        AWS's Content Delivery Network (CDN)
        delivers content to users from locations close to them.
        has many Edge Locations / Points of Presence (PoPs) around the world.
        can become the global front door of your application.
        map: https://aws.amazon.com/cloudfront/features/
        Files are cached for a TTL(maybe a day)
        Edge Location:
            a location where CloudFront can cache and serve content.
        Points of Presence(POPs):
            locations where AWS has infrastructure close to end users.
            Edge Location / CloudFront location where content can be cached and served.
            think:
                PoP
                │
                ├── CloudFront Edge Location  ← CloudFront caching/delivery
                │
                └── Other AWS network infrastructure
        Cache Hit vs Cache Miss:
            for first time user, called Cache Miss:
                User => CloudFront Edge => Not cached => origin 
            for 2nd time user, called Cache Hit:
                User => CloudFront Edge => cached => user
        What kind of content can CloudFront cache?
            common examples: static files.
        CloudFront doesn't replace your origin.
            is usually in front of an origin.
                CloudFront = delivery/caching layer
            not your actual application server.
            possible origins include:
                CloudFront
                    |
                    +── S3
                    |
                    +── ALB
                    |
                    +── EC2
                    |
                    +── API Gateway
                    |
                    +── Custom HTTP server
        CloudFront + ALB
            can also put CloudFront in front of your web application.
            flow:
                CloudFront
                    ↓
                Caching + global delivery
                    ↓
                ALB
                    ↓
                Load balancing
                    ↓
                ASG
                    ↓
                Horizontal scalability
            Using VPC origins
                CloudFront VPC Origins:
                    allows CloudFront to connect to origins inside your VPC
                    without publicly exposed.
                    ALB doesn't need to be Internet-facing. means private ALB.
                    CloudFront → private ALB → private EC2 ASG
                    What about security?
                        application servers don't need public IP addresses.
            Using Public Network:
                CloudFront → Public ALB.
                CloudFront → Public EC2.
                Why would we use the Public Network approach?
                    Your origin is already public.
                    You have an existing Internet-facing ALB.
                    You don't need to keep the origin private.
        DDoS Protection:
            also helps with DDoS protection.
            Why?
            Instead of your origin directly receiving traffic
            AWS provides infrastructure designed to absorb and mitigate large-scale traffic attacks.
        CloudFront + AWS Shield:
            integrates with: AWS Shield
            Shield provides DDoS protection.
            includes protection from common network/transport-layer DDoS attacks through AWS Shield Standard.
        CloudFront + AWS WAF:
            WAF can inspect HTTP/HTTPS requests and block things such as:
            SQL injection
            XSS
            Malicious IPs
            Specific countries/regions
            Suspicious request patterns
            Bot traffic, depending on configuration
        CloudFront Geo Restriction:
            can restrict access distribution.
            Allowlist: allow users in approved countries.
            Blacklist: prevent users in banned countries.
        CloudFront Cache Invalidation:
            Solve problem: 
                I updated the origin, but CloudFront is still serving the old cached content.
            Without invalidation,
                you normally wait for the cached object to expire according to its cache policy/TTL.
            Can Invalidate one file or Path using wildcard or everything(/*)
                Invalidate all cached objects => /*
                Invalidate only images" => /images/*
            Important: Invalidation does NOT delete the origin object.
            Invalidation vs TTL:
                |                 | TTL                     | Invalidation               |
                | --------------- | ----------------------- | -------------------------- |
                | What happens?   | Cache expires naturally | You force cache removal    |
                | Timing          | Wait until expiry       | Can request immediately    |
                | Scope           | Based on cache policy   | Specific path or all files |
                | Example         | 1 hour                  | `/images/*`                |
                | Origin deleted? | ❌                       | ❌                          |
        Note:
            CloudFront = deliver/cache
            Shield     = DDoS protection
            WAF        = HTTP/HTTPS request filtering
            CloudFront vs Route 53:
                Route 53: DNS / routing
                CloudFront: content delivery/caching.
    Global Accelerator:
        improves the network performance and availability of applications
        Unlike CloudFront, Global Accelerator is not primarily a caching service.
        Without Global Accelerator:
            USA User
                |
                | Internet
                | many networks/hops
                ▼
            AWS Tokyo
                |
                ▼
            Application
            The traffic travels across the public Internet.
        No caching:
            biggest difference from CloudFront.
        Global Accelerator uses static Anycast IP addresses:
            when create Global Accelerator, get Two static Anycast IPv4 addresses.
            Unicast: One address → one destination.
            Anycast:
                The same IP address is advertised from multiple AWS locations.
                One static IP can represent multiple AWS network entry points.
            Users connect to these IP addresses.
            The same IP addresses are used globally.
            Anycast IP send traffic directly to Edge Locations.
            Edge Location as the entry point into AWS's global network.
            Anycast → Edge → AWS Network.
        Global Accelerator + ALB:
            Very common architecture:
                        Global Users
                            |
                            ▼
                    Global Accelerator
                            |
                    AWS Global Network
                            |
                            ▼
                            ALB
                            |
                    ┌───────┴───────┐
                    ▼               ▼
                    EC2             EC2
        Global Accelerator + multiple Regions:
            can route traffic to the appropriate healthy endpoint.
            Architecture:
                            Users
                            |
                            ▼
                    Global Accelerator
                            |
                ┌──────────┼──────────┐
                ▼          ▼          ▼
                Tokyo      Singapore     US
                ALB          ALB        ALB
                |            |           |
                EC2          EC2         EC2
        Health checks:
            monitors endpoint health.
            can stop sending traffic to the unhealthy endpoint.
        Traffic Dial:
            can control how traffic is distributed between Regions.
            Useful for:
                Blue/green deployments
                Gradual migration
                Regional traffic management
        Why not just use Route 53?
            Route 53 => DNS-based routing.
            Global Accelerator provides:
                Static Anycast IPs + network-level routing through AWS's global network.
        No issue with client cache(because IP doesn't change)
        Security:
            Only 2 IP need to be whitelisted.
            DDoS protection thanks to AWS Shield.
        CloudFront vs Global Accelerator:
            both use AWS global network and its edge location.
            both integrated with AWS Shield for DDoS protection.
            CloudFront:
                Improves performance for both cacheable content (such as images and videos) 
                Dynamic content (such as API acceleration and dynamic site delivery)
                Content is served at the edge
            Global Accelerator:
                application/network traffic acceleration
                proxies traffic through AWS's global network.
                supports applications using: TCP or UDP
                proxying packets at the edge
                    proxy:
                        Client နဲ့ Server ကြားမှာ အလယ်ကနေ traffic ကို လက်ခံပြီး နောက်တစ်ဖက်ကို ပြန်ပို့ပေးတဲ့အရာ
                            Client က Server ကို တိုက်ရိုက်မသွားဘဲ Proxy ကို အရင်သွားတယ်။   
                            Proxy က request ကို လက်ခံပြီး Server ဆီ ဆက်ပို့တယ်။

                            Forward Proxy → represents the CLIENT
                                The proxy represents the client side.
                                purpose:
                                    Security: want to control internet access.
                            Reverse Proxy → represents the SERVER
                                sits in front of the servers.
                                client doesn't directly communicate with the backend servers.
                                Nginx.
                                    Nginx receive request /users, andthen application server(laravel)
                                CloudFront acts as a reverse proxy/CDN And it adds caching:
                    Packet:
                        Network ပေါ်မှာ data ပို့တဲ့အခါ data ကို သေးသေးလေး အပိုင်းတွေခွဲပြီး ပို့တယ်။ အဲဒီအပိုင်းတွေကို packet လို့ခေါ်
                    User ရဲ့ network traffic ကို User နဲ့နီးတဲ့ AWS Edge Location မှာ အရင်လက်ခံပြီး၊
                    အဲဒီကနေ AWS ရဲ့ global network ကိုသုံးပြီး Application ဆီ ဆက်ပို့ပေးတာ
                static IP addresses
                Deterministic regional failover
                    the traffic doesn't need to wait for DNS TTL-based changes.
                Differences btn CloudFront and Global Accelerator:
                    |                    | **CloudFront**      | **Global Accelerator**           |
                    | ------------------ | ------------------- | -------------------------------- |
                    | Main purpose       | Content delivery    | Network/application acceleration |
                    | Cache              | ✅ Yes               | ❌ No                             |
                    | Edge Locations     | ✅                   | ✅                                |
                    | TCP                | HTTP/HTTPS-oriented | ✅                                |
                    | UDP                | ❌                   | ✅                                |
                    | Static content     | ⭐ Excellent         | Not primary                      |
                    | Dynamic HTTP       | ✅                   | ✅                                |
                    | Gaming             | Not typical         | ⭐ Excellent                      |
                    | IoT                | Not typical         | ⭐ Good                           |
                    | VoIP               | Not typical         | ⭐ Good                           |
                    | Static Anycast IP  | ❌                   | ✅                                |
                    | Regional failover  | Good                | ⭐ Very fast/predictable          |
                    | AWS global network | ✅                   | ✅                                |
Storage Extras:
    AWS Snowball:
        physical Snowball device.
        purpose:
            Migrate terabytes/petabytes of data to AWS.
            Collect and process data at the edge.
        Is Snowball only for migration?
        NO.
        Snowball devices can also be used when data is generated
            somewhere with limited or no Internet connectivity.
        unlike s3: not another permanent storage service.
        Snow Family:
            by size and purpose.
            Snowcone:
                Smallest, think: a small, portable device at a remote edge location.
            Snowball Edge:
                What is "Edge Computing"?
                    Normally:
                        Device / Factory
                            │
                            │ Internet
                            ▼
                        AWS Region
                            │
                            ▼
                        Compute
                    on Edge Computing:
                        Edge computing = processing data close to where the data is created, 
                        instead of sending everything to a distant cloud/data center.
                        Device / Factory
                            │
                            ▼
                        Snowball Edge
                            │
                            ▼
                        Process data locally
                Snowball Edge Storage Optimized:
                    think: I need a lot of storage + some compute.
                Snowball Edge Compute Optimized:
                    think: I mainly need computing power at the edge.
            Snowmobile:
                exabytes, large-scale physical data transfer solution using a shipping container/truck.
        Note:
            CloudFront "Edge Location" mean Bring content closer to users.
                AWS's global CDN infrastructure.
            Snowball "Edge" = remote location 
                where data is being generated or where computing needs to happen.
                Physical Snowball device.
            Snowball cannot import to Glacier directly.
                must use Snowball -> s3 -> s3 lifecycle policy -> Amazon Glacier.
            | Device            | Think           | Main use                             |
            | ----------------- | --------------- | ------------------------------------ |
            | **Snowcone**      | 🥫 Small        | Small/portable edge data collection  |
            | **Snowball Edge** | 📦 Medium/Large | TB/PB data transfer + edge computing |
            | **Snowmobile**    | 🚛 Huge         | Exabyte-scale data transfer          |
Amazon FSx:
    File Storage
    Fully managed file system service on AWS.
    think: "I need a special/high-performance file system, but I don't want to install, configure, patch, maintain, 
        and manage the servers myself."
        not like S3, S3 is object storage
    4 types:
        | FSx type                        | Think about                      |
        | ------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
        | **FSx for Lustre**              | 🚀 High-performance computing    |
        | **FSx for Windows File Server** | 🪟 Windows / SMB                 |
        | **FSx for NetApp ONTAP**        | 🏢 Enterprise / advanced storage |
        | **FSx for OpenZFS**             | 🐧 Linux / ZFS                   |
        Lustre:
            high-performance distributed file system.
            AI/ML training + HPC + Big Data = Lustre
            can integrate with Amazon S3.
            the name Lustre is derived from Linux and Cluster.
            Parallel Distributed File System can handle.
                Distributed = across multiple computer.
                Parallel = access at same time.
            Storage options: SSD vs HDD
                SSD(Solid State Drive):
                    Low latency / small & random file operations
                HDD(Hard Disk Drive):
                    throughput-intensive workloads
                    large & sequential file operations
                Think workloads:
                    | Workload                | Storage |
                    | ----------------------- | ------- |
                    | Low latency             | ⚡ SSD   |
                    | IOPS intensive          | ⚡ SSD   |
                    | Small/random operations | ⚡ SSD   |
                    | Throughput intensive    | 💾 HDD  |
                    | Large files             | 💾 HDD  |
                    | Sequential operations   | 💾 HDD  |

                flow:
                            S3
                        Training Dataset
                            ↓
                    FSx for Lustre
                            ↓
                    EC2 / ML servers
                S3 = store the data
                Lustre = process the data very fast
            FSx File System Deployment Options:
                flow:
                        FSx for Lustre
                                │
                        ┌─────────┴─────────┐
                        ↓                   ↓
                    Scratch             Persistent
                        │                   │
                    Temporary           Long-term
                    No replication      Replicated
                    Very fast burst     More durable
                    Lower cost          Better for important data
                Scratch File System:
                    temporary + cheaper + very fast burst
                        Scratch မှာ data ကို replicate မလုပ်ထားတဲ့အတွက် file server failure ဖြစ်ရင်
                        data ပျောက်နိုင်တယ်။ important permanent data အတွက် မသင့်တော်ဘူး။
                Persistent File System:
                    long-term + replicated + more durable
                    Data is replicated within the same AZ
        Amazon FSx for Windows File Server:
            Windows file system managed by AWS
            smb support.
                SMB = Server Message Block
                    a protocol used for file sharing, especially in Windows environments.
                        flow:
                            Windows PC
                                ↓
                            SMB
                                ↓
                            FSx for Windows
                                ↓
                            Shared folders/files
            NTFS:
                NTFS = New Technology File System
                    Microsoft's file system.
            can integrate with Microsoft Active Directory
                ACL = permission control for files/folders
                User quotas: သုံးနိုင်တဲ့ storage ပမာဏကို ကန့်သတ်ထားတာ
                eg:
                    Company မှာ Windows user account တွေကို Active Directory (AD) နဲ့ manage လုပ်ထားတယ်ဆိုပါစို့။
                    FSx for Windows ကို AD နဲ့ integrate လုပ်ပြီး—
                    ဘယ်သူက ဘယ် folder ကို access လုပ်ခွင့်ရှိလဲ
                    ဆိုတာကို control လုပ်နိုင်ပါတယ်
            can also be accessed/mounte from Linux clients.
