            DFS Namespaces:
                group files across multiple FS
                    File system အများကြီးကို user ဘက်ကနေ တစ်နေရာတည်းလို မြင်အောင် စုစည်းပေးတာ
            Storage options: SSD vs HDD
                SSD(Solid State Drive):
                    Low latency / high performance
                HDD(Hard Disk Drive):
                    General/cheaper purpose
            existing on-premises infrastructure can access FSx.
                using VPN or Direct Connect.
            keyword:
                Windows applications
                SMB
                Windows file sharing
                Active Directory
            Important:
                don't make this mistake:Windows clients only
                FSx for Windows = Windows-based file system, primarily using SMB, 
                    and it can also be accessed from Linux clients.
        Amazon FSx for NetApp ONTAP:
            AWS-managed NetApp storage
            with advanced enterprise file-storage features.
            think: "I need enterprise-grade shared storage with lots of advanced features."
            NFS commonly used with: Linux / Unix
            SMB commonly used with: Windows
            iSCSI commonly used with: Block storage access
            Storage shrinks or grows automatically
            Snapshots
            Replication
            Compression
            Data deduplication
                remove unnecessary copies → save storage
                တူညီနေတဲ့ data ကို ထပ်ခါထပ်ခါ အပြည့်အစုံသိမ်းမယ့်အစား
                duplicate data ကို လျှော့ပြီး storage space ချွေတာတာ
            Point-in-time instantaneous cloning
                can create a clone data.
                    Data ရဲ့ သတ်မှတ်ထားတဲ့အချိန်က state ကို အခြေခံပြီး
                    test environment တစ်ခုကို အလွန်မြန်မြန် ဖန်တီးတာ
                    eg: want to test environments from Production data.
                        Production
                        10 TB data
                            │
                            ↓
                            Clone
                            │
                            ↓
                        Test workload
            keyword:
                Enterprise storage
                NetApp
                NFS + SMB
                Advanced storage management
                Snapshots / cloning / replication
                Existing NAS workload migrating to AWS
        Amazon FSx for OpenZFS:
            Unix/Linux environments.
            managed OpenZFS file system for workloads that use NFS, 
                especially when migrating existing ZFS-based workloads to AWS.
            ZFS is a file system/storage technology
                Network ကနေ တခြား server ရဲ့ file/folder ကို
                ကိုယ့်စက်မှာ local folder လို access လုပ်နိုင်အောင်သုံးတဲ့ protocol
            IOPS = တစ်စက္ကန့်အတွင်း read/write operation ဘယ်နှစ်ကြိမ်လုပ်နိုင်လဲ
            Latency = request လုပ်ပြီး response ရဖို့ ဘယ်လောက်ကြာလဲ
            high IOPS + very low latency workload
            Snapshots
            Compression
            Point-in-time instantaneous cloning
        OpenZFS vs NetApp ONTAP
            ZFS → NFS → OpenZFS
            NetApp → Enterprise NAS → ONTAP
            | Requirement              | Think          |
            | ------------------------ | -------------- |
            | Existing ZFS workload    | 🐧 **OpenZFS** |
            | NFS + ZFS                | 🐧 **OpenZFS** |
            | Existing NetApp workload | 🏢 **ONTAP**   |
            | NetApp ONTAP             | 🏢 **ONTAP**   |
            | NFS + SMB + iSCSI        | 🏢 **ONTAP**   |
            | Enterprise NAS features  | 🏢 **ONTAP**   |
AWS Storage Gateway:
    bridge between on-premises and cloud.
    types of storage:
        S3 file gateway:
            using NFS or SMB, can configure s3 buckets
                (without directly to glacier) later can transition to glacier using life cycle policy
            most recently used data is cached in file gateway.
            using IAM roles to access bucket.
        Volume gateway:
            block storage using iSCSI protocol backed by s3.
            combines on-premises storage + iSCSI + S3 + EBS snapshots
            On-premises မှာရှိတဲ့ server/application တွေက iSCSI block storage ကို အသုံးပြုပြီး
            AWS cloud storage ကို backend အနေနဲ့ အသုံးချနိုင်အောင်လုပ်ပေးတဲ့ service ဖြစ်ပါတယ်။
            Block storage using iSCSI
                iSCSI = Internet Small Computer Systems Interface
                    Network ကနေ remote block storage ကို local disk လို အသုံးပြုနိုင်အောင်လုပ်တဲ့ protocol
            Backed by Amazon S3
            two modes:
                Cached Volumes:
                    AWS stores the main data, local keeps the frequently/recently accessed data
                    low latency access to most recent data
                        Data ရဲ့ အဓိက storage ကို AWS ဘက်မှာထားပြီး 
                        မကြာသေးခင်က/မကြာခဏအသုံးပြုတဲ့ data ကို on-premises မှာ cache လုပ်ထားတာ
                Stored Volumes:
                    entire dataset is on premise
                    On-premises keeps the main data, AWS keeps backups
                        Data အားလုံးကို on-premises မှာ အဓိကထားသိမ်းပြီး၊ schedule အတိုင်း AWS/S3 ကို backup လုပ်ပေးတာ
            can use Amazon EBS snapshots
                On-premise Volume ရဲ့ အချိန်တစ်ခုမှာရှိတဲ့ state ကို backup အနေနဲ့ သိမ်းထားတာ
            Note:
                Cached → Cloud first
                Stored → On-prem first
                CACHED
                    Cloud → Main
                    Local → Cache
                STORED
                    Local → Main
                    Cloud → Backup
        Tape gateway:
            replace physical backup tapes with virtual tapes in AWS, 
                without changing the company's existing backup process too much.
                    from:
                        Company Servers
                            ↓
                        Backup Software
                            ↓
                        Physical Tape
                            ↓
                        📼 Tape Library
                    to:
                        Company Servers
                            ↓
                        Existing Backup Software
                            ↓
                        Tape Gateway
                            ↓
                        Virtual Tape
                            ↓
                        Amazon S3 / Glacier
                Company က အရင်က tape နဲ့ backup လုပ်နေတယ်။
                AWS ကိုပြောင်းချင်ပေမယ့် backup process တစ်ခုလုံးကို ပြန်မပြင်ချင်ဘူး။
            VTL backed by Amazon S3 and Glacier
                VTL = Virtual Tape Library
            Works with leading backup software vendors
    Note:
        user/group file share => NFS/SMB => File Gateway => S3 File Gateway -> s3 --> Glacier
        application Server => iSCSI + Block Storage → Volume Gateway -> s3 --> EBS.
        Backup application => VTL => Tape Gateway -> s3 tap Library -> tap archive(glacier)
AWS Transfer Family:
    A fully-managed service by AWS for file transfers into and out of Amazon S3 or Amazon EFS
    supported protocol:
        FTP: File Transfer Protocol.
        FTPS: FTP over SSL.
        SFTP: Secure File Transfer Protocol
    can use:
        S3 and EFS.
    Highly Available / Multi-AZ
    Authentication:
        Manage credentials itself
            can store/manage user credentials within the service.
        Use an existing authentication system
            with systems such as Microsoft Active Directory
    Note:
        "FTP/FTPS/SFTP နဲ့ user/partner တွေက AWS ရဲ့ S3/EFS ထဲကို file upload/download လုပ်ချင်တယ်" → AWS Transfer Family
        FTP family → Transfer Family
        NFS/SMB → File Gateway
        iSCSI/block → Volume Gateway
        VTL/backup → Tape Gateway
AWS DataSync:
    a high-speed data migration and synchronization service.
        think: moving truck for large amounts of data.
    Main purpose = Move LARGE amounts of data
    Important
        Outside AWS → AWS
            ↓
        Agent needed

        AWS → AWS
            ↓
        No agent
    Agent = software/appliance that you deploy close to your on-premises data source.
    Scheduled replication
        DataSync ကို တစ်ခါပဲ migration လုပ်တာမဟုတ်ဘဲ schedule ထားပြီး data ကို ထပ်ခါထပ်ခါ synchronize လုပ်နိုင်တယ်။
    File permissions and metadata are preserved
        File ကို data တစ်ခုတည်းအဖြစ် ရွှေ့တာမဟုတ်ဘဲ—
        ဘယ်သူပိုင်လဲ၊ group ဘာလဲ၊ permission ဘယ်လိုလဲ စတဲ့ file metadata/permissions တွေကို preserve လုပ်နိုင်တယ်။
    One agent task can use 10 Gbps
        but, you can configure Bandwidth limit.
            Imagine your office internet connection is:
                1 Gbps
                But DataSync tries to use too much bandwidth.
                You don't want:
                    DataSync 🚀🚀🚀
                        ↓
                    Office Internet
                        ↓
                    Other applications ❌

                So you can limit the bandwidth DataSync uses.

                    DataSync
                    ↓
                    Bandwidth limit = X
                    ↓
                    Other applications still have bandwidth
                DataSync က bandwidth အကုန်မစားသွားအောင် သုံးနိုင်တဲ့ bandwidth ကို limit သတ်မှတ်နိုင်တယ်။
    Big Different:
        Transfer Family => "I need FTP/SFTP."
            Typical use:
                External partners
                File exchange
                FTP/FTPS/SFTP
                CRM/ERP file transfers
        DataSync => "I need to move a HUGE amount of data."
            Typical use:
                Data migration
                Large-scale transfer
                Data synchronization
                Recurring replication
        DataSync vs Transfer Family vs Storage Gateway
            📂 "Users/partners transfer files using FTP/SFTP"
                        ↓
                TRANSFER FAMILY

            🚚 "Move/sync a huge amount of data"
                        ↓
                    DATASYNC

            💽 "On-premises application needs AWS-backed storage"
                        ↓
                STORAGE GATEWAY
            Transfer Family → File ပို့/ယူဖို့ FTP/SFTP
            DataSync → Data အများကြီးကို ရွှေ့/Sync လုပ်ဖို့
            Storage Gateway → On-premises application ↔ AWS storage ကို bridge လုပ်ဖို့
Storage Comparison:
    S3: Object Storage
    S3 Glacier: Object Archival
    EBS volumes: Network storage for one EC2 instance at a time
    Instance Storage: Physical storage for your EC2 instance (high lOPS)
    EFS: Network File System for Linux instances, POSIX filesystem
    FS for Windows: Network File System for Windows servers
    FSx for Lustre: High Performance Computing Linux file system
    FS for NetApp ONTAP: High OS Compatibility
    FSx for OpenZFS: Managed ZFS file system
    Storage Gateway: S3 & FS× File Gateway, Volume Gateway (cache & stored), Tape Gateway
    Transfer Family: FTP, FTPS, SFTP interface on top of Amazon S3 or Amazon EFS
    DataSync: Schedule data sync from on-premises to AWS, or AWS to AWS
    Snowcone / Snowball / Snowmobile: to move large amount of data to the cloud, physically
    Database: for specific workloads, usually with indexing and querying
Decoupling Application:
    when deploying multiple applications, they will inevitably need to communicate with one another.
        inevitably = မလွဲမသွေ
    two patterns:
        synchronous communications:
            eg: buying service => shipping service(directly connect)
            when a sudden spike of purchases or whatever,
                synchronous between applications can be a little bit problematic sometimes
                it's better to decouple your applications.
        asnchronous/Event Based communications:
            eg: buying service => queue => shipping service(not directly connect, queue in between)
            To decouple applications:
                using SQS: queue model.
                using SNS: pub/sub model.
                using Kinesis: real-time streaming model.
    SQS:
        Amazon Simple Queue Service
        Queue:
            is like a waiting line.
            The person who comes first is normally served first.
            FIFO = First In, First Out
        whatever msg go into the queue,
        consumers will poll the messages from the queue.
        Producer:
            The application that sends messages to the queue.
            Producing Messages:
                app => AWS SDK's SendMessage API -> SQS queue
                The message is persisted in SQS until a consumer deletes it
                    Producer က message ပို့ပြီးသွားတာနဲ့ ချက်ချင်းပျောက်မသွားဘူး။
                    Consumer က message ကို receive လုပ်ပြီး process လုပ်ပြီးတဲ့အခါ DeleteMessage လုပ်ရတယ်။
                    Consumer က message ကို delete မလုပ်သေးရင် SQS က retention period အတွင်း message ကို သိမ်းထားနိုင်တယ်။
        Consumer:
            The application that reads/processes messages from the queue.
            Consuming Messages:
                Consumer = Queue ထဲက message ကိုယူပြီး အလုပ်လုပ်ပေးတဲ့ application.
                Consumers can be EC2, servers, Lambda...
                consumer's job: 
                    SQS ထဲမှာ message ရှိလား? ရှိရင် ယူပြီး process လုပ်မယ်.
                Poll SQS for message:
                    Poll = "message ရှိလား?" လို့ SQS ကို သွားမေးတာ။
                Receive up to 10 messages at a time.
                    for Efficiency, တစ်ခါ polling လုပ်တဲ့အခါ up to 10 messages ရယူနိုင်တယ်။
                Process the messages
                    Message ကိုရပြီဆိုရင် application က actual work လုပ်တယ်။
                Receive လုပ်တာနဲ့ Delete မဖြစ်ဘူး
                    permanent delete မလုပ်သေးဘူး။
                    အဲဒီအချိန်မှာ SQS ရဲ့ Visibility Timeout
                    ဥပမာ Consumer က message ကိုယူပြီး process လုပ်နေတုန်း crash ဖြစ်သွားတယ်။
                        SQS
                        ↓
                        Consumer
                        ↓
                        💥 EC2 crashes

                        Message ကို Delete မလုပ်ရသေးဘူး။
                        ဒါကြောင့် SQS က message ကို နောက်ပိုင်းမှာ ပြန်ပေးနိုင်တယ်။
                        The golden rule:
                            Receive → Process → Delete
                        Process မအောင်မြင်သေးရင် Delete မလုပ်သင့်ဘူး။
        Flow:
            Producer → SQS Queue → Consumer
        Visibility Timeout:
            after receive msg on consumer, SQS doesn't immediately permanently delete it.
            Instead, the message becomes temporarily invisible to consumers.
            called Visibility Timeout.
        SQS absorbs/buffers the spike.
            buffer:
                SQS acts as a temporary storage area between the producer and consumer,
                allowing the consumer to process messages at its own pace.
            absorb the spike:
                Instead of the sudden traffic spike directly hitting and overwhelming the consumer,
                the queue accepts the messages and holds them until the consumer can process them.
        idea:
            SQS
            │
            └── Order A
                    │
                    ↓
            Shipping Service processing...
                    │
                    ├── SUCCESS → Delete message
                    │
                    └── FAILURE → message becomes visible again
                    So the order isn't simply lost.
        SQS Standard Queue:
            Oldest offering (over 10 years old)
            Fully managed service by AWS.
            Unlimited throughput
                queue ထဲရှိနိုင်တဲ့ message အရေအတွက်အတွက် unlimited
                massive scale အတွက် design လုပ်ထားတာ
            Default Rentention = 4 days, Max: 14 days.
                Retention: SQS က message တစ်ခုကို ဘယ်လောက်ကြာကြာ သိမ်းထားမလဲ ဆိုတာ။
                Consumer က Message A ကို မယူဘူးဆိုရင် SQS က forever သိမ်းထားမှာမဟုတ်ဘူး။
                Retention period ကျော်သွားရင် message ကို SQS က remove လုပ်နိုင်တယ်။
            Low latency (<10 ms(0.01 sec) on publish and receive):
            One SQS message can be up to 1,024 KB (1 MB).
                SQS ≠ S3
                Large file ကို SQS message ထဲ ထည့်ဖို့ မသင့်တော်ဘူး။
                ဥပမာ user က:
                video.mp4 = 500 MB
                ဆိုရင် SQS ထဲကို video file တစ်ခုလုံးထည့်တာ မလုပ်သင့်ဘူး။
                SQS message ထဲမှာ reference ပဲထားနိုင်တယ်:
                    {
                        "bucket": "my-video-bucket",
                        "key": "videos/video123.mp4"
                    }

                    ဒီလို architecture ကို SAA-C03 မှာ အများကြီးတွေ့ရတတ်တယ်။
                        Large object → S3
                        Small message / event → SQS
            at least once delivery
                SQS က message ကို အနည်းဆုံး တစ်ကြိမ်တော့ deliver လုပ်ပေးမယ် လို့ guarantee ပေးတယ်။
                ဒါပေမယ့်...
                တစ်ကြိမ်ထက်ပိုပြီး deliver ဖြစ်နိုင်တယ်။
                ဒါကြောင့် application က duplicate ဖြစ်ရင် ပြဿနာမဖြစ်အောင် design လုပ်သင့်တယ်။
            Messages can be many(Duplicate possible).
            Out of order(Best-effort ordering)
                Standard Queue က strict ordering ကို မ guarantee လုပ်ဘူး။
                Best effort = AWS က order ကို ထိန်းထားဖို့ ကြိုးစားတယ်, ဒါပေမယ့် 100% guarantee မပေးဘူး။
        FIFO Queue:
            FIFO = First In First Out(Messages are processed in the same order they were sent.)
            The idea is that batching allows multiple messages to be handled in one API operation.
                Without batching:
                    300 messages/second

                With batching:
                    3,000 messages/second
            Message deduplication:
                Deduplication ID:
                    producer can provide a: Message Deduplication ID
            Message Group ID:
                Ordering by Message Group ID(mandatory parameter)
                    SQS can maintain ordering for each Producer(customer) independently.
                    eg:
                        FIFO Queue
                        ────────────────────────────
                        Group A:
                        A1 → A2 → A3 → A4

                        Group B:
                        B1 → B2 → B3

                        Group C:
                        C1 → C2 → C3 → C4
                    This allows FIFO to have parallelism while preserving ordering within each group.
            Visibility Timeout still have.
        SQS with ASG:
            SQS acts as a buffer between a fast producer and a slower consumer.
            Remember:
                SQS Queue Length → CloudWatch Metric → CloudWatch Alarm → ASG Scaling → More/Fewer EC2 Consumers
            CloudWatch can monitor the queue and detect:
                Queue length = messages waiting to be processed
            Then CloudWatch can trigger an Alarm.
            Scale OUT.
            when the spike is over, Scale IN.
            architecture:
                 │    Producer   │
                 └───────┬───────┘
                         │
                    SendMessage
                         │
                         ▼
                 ┌───────────────┐
                 │   SQS Queue   │
                 │               │
                 │ 100 messages  │
                 └───────┬───────┘
                         │
                  Queue length
                         │
                         ▼
                 ┌───────────────┐
                 │  CloudWatch   │
                 └───────┬───────┘
                         │
                  threshold reached
                         │
                         ▼
                 ┌───────────────┐
                 │ CloudWatch    │
                 │    Alarm      │
                 └───────┬───────┘
                         │
                   Scaling action
                         │
                         ▼
                 ┌───────────────┐
                 │      ASG      │
                 │               │
                 │ EC2 #1        │
                 │ EC2 #2        │
                 │ EC2 #3        │
                 │ EC2 #4        │
                 └───────────────┘
                         │
                    Process SQS
                    messages
                SQS protects the consumer from the producer's sudden workload.
                CloudWatch monitors the queue backlog, while ASG automatically adjusts the number of workers.
        SQS Security:
            Encryption:
                In-flight encryption = HTTPS
                SQS at rest → KMS
                                SQS
                                │
                        ┌─────────┴─────────┐
                        │                   │
                In transit            At rest
                HTTPS/TLS                 KMS
                Client-side encryption:
                    You can also encrypt the message before sending it to SQS.
                    When the consumer receives it, decrypt and then get original message.
                    need to : your application manages the encryption/decryption.
            IAM Policies:
                IAM Policy → controls what an IAM principal can do with SQS.
            SQS Queue Access Policy:
                own resource-based policy.
                    similar to an S3 Bucket Policy.
                especially useful for cross-account access and AWS services sending messages to the queue.
                    Allow AWS services to send messages
            Note:
                | Requirement                   | Think                      |
                | ----------------------------- | -------------------------- |
                | Encrypt SQS communication     | **HTTPS/TLS**              |
                | Encrypt stored messages       | **KMS / SSE**              |
                | Client manages encryption     | **Client-side encryption** |
                | Control IAM user/role access  | **IAM Policy**             |
                | Cross-account access to queue | **SQS Queue Policy**       |
                | Allow S3/SNS to send to SQS   | **SQS Queue Policy**       |
        SQS Short Polling:
            consumer immediately gets the response.
            Then perhaps it asks again:
                10:00:00  → ReceiveMessage → No message
                10:00:01  → ReceiveMessage → No message
            This creates lots of API calls.
        SQS Long Polling:
            SQS, give me a message if one is available. If there isn't one yet, please wait for a while.
            eg:
                Consumer
                │
                │ ReceiveMessage
                │ WaitTimeSeconds = 20
                ▼
                SQS
                │
                │ ... waiting ...
                │
                │ Message arrives!
                ▼
                Consumer receives message
            Instead of immediately saying: "There are no messages."
            reduce API calls.
            WaitTimeSeconds:
                wait time can be between 1 sec to 20 sec
                20 seconds is generally preferred for long polling.
                20 sec is not exactly waiting time. SQS can return the message immediately.
                Queue-level configuration:
                    consumers can use long polling without specifying it on every request.
                        MyQueue
                            │
                            └── ReceiveMessageWaitTimeSeconds = 20
                API-level configuration:
                    consumer can controls the waiting behavior for particular API call.
        Important:
            SQS doesn't process the message.
            SQS itself isn't your business application.
                It doesn't say: "I'll ship the package."
                It only says: "I'll safely hold this message until a consumer processes it."
                So:
                    Producer
                    ↓
                    SQS
                    ↓
                    Consumer

                SQS = message storage / waiting line
                Consumer = does the actual work
            on AWS management console, 
                delete = Delete the entire SQS queue itself.
                purge = completely clean/remove the contents.
                    Delete all messages inside the queue, but keep the queue itself.
            Standard = high throughput, ordering not guaranteed
                🚀 Very high throughput
                ❌ Ordering not guaranteed
                ⚠️ Duplicate messages possible
            FIFO = ordering guaranteed, throughput is more limited
                Ordering + deduplication
                FIFO
                ├── 300 msg/s without batching
                ├── 3,000 msg/s with batching
                ├── Deduplication ID
                └── Message Group ID = mandatory
                Message Group ID → controls ordering lane
                Message Deduplication ID → detects duplicate sends
                Default deduplication scope → entire FIFO queue (can change on FIFO queue settings)
            SQS + ASG + CloudWatch + decoupling + database protection.
            SQS Terms:
                | Question says...                            | Think...                          |
                | ------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
                | Decouple applications                       | **SQS**                           |
                | Asynchronous processing                     | **SQS**                           |
                | Buffer traffic spikes                       | **SQS**                           |
                | Queue messages                              | **SQS**                           |
                | Producer and consumer                       | **SQS**                           |
                | Downstream application is overloaded        | **SQS**                           |
                | Process jobs later                          | **SQS**                           |
                | Work queue                                  | **SQS**                           |
                | Message temporarily hidden while processing | **Visibility Timeout**            |
                | Process message successfully                | **Delete message**                |
                | Processing failed                           | **Message becomes visible again** |
            Long Polling vs Short Polling
                |                              | Short Polling                         | Long Polling     |
                | ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ---------------- |
                | Wait for messages?           | ❌ No                                  | ✅ Yes            |
                | Maximum wait                 | Immediate                             | Up to **20 sec** |
                | Empty responses              | More                                  | Fewer            |
                | API calls                    | More                                  | Fewer            |
                | Cost efficiency              | Lower                                 | Better           |
                | Latency when message arrives | Can be higher due to repeated polling | Generally better |
                | Recommended                  | ❌                                     | ✅                |
    SNS:
        Simple Notification Service
        message တစ်ခုကို receiver တစ်ယောက်တည်းဆီ ပို့တာမဟုတ်ဘဲ၊
        receiver အများကြီးဆီကို တစ်ပြိုင်နက်တည်း ဖြန့်ပို့ပေးတဲ့ service
        as a broadcast system.
        Explanation:
            With SQS:
                Producer
                ↓
                SQS Queue
                ↓
                Consumer
                Usually, a message in a queue is processed by one consumer.
            With SNS:
                                ┌──→ SQS Queue
                                │
                Producer → SNS Topic
                                │
                                ├──→ Lambda
                                │
                                ├──→ Email
                                │
                                └──→ HTTP endpoint
                One message can be delivered to multiple subscribers.
        Topic:
            a communication channel that 
            publishers send messages to and subscribers receive messages from.
            Note:
                event producer only sends message to one SNS topic.
                producer doesn't need to know who is listening.
                    The producer doesn't directly communicate with:
                        Payment Service
                        Shipping Service
                        Email Service
                        Analytics Service
                    This is an important part of decoupling.
        Publisher:
            application that sends a message to SNS.
        Subscriber:
            Something that receives messages from the SNS Topic.
                eg:
                    SQS
                    Lambda
                    HTTP/HTTPS endpoint
                    Email
                    SMS
            subscription:
                Each one receiver creates a subscription to the SNS topic.
                A subscription basically means:
                    "I want this destination to receive messages from this SNS Topic."
                Subscriber = who receives
                    The destination that wants the messages.
                Subscription = the connection saying they want to receive
                    The relationship connecting that destination to the SNS Topic.
            One message published to SNS → every subscription receives a copy.
                This is the fan-out pattern.
                massive fan-out:
                    One topic can have a huge number of subscriptions: 12500,000/topic.
                    aws account has a service quota: 100,000 topics limit.
        Why do we need SNS?
            Imagine you have an e-commerce application.
            When an order is created, several things need to happen:
            Order Created
            You need to:
                Process payment
                Send confirmation email
                Update inventory
                Generate shipping task
                Send analytics event
            Without SNS:
                    Order Application
                    ├──→ Payment
                    ├──→ Email
                    ├──→ Inventory
                    ├──→ Shipping
                    └──→ Analytics
                The application needs to know about every downstream service.
                This creates tight coupling.
            With SNS
                Instead:

                                    SNS
                            OrderCreated Topic
                                    │
                    ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐
                    ↓               ↓               ↓
                    SQS             Lambda          SQS
                Payment          Email           Inventory
                    │
                    ↓
                Payment Service

                The original application only needs to know: "Publish OrderCreated to SNS."
                It doesn't need to directly communicate with every service.
                This is decoupling.
        SNS = Pub/Sub
            Pub = Publish
            Sub = Subscribe
            flow:
                Publisher
                    ↓
                Publish
                    ↓
                SNS Topic
                    ↓
                Subscribers
        Subscription by filter policies:
            To not send every subscribers, can filter by attributes.
            This avoids sending unnecessary messages to subscribers.
        How to publish:
            Topic Publish:
                using the SDK: Producer -> SNS Topic -> subscribers
                SNS -> Publish API.
            Direct Publish (for mobile apps SDK):
                for mobile push notifications.
                your app -> SNS -> user's smartphone
                Why does mobile SNS need a Platform Application?
                    Mobile push notification systems are different depending on the platform.
                    Platform Application:
                        the configuration that allows SNS to communicate with a particular mobile push platform
                        Android:
                            Google Firebase Cloud Messaging / FCM
                            Older AWS course material may say:GCM (Google Cloud Messaging)
                            GCM has been replaced by FCM.
                        Apple:
                            APNs — Apple Push Notification service
                        Amazon device:
                            ADM — Amazon Device Messaging
                    Platform Endpoint:
                        a specific device/app endpoint
                    flow:
                        Create a platform application
                                ↓
                        Create a platform endpoint
                                ↓
                        Publish to the platform endpoint

                                    SNS
                                    │
                            Platform Application
                                    │
                            ┌──────┼──────┐
                            ↓      ↓      ↓
                        Endpoint A Endpoint B Endpoint C
                            ↓
                            📱 User's phone
        SNS Security:
            Encryption:
                in-flight: HTTPS API.
                at-rest: KMS keys.
                Client side encryption(client itself)
            Access Controls:
                IAM policies to regulate access to SNS API.
            Topic Access Policy:
                resource-based policy similar to s3 bucket policy.
                useful for cross-account access, allowing other services.
                IAM is not enough for above resource-to-resource permission(s3 -> SNS Topic)
        SNS Standard:
        SNS FIFIO:
            Message Group ID
            Deduplication by Deduplication ID and Content-Based Deduplication.
        SNS VS SQS:
            |                 | SQS                                         | SNS                                          |
            | --------------- | ------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
            | Model           | Queue                                       | Pub/Sub                                      |
            | Main idea       | Store messages                              | Broadcast messages                           |
            | Consumer        | Usually one consumer processes each message | Multiple subscribers can receive the message |
            | Pull/Push       | Consumer pulls                              | SNS pushes to subscribers                    |
            | Decoupling      | Yes                                         | Yes                                          |
            | Buffer messages | ✅ Excellent                                 | Not its primary purpose                      |
            | Fan-out         | ❌                                           | ✅                                            |
            | Topic           | ❌                                           | ✅                                            |
            SNS + SQS used together:
                            SNS Topic
                            OrderCreated
                                    │
                        ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                        ↓                     ↓
                    SQS Queue             SQS Queue
                Payment Service       Shipping Service
                        ↓                     ↓
                    Worker                 Worker
        Note:
            Fan-out: One message → many destinations.[all services receive their own copy.]
                SQS Standard
                    ↓
                High throughput
                At-least-once
                Best-effort ordering


                SQS FIFO
                    ↓
                Ordering
                Deduplication
                Message Group ID


                SNS Standard
                    ↓
                Fan-out
                Pub/Sub
                High throughput


                SNS FIFO
                    ↓
                Fan-out
                + Ordering
                + Deduplication
                + Message Group ID
    Amazon Kinesis Data Steams:
        a service for continuously collecting and processing streaming data in real time.
            Users clicking websites
                    ↓
            IoT devices sending data
                    ↓
            Application metrics
                    ↓
            Server logs
                    ↓
                    Kinesis Data Streams
                    ↓
            Process in real time
        Steaming Data:
            Streaming data means data that arrives continuously over time
                rather than being collected as one large batch.
        Producers:
            sends data into Kinesis.
        Applications:
            own app can use the Kinesis SDK/API to send records.
        Kinesis Agent:
            software that can collect data such as logs from servers
        Kinesis Data Streams:
            central part.
            Kinesis stores incoming records in a stream like a continuous conveyor belt.
            continuous conveyor belt = စဉ်ဆက်မပြတ် သယ်ယူပို့ဆောင်ရေးခါးပတ် in industry/airport.
            Rention: configurable up 365 days
                Data remains in Kinesis even after a consumer reads it. supports replay.
                Replay = read previously processed streaming data again.
                Data can't be deleted from Kinesis.
                    consumers don't individually delete records.
            Data up to 10 MiB:
            Ordering guarantee with the same Partition ID:
                Same partition key → same partition → ordering preserved.
            Encryption:
                In-flight: HTTPS
                at-rest: KMS
            Kinesis Producer Library (KPL):
                Producer side.
                an optimized application that writes data to Kinesis.
            Kinesis Client Library (KCL):
                Kinesis Client Library
                optimized application that reads/processes data from Kinesis.
            Cheat sheet:
                | Feature                  | Remember                         |
                | ------------------------ | -------------------------------- |
                | Purpose                  | Real-time streaming data         |
                | Data examples            | IoT, clickstreams, logs, metrics |
                | Retention                | Up to **365 days**               |
                | Consumer reads data      | Data **isn't deleted**           |
                | Replay                   | ✅ Yes                            |
                | Delete individual record | ❌ No                             |
                | Expiration               | When retention period expires    |
                | Maximum record size      | **10 MiB**                       |
                | Ordering                 | Within the **same partition**    |
                | Encryption in transit    | HTTPS                            |
                | Encryption at rest       | KMS                              |
                | Producer library         | **KPL**                          |
                | Consumer library         | **KCL**                          |
            Capacity Modes:
                Provisioned = You choose/manage the capacity.
                    choose how many shards your Kinesis stream has.
                    Each shard has a certain capacity.
                    Per shard
                        Write/Ingestion: 1 MB/s
                        Read/Output: 2 MB/s
                    What does "shard" mean?
                        shard as a capacity unit / lane.
                    Scaling:
                        Manual
                        AWS doesn't automatically change your shard count based on your traffic.
                    Cost:
                        pay based on the number of shards provisioned.
                            1 shard → $$$
                            5 shards → $$$$$
                On-demand = AWS chooses/scales the capacity for you.
                    Scaling: 
                        AWS automatically manages capacity
                    Default capacity provisioned: 4 MB/s in or 4,000 records/s
                Note:
                    |                       | **Provisioned** | **On-demand**          |
                    | --------------------- | --------------- | ---------------------- |
                    | Shards                | You choose      | AWS manages            |
                    | Capacity management   | Manual          | Automatic              |
                    | Scaling               | Manual          | Automatic              |
                    | Predictable traffic   | ✅ Good          | ✅                   |
                    | Unpredictable traffic | More management | ⭐ Good               |
                    | Cost                  | Per shard/hour  | Per stream/hour + data |
                    | Operational effort    | Higher          | Lower                  |
        Consumers:
            reads/processes data from Kinesis.
            eg: 
                own Application,
                Lambda,
                Amazon Data Firehose
                    delivering streaming data to destinations such as S3, Redshift, etc..
                Managed Service for Apache Flink:
                    Flink is used for more advanced stream processing.
                    can perform things like:
                        filtering
                        aggregations
                        transformations
                        analytics
                        detecting patterns
        Why not just use SQS?
            on SQS: Producer → SQS → Consumer
                think: "I have work that needs to be processed."
                Consumer processes the order and eventually deletes the message.
            on Kinesis: Producer → Kinesis → Consumer
                think: "I have a continuous stream of events/data that multiple consumers may want to process."
                Multiple applications can consume the stream independently.
            Their purposes are different. major reason Kinesis is useful.
        Kinesis Data Streams vs SNS:
            SNS => Publish/Subscribe → notify subscribers
            Kinesis => Streaming data collection + real-time processing
            think:
                SNS → "Something happened. Tell interested subscribers."
                Kinesis → "Here is a continuous stream of data. Process/analyze it."
        Note:
            SQS = queue model
            SNS = pub/sub model
            Kinesis = real-time streaming model.

                SQS
                │
                └─ Queue work
                "Process this job."

                SNS
                │
                └─ Pub/Sub
                "Tell everyone interested."

                Kinesis Data Streams
                │
                └─ Streaming data
                "Continuously collect and process this data."
    Amazon Data Firehose:
        renamed from Kinesis Data Firehose.
        like a delivery truck.
        Fully Managed Service
        Where can Firehose deliver data?
            AWS destination:
                can deliver to:
                    Amazon S3
                    Amazon Redshift
                    Amazon OpenSearch Service
            Third-party destinations:
                Custom HTTP endpoint
        Automatic Scaling unlike Kinesis Data Streams's Provisioned mode.
        Pay for what you use
        Near Real-Time
            has a buffer.
            Firehose waits until either:
                Buffer size is reached
                or
                Buffer time is reached
            Then it delivers the batch to the destination.
        Supported data formats:
            CSV
            JSON
            Parquet
            Avro
            Raw Text
            Binary data
        can Convert data to Parquet / ORC
            Why Parquet/ORC?
            They are columnar storage formats, which are very useful for analytics.
            eg: "Stream data into S3 and automatically convert it to Parquet".
        Compression:
            can also compress data.
            ompression can reduce storage and/or transfer costs and improve efficiency.
        AWS Lambda transformations:
            app send data with json, but, want to transform data before sent s3.
            can invoke Lambda for custom transfromation.
                Producer
                ↓
                Firehose
                ↓
                Lambda
                ↓
                Transform data
                ↓
                S3
        Kinesis Data Streams vs Data Firehose
            |                     | Kinesis Data Streams                       | Amazon Data Firehose                |
            | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------- |
            | Main purpose        | Real-time streaming platform               | Deliver streaming data              |
            | Storage             | Yes, retention                             | ❌ Not designed as long-term storage |
            | Replay              | ✅ Yes                                      | ❌ Not the main purpose              |
            | Consumer            | Applications, Lambda, Firehose, etc.       | Destination                         |
            | Shards              | Provisioned mode uses shards               | ❌ No shard management               |
            | Scaling             | Provisioned/on-demand                      | Automatic                           |
            | Serverless          | Managed, but Streams has capacity concepts | ⭐ Serverless                        |
            | Buffering           | Not the same delivery-buffer concept       | ⭐ Yes                               |
            | S3 delivery         | Can feed Firehose                          | ⭐ Direct destination                |
            | Data transformation | Consumer-side processing                   | ⭐ Lambda transformation             |
            | Parquet conversion  | Not its main feature                       | ⭐ Yes                               |
            | ORC conversion      | Not its main feature                       | ⭐ Yes                               |
    Amazon MQ:
        managed message broker service.
        broker:
            traditional messaging protocols such as:
                ActiveMQ
                RabbitMQ
                    message broker software.
                        program that sits between applications and helps them exchange messages.
        unlike SQS/SNS, they are AWS-native messaging services.
        scalable is based on broker servers.
        can use multiple Availability Zones.
Containers on AWS:
    Docker Introduction:
        First: What problem does Docker solve?
        Docker solve Problem of OS difference, extension difference, etc.. eg: It works on my machine!
        Container:
            an isolated environment where your application and its required dependencies can run together.
            ackage the application and its environment consistently.
        What is Docker?
            a platform/tool for creating and running containers.
        Image vs Container:
            An image is a blueprint/template used to create containers.
            A container is the running instance created from an image.
        Docker Image come from container registry.
            container registry: a place where container images are stored.
            Docker Hub: public repository
            Amazon ECR: Elastic Container Registry
                private repo.
                public repo is Amazon ECR Public Gallery
        Dockerfile:
            How do you create your own Docker image?
            with a Dockerfile.
            A Dockerfile contains instructions for building an image.
        Container vs Virtual Machine:
            VM includes a whole guest operating system.
                each VM has its own OS.
                structure: Application + dependencies + Guest OS
            Container share the OS kernel.
                structure: Application + dependencies
                The container does not normally need a complete guest OS.
                more efficient than VMs
        Are containers completely isolated?
            All container are isolated from each other, but they share the host kernel.
                Each container has its own:
                    filesystem view
                    processes
                    networking
                    environment/configuration
        Containers are excellent for microservices:
            Container
            ↓
            User Service

            Container
            ↓
            Order Service

            Container
            ↓
            Payment Service
        Docker doesn't manage everything:
            Docker can:
                Build image
                    ↓
                Run container
                    ↓
                Stop container
                    ↓
                Manage container

                But imagine you have:
                100 containers
                You need to automatically:
                    start containers
                    stop unhealthy containers
                    replace failed containers
                    scale containers
                    distribute traffic
                    manage networking
                    schedule containers across servers

                That's where container orchestration comes in.
            Container orchestration:
                think: Docker -> run this container.
                Orchestration:
                    Orchestrator
                        │
                        ├── Run 100 containers
                        ├── Scale to 200
                        ├── Replace failed container
                        ├── distribute containers
                        └── manage networking
                    Examples:
                        Kubernetes
                        Amazon ECS
                        Amazon EKS
                    And this is where your AWS container topic will start becoming important.
            Note:
                | Term           | Easy meaning                       |
                | -------------- | ---------------------------------- |
                | **Docker**     | Tool/platform for containers       |
                | **Image**      | Blueprint/template                 |
                | **Container**  | Running instance of an image       |
                | **Dockerfile** | Instructions for building an image |
                | **Registry**   | Place to store images              |
                Hypervisor manage VM.
                Docker Daemon manage Docker container. run/manage လုပ်ပေးတဲ့ background service
    Docker Containers Management on AWS:
        Amazon ECS:
            Amazon's own container platform.
        Amazon EKS:
            Amazon managed Kubernetes(open source)
        Amazon Fargate:
            Amazon own serverless container platform.
            work with ECS, EKS.
        Amazon ECR:
            Store container images.
    Amazon ECS:
        Elastic Container Service
        Launch Docker containers on AWS = Launch ECS Tasks on ECS Clusters
        ECS Clusters:
            logical grouping of compute resources where your ECS tasks run.
            ECS Cluster
                │
                ├── Task
                ├── Task
                └── Task
        ECS Tasks:
            a running instance of your container(s), based on an ECS Task Definition.
            Task Definition
                ↓
                Task
                ↓
            Running Container
            A Task can contain one or more containers.
        sample architecture:
            For ECS with EC2 Launch Type:

                                ECS Cluster
                                    │
                        ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                        ↓                     ↓
                    EC2 #1                 EC2 #2
                        │                     │
                    ┌─────┴─────┐          ┌────┴────┐
                    ↓           ↓          ↓         ↓
                Task        Task       Task      Task
                │            │          │         │
            Container    Container  Container Container

            The EC2 instances provide the actual compute capacity.
        two launch Types:
            EC2 Launch Type:
                you must provision & maintain the infrastructure (the EC2 instances)
                You are responsible for the EC2 infrastructure.
            Fargate Launch Type:
                launch Docker containers on AWS.
                do not provision the infrastructure(no ec2 to manage)
                all serverless,
                just create task definitions.
        ECS Agent:
            software running on the EC2 instance that allows ECS to communicate with and manage that instance.
            EC2 instance နဲ့ ECS service ကြားမှာ communication လုပ်ပေးတဲ့ agent/software
        AWS takes care of starting/stopping containers.
            we don't need to manually run/stop containers. ECS manage the tasks.
        Where does Docker image come from?
            Developer
            ↓
            Docker build
            ↓
            Docker Image
            ↓
            Amazon ECR
            ↓
            ECS
            ↓
            Task
            ↓
            Container
        What does ECS actually manage?
            Suppose you have:
                ECS Cluster
                    │
                    ├── EC2 #1
                    ├── EC2 #2
                    └── EC2 #3
            You tell ECS:
            Run 5 tasks.
            ECS determines where those tasks should run based on the available resources and scheduling.
            Conceptually:
                ECS
                │
                ├── Task → EC2 #1
                ├── Task → EC2 #1
                ├── Task → EC2 #2
                ├── Task → EC2 #2
                └── Task → EC2 #3
            So ECS is the container orchestrator.
            Docker = container technology/tool
            ECS = AWS container orchestration service
        Two ECS IAM Roles:
            think:
                                ECS
                                │
                    ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                    ↓                     ↓
            ECS Agent / EC2         Application / Task
                    │                     │
                    ↓                     ↓
            EC2 Instance Profile      ECS Task Role

            EC2 Instance Profile:
                EC2 Launch Type only.
                EC2 instance ကို AWS service တွေကို access လုပ်ခွင့်ပေးတဲ့ IAM role
                    used ECS agent.
                    Make API call to ECS service.
                    send container logs to CloudWatch logs
                    Access Secrets Manager / SSM Parameter Store
                    Pull Docker images from ECR
            ECS Task Role:
                Task Role belongs to the application running inside the task.
                container အတွင်းက application ကို AWS resources တွေ access လုပ်ခွင့်ပေးတဲ့ IAM Role
                defined in the Task Definition
                    eg Task Definiton:
                        Task Definition
                            │
                            ├── Container configuration
                            ├── CPU / Memory
                            ├── Image
                            ├── Port
                            └── Task Role ← IAM Role
            Summary for Roles:
                |                  | EC2 Instance Profile           | ECS Task Role              |
                | ---------------- | ------------------------------ | -------------------------- |
                | Used by          | ECS Agent / EC2 infrastructure | Application in the task    |
                | EC2 Launch Type? | **Yes**                        | Both ECS launch types      |
                | Defined where?   | EC2 instance configuration     | Task Definition            |
                | Purpose          | ECS infrastructure operations  | Application AWS API access |
                | Example          | Pull image from ECR            | Upload file to S3          |
                | Example          | Send logs to CloudWatch        | Read DynamoDB              |
                | Scope            | EC2 instance                   | Individual task            |
                Ask:
                    "Who needs permission?"

                If ECS/EC2 infrastructure needs permission:
                    EC2 Instance Profile
                If my application/container needs permission:
                    ECS Task Role
        LB integration:
            Instead of users connecting directly to ECS tasks, put a Load Balancer in front of the tasks.
            no need to remember the IP address of Task 1, Task 2,
                             Internet
                                │
                                ↓
                        Application
                        Load Balancer
                                │
                    ┌────────┼────────┐
                    ↓        ↓        ↓
                    Task 1    Task 2    Task 3
            supported:
                ALB: Most common choice.
                    using Target Group:
                        ALB
                        ↓
                        Target Group
                        ↓
                        ┌───────────────┐
                        │ ECS Task 1    │
                        │ ECS Task 2    │
                        │ ECS Task 3    │
                        └───────────────┘
                    using Health Check = Task/Server က request လက်ခံပြီး ပုံမှန်အလုပ်လုပ်နေသလား စစ်တာ
                NLB: only for high throughput/performance. pair it with AWS Private link.
                CLB: not recommended.
                EC2 Launch Type: two levels of scaling:
                    Level 1:
                        note enough container, increases tasks.
                    Level 2:
                        add more ec2 instances:
                        very useful architecture.
            Data Volume(EFS):
                ECS + EFS = shared, persistent file storage across AZs, and it works with both EC2 and Fargate.
                Main Problem is Container storage is usually temporary.
                Tasks running in any AZ will share same data in EFS.
                s3 cannot be mounted as a file system.
            Lab:
                using this image: nginxdemos/hello: https://hub.docker.com/r/nginxdemos/hello/
        ECS Service Auto Scaling vs EC2 Auto Scaling:
            ECS Service Auto Scaling => scales Tasks
                "How many containers/tasks do I need?"
                Scaling:
                    automatically changes the desired number of ECS tasks in ECS service.
                        CPU scaling = CPU အလုပ်များလာရင် Task တိုး
                        Memory scaling = RAM အသုံးပြုမှုများလာရင် Task တိုး
                        can use ALB Request Count Per Target as the scaling signal.
                            metric comes from the Application Load Balancer.
                    Target Tracking:
                        think: "Keep this metric around this target." => target CPU = 50%.
                    Step Scaling:
                        You create a CloudWatch Alarm and define scaling steps.
                        architecture:
                            CloudWatch Metric
                                ↓
                            CloudWatch Alarm
                                ↓
                            Step Scaling Policy
                                ↓
                            ECS Service
                                ↓
                            Tasks
                        Example:
                            CPU > 70%
                                ↓
                            Add 2 Tasks

                            CPU > 90%
                                ↓
                            Add 5 Tasks

                            And scale in:
                                CPU < 30%
                                    ↓
                                Remove 1 Task
                    Scheduled Scaling:
                        For predictable workloads.
                        ဘယ်အချိန်မှာ load တက်မယ်/ကျမယ်ဆိုတာ ကြိုသိထားတဲ့အခါ schedule နဲ့ scale လုပ်တာ
            EC2 Auto Scaling => scales EC2 Instances
                uses AWS Application Auto Scaling
                "How many EC2 servers do I need?"
                AWS service/API used to automatically scale various application-level resources.
            ECS Capacity Provider:
                bridge between ECS and your Auto Scaling Group.
                why need ECS Capacity Provider?
                    The problem is: ECS schedules tasks based on available resources such as CPU and memory, 
                        not simply EC2 CPU utilization.
                flow:
                    ECS
                    │
                    │ "I need more capacity!"
                    ↓
                    Capacity Provider
                    │
                    ↓
                    Auto Scaling Group
                    │
                    ↓
                    EC2 Instances
                Capacity:
                    The word capacity here means: Available resources for running ECS tasks.

                    Mainly think:
                        CPU
                        Memory

                    example:
                        ECS wants to run:
                            Task A → 1 CPU + 2 GB RAM
                            Task B → 2 CPU + 4 GB RAM

                    But your cluster has:
                        Available:
                            0.5 CPU
                            1 GB RAM

                    There isn't enough capacity.
                    So:

                        Need more capacity
                            ↓
                        Capacity Provider
                            ↓
                        ASG
                            ↓
                        More EC2
                Capacity Provider doesn't replace the ASG.
                architecture:
                                        ALB
                                        │
                                        ↓
                                ECS Service
                                        │
                                        ↓
                            ECS Service Auto Scaling
                                        │
                                "Need 8 Tasks"
                                        │
                                        ↓
                            ┌────────────────┐
                            │ ECS Tasks      │
                            └───────┬────────┘
                                    │
                                Need capacity?
                                    │
                                    ↓
                            ECS Capacity Provider
                                    │
                                    ↓
                            Auto Scaling Group
                                    │
                            ┌─────────┼─────────┐
                            ↓         ↓         ↓
                        EC2       EC2       EC2
            ECS Tasks Invoked by EventBridge:
                EventBridge detects an event → EventBridge starts an ECS Task
                Instead of having an ECS Service continuously running Tasks, 
                you can start a Task only when something happens.
                Amazon EventBridge:
                    an event bus/service that receives events and routes them to targets.
                    Think: "When X happens, do Y."
                    The target could be:
                        Lambda
                        ECS Task
                        SQS
                        SNS
                        Step Functions
                        etc.
                Event occur:
                    Imagine you have a video-processing application.
                    Normally, you don't want an ECS container running 24/7 because there may be no videos to process.
                    Instead:
                    New video uploaded
                            ↓
                        EventBridge
                            ↓
                    Start ECS Task
                            ↓
                    Container processes video
                            ↓
                    Task finishes

                    The ECS Task is basically a one-time job.
                Scheduled ECS Task:
                    EventBridge doesn't only react to events.
                    It can also trigger something according to a schedule.
                        EventBridge Scheduler / Rule
                                │
                                │  2:00 AM
                                ↓
                            ECS Task
                                ↓
                        Run backup script
                                ↓
                            Finished
                | Pattern                    | Typical purpose                              |
                | -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
                | **ECS Service**            | Long-running application                     |
                | **EventBridge → ECS Task** | Trigger a task when an event/schedule occurs |
            Note:
                ECS Service Auto Scaling = add/remove ECS Tasks
                EC2 Auto Scaling = Add/remove Number of EC2 Instances.
                    we might need both.
                                    ALB
                                    ↓
                                ECS Service
                                    ↓
                            ECS Service Auto Scaling
                                    ↓
                            Number of Tasks
                                    ↓
                            EC2 Auto Scaling
                                    ↓
                        Number of EC2 Instances
                when we use Fargate makes it easier,
                    With Fargate:
                        ALB
                        ↓
                        ECS Service
                        ↓
                        Fargate Tasks

                        You don't manage EC2 instances.


        ECS Vs Fargate:
            |                        | ECS EC2       | ECS Fargate         |
            | ---------------------- | ------------- | ------------------- |
            | EC2 management         | **You**       | **AWS**             |
            | Infrastructure         | You provision | AWS manages         |
            | ECS Agent on your EC2  | ✅             | You don't manage it |
            | Server patching        | You           | AWS                 |
            | Scaling infrastructure | You           | AWS                 |
            | Container management   | ECS           | ECS                 |
            | Serverless containers  | ❌             | ✅                |
    Amazon ECR:
        Elastic Container registry.
        store and manage docker images on AWS.
        fully integrated with ECS. backed by S3.
        controlled by IAM.
    Amazon EKS:
        Amazon Elastic Kubernetes Service.
        managed Kubernetes clusters on AWS.
        Kubernetes:
            open-source system for automatic deployment, scaling and management of containerized(Docker) application.
        alternative to ECS, but, different API.
        supports EC2 if you want to deploy worker nodes or Fargate to deploy serverless containers.
        Kubernetes is cloud-agnostic(can used any cloud- GCP, Azure, ..)
            agnostic = Independent
        architecture:
                             Amazon EKS
                                │
                    ┌────────────┴────────────┐
                    │                         │
            Control Plane              Worker Nodes
            (Kubernetes)                    │
                    │                  ┌───────┴───────┐
                    │                  │               │
                API Server         Node            Node
                                    │               │
                                    Pods            Pods
                                    │               │
                                Containers      Containers
        EKS Control Plane:
            Control plane is managed by AWS
            Kubernetes brain.
                manages things like:
                    Kubernetes API
                    scheduling
                    cluster state
                    orchestration
        Worker Nodes:
            They run:
                Pods
                ↓
                Containers
        Pod:
            the smallest deployable unit in Kubernetes.
            can run with EC2 or Fargate.
            can also contain multiple closely-related containers:
                Pod
                ├── Application container
                └── Sidecar container

                The containers inside the same Pod share certain resources, such as networking.
        Node Types:
            who manages the underlying compute.
            architecture:
                                EKS Cluster
                                    │
                        ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                        │                     │
                    Control Plane          Worker Compute
                                            │
                                ┌──────────────┼──────────────┐
                                ↓              ↓              ↓
                        Managed Node    Self-Managed     Fargate
                            Groups          Nodes
                                │              │              │
                            EC2            EC2          Serverless
            Managed Node Groups:
                AWS/EKS manages the EC2 nodes for you.
                    Creates and manages Nodes (EC2 instances) for you
            Self-Managed Nodes:
                you managed the EC2 nodes yourself.
                Amazon EKS Optimized AMI:
                    a prebuilt AMI containing the software/configuration needed for EKS worker nodes.
                    So instead of building an EC2 AMI completely from scratch, you can use:
                        EKS Optimized AMI
                            ↓
                            EC2
                            ↓
                        Registered with EKS
            Fargate:
                No EC2 worker nodes for you to manage.
                    EKS
                    │
                    └── Pod
                        ↓
                    Fargate
                        ↓
                    Container
            Summary:
                |                    | Managed Node Group | Self-Managed Nodes | Fargate    |
                | ------------------ | ------------------ | ------------------ | ---------- |
                | Compute            | EC2                | EC2                | Serverless |
                | Who manages nodes? | AWS/EKS            | You                | AWS        |
                | ASG                | Managed by EKS     | You manage it      | ❌          |
                | EC2 visible to you | ✅                  | ✅                  | ❌          |
                | On-Demand          | ✅                  | ✅                  | N/A        |
                | Spot               | ✅                  | ✅                  | N/A        |
                | Node maintenance   | Less               | More               | None       |
        Data Volumes:
            EKS Pods are temporary.
            for persistent data:
                EKS storage → StorageClass → CSI driver → AWS storage
                    Pod
                    │
                    │ mount
                    ↓
                    AWS Storage

                    example:
                        Pod → EBS
                        Pod → EFS
                        Pod → FSx for Lustre
                        Pod → FSx for NetApp ONTAP
            StorageClass:
                When a Pod asks for storage, what kind of storage should I create/use?
                to dynamically provision storage.
            CSI:
                Container Storage Interface.
                standard interface that allows Kubernetes to communicate with storage systems.
                          Kubernetes
                            │
                            │ CSI
                            ↓
                    Storage provider
                    /      |       \
                    EBS     EFS      FSx
        Note:
            Control plane is managed by AWS
            Applications run in Pods
            Pods can run on:
                EC2 worker nodes
                Fargate
            Good choice when:
                company already uses Kubernetes
                wants Kubernetes ecosystem/tools
                needs Kubernetes compatibility.

            EKS Fargate needs persistent/shared file storage.
                think: EFS.
                |                         | EBS                         | EFS                       |
                | ----------------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------- |
                | Type                    | Block storage               | File storage              |
                | Typical access          | Single node/AZ-oriented     | Shared                    |
                | Multi-AZ                | ❌ Not normally              | ✅                         |
                | EKS                     | ✅                           | ✅                         |
                | Fargate                 | Not the typical choice      | ✅                         |
                | Shared by multiple Pods | Limited/not the usual model | ✅                         |
                | Think                   | Hard disk                   | Shared network filesystem |
Serverless:
    You don't manage the underlying servers.
    FaaS = Function as a Service
    nowadays, today, "Serverless" is broader:
        compute => Lambda
        database => DynamoDB
        storage => s3
        messaging => SQS, SNS.
        api => API Gateway
        container => Fargate
    AWS Lambda:
        virtual functions.
        limited by time - short execution.
            Maximum execution time = 15 minutes
            For long-running workloads, think about EC2, ECS/Fargate, AWS Batch
        You upload your code → AWS runs it when needed → AWS manages the servers.
        event-driven:
            Something happens → Lambda is invoked.
            Lambda doesn't normally run continuously like EC2.
        stateless
            not depend on local files or local memory
        Run on-demand
            On-demand = လိုအပ်တဲ့အချိန်မှာပဲ run ခိုင်းတာ။
        Pricing:
            pay per request and compute time.
            free tier
        support many languages.
        Limits - per region
            Execution:
                memory: 128 MB - 10 GB (1 MB increment)
                    More Lambda memory also gives the function more CPU.
                    Lambda is CPU-intensive, increasing memory can also make it execute faster
                max execution time: 900 sec (15 min)
                environment variable (4 KB)
                    Don't use Lambda environment variables for storing large amounts of data.
                    think: Small configuration
                                ↓
                            Environment variables

                            Secrets
                                ↓
                            Secrets Manager / Parameter Store
                disk capacity in the "functin container" (in /tmp) : 512 MB - 10 GB.
                    Every Lambda execution environment has a temporary filesystem:
                        Lambda
                        │
                        └── /tmp
                Concurrency: 1,000(account/Region-level quota.)
                    can be increase by request to aws.
                    if over 1000 quota, eg:
                        AWS Account
                        │
                        └── ap-northeast-1
                            │
                            ├── Lambda A → 600 concurrent
                            ├── Lambda B → 300 concurrent
                            └── Lambda C → 100 concurrent
                                            ─────────
                                            1,000

                    If all 1,000 concurrency slots are being used, another Lambda invocation may be throttled.
                    What is throttling?
                    Throttle = AWS temporarily refuses/delays additional Lambda invocations.
                    Reserved Concurrency:
                        can reserved for a specific Lambda function.
                        cannot run more than reserved limits.
                            Lambda A → Reserved = 100
                            Reserved Concurrency = 100

                            Concurrent executions:
                            0 ─────────────────── 100
                                        ↑
                                    MAXIMUM
                    What happens when throttling occurs?
                        depends on how Lambda was invoked.
                            Invocation
                            │
                            ├── Synchronous
                            │
                            └── Asynchronous
                            Synchronous invocation → 429 ❌
                            Asynchronous invocation → retry
                                go to Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ).
                                    Event
                                    ↓
                                    Lambda
                                    ↓
                                    ❌
                                    ↓
                                    Retry
                                    ↓
                                    ❌
                                    ↓
